# V·π continuous benchmark and performance-proof rail — harden

**Status:** controlling harden input for an E-3 addendum; not ratified and not
an implementation or measurement authority.  
**Scope:** `pi/mirror/` prototype evidence only; no production execution.  
**Date:** 2026-07-21.

## 0. Verdict

The research packet is directionally right and should proceed, but it is **not
safe to ratify verbatim**. Its two-lane quarantine, content-addressed workload
lattice, correctness-before-timing rule, strict peer objective, and PB13P
matrix are accepted in substance. The controlling amendments below are
required before a harness is authored or a V·π performance verdict is emitted.

The largest corrections are:

1. a comparator is eligible for a speed claim only on the complete,
   pre-frozen denominator where it performs the **same semantic operation**;
   independently correct but differently shaped work may be reported but
   cannot establish “faster”;
2. qualification comparisons are matched-block comparisons, not ratios of
   unrelated process populations, and an unchanged candidate may not be rerun
   until it passes;
3. the 5% wave threshold is a triage boundary, not a ratchet: every door also
   retains a fixed accepted anchor and all debt must be cleared at perfection;
4. a claimed two-factor parse-that uplift is factorial only when all four cells
   execute the same grammar semantics; an uplift-only API otherwise forms a
   separate cohort, not evidence of an engine effect;
5. PB0 must freeze the Phase-B denominator, work/resource budgets, and bench
   dispositions before Gate 2. No pre-PB0 document can truthfully close PB13P.

No V·π mirror benchmark exists today. P-3/P-4 remain evidence about the three
old subjects only; this harden seat creates no new measurement.

## 1. Evidence findings and challenges

### BH-1 — P-3 is evidence, not yet a reproducible V·π baseline package

The durable aggregate pins the observed bundles and inputs, but the executable
harness, raw runs, profiles, recovered deposed tree, and subject bundles remain
under `~/.claude/.../tmp`. The live receipt names only two source files; a
working-tree source hash is not a repository reconstruction receipt. The
deposed bundle has a bundle hash but its complete source/tree/build closure is
not durable in `pi/`. The recovered harness also hard-codes absolute local
paths. Therefore the old numbers are authentic historical evidence, but no new
“same comparator” claim is reproducible until the comparator registry in BR-2
is sealed in the prototype proof tree.

### BH-2 — success is not semantic overlap

P-3 checks only success or absence of a throw. Its value corpus crosses typed,
generic, and version-different ASTs; its deposed `okThrow` accepts any return;
and approved LIVE/spec divergences do not make both sides equivalent. Timing
two subjects after verifying each against its own expected answer proves cost
on the same bytes, not cost for the same operation. Such rows are useful
compatibility observations but are ineligible for the strict peer-win gate.

The qualification lane needs an untimed, frozen semantic projection per
operation. If lossless common meaning cannot be projected without embedding a
second parser or evaluator in the adapter, that comparator/row is
**INCOMPARABLE**, never silently normalized into eligibility.

### BH-3 — the compatibility lane must remain intentionally defective

The recovered `bytesOf` counts UTF-16 code units, subjects always run in one
fixed order, outputs are unused, the normalizer is sampled after all subject
scenarios, and the gate uses peak-of-15. Those choices must remain byte-for-byte
in a named P-3 compatibility reproduction or the historical vector changes.
They must not leak into new headline claims. In particular, the 12,748 label
for `animations.css` remains a legacy code-unit label; it may not be relabeled
as bytes. New results use ingress UTF-8 bytes.

### BH-4 — the proposed statistical population is under-specified

“Ratio of invocation medians” is ambiguous when each subject is a fresh
process. Separate unpaired process populations can turn load/order drift into a
parser effect. Five final invocations and an 80% win count are also too weak for
a strict “fastest” claim: at five blocks that means four wins; even at ten,
eight wins does not clear a one-sided 5% sign test. A seeded bootstrap is
reproducible only if the paired unit, transform, interval type, RNG/seed, and
invalid-run policy are fixed.

### BH-5 — retries can become benchmark p-hacking

The research forbids deleting inspected outliers, but does not prohibit
rerunning the same unchanged bundle after a valid RED result. A qualification
batch is one sealed experiment. A valid batch remains authoritative for that
candidate digest; another gate attempt requires a code/config/corpus change
with a new digest. A process may be invalidated only by a predeclared machine
or harness failure recorded before results are inspected, and the entire
matched block—not the losing subject—is rerun.

### BH-6 — corpus derivation alone does not prevent gaming

An author can make a conformance bank large with duplicated easy literals,
underrepresent long or ambiguous productions, or add favorable rows after
timing. “All rows while tractable” also lacks a tractability boundary. Exact
source strings must be deduplicated within semantic class; production,
maturity, result class, and size strata must be frozen before candidate timing;
additions append and cannot rewrite the historical denominator. Generated or
mutated fixtures never count as real-source throughput.

The present Phase-A banks were not designed under this law. They need one
independent freeze pass before they acquire performance authority. This does
not invalidate their semantic evidence.

### BH-7 — UTF-8 accounting needs an ingress rule

`Buffer.byteLength(text, "utf8")` is correct for in-memory strings, but a file
corpus also needs its raw-byte hash, declared UTF-8 decode policy, BOM status,
decoded-text hash, and CSS preprocessing counts. Lone surrogate replacement,
CRLF folding, form feed, and NUL replacement otherwise make “bytes processed”
ambiguous. Headline MB/s counts raw ingress UTF-8 bytes exactly once per public
operation. UTF-16 units, CSS code points, preprocessed units, tokens, and nodes
are separate denominators; none may be renamed bytes.

### BH-8 — a timed checksum can measure the checksum

Hashing or canonicalizing every result inside a tiny-value timing loop can
dominate the parser and can penalize subjects with richer correct output.
Conversely, discarding every result makes the timed obligation unclear. The
timed adapter must store the actual result in a module-scoped opaque sink and
do no semantic hashing. Full result/diagnostic projection and digest checks run
immediately before and after the timed sample, outside its clock. A distinct
parse-plus-serialize scenario may time serialization; parse-only rows may not.

### BH-9 — continuous qualification is otherwise prohibitively multiplicative

Five processes × fifteen samples × every door × every repair × all historical
subjects, plus a CPU profile for every door, would turn the proof rail into the
tranche's dominant authoring cost. It would also encourage authors to batch or
avoid repairs for procedural reasons. Continuous evidence should mean a cheap
smoke at author close, one full door qualification at the candidate's semantic
acceptance boundary, dependency-triggered replays, and aggregate graduation—not
a full predecessor tournament after every edit.

### BH-10 — the 5% trigger permits cumulative decay if used alone

Repeated 4.9% regressions can best every immediate threshold while destroying
the final result. Every established door needs both an immediate predecessor
and a fixed first-accepted performance anchor on the identical corpus. AMBER
dispositions are explicit debt, not acceptance of a permanent budget. W7 and
PB13P close with no unresolved debt and compare the final candidate with both
anchors.

### BH-11 — profiles and heap observations are not timing samples

A V8 profiler changes execution. “The invocation nearest the median” cannot be
captured retroactively unless all timing invocations were perturbed. Select the
median-duration workload after the unprofiled aggregate, then run a separate
profile process with the same manifest/N; label it attribution evidence, not a
timing sample. CPU profiles are mandatory before and after any O-4/O-5 change,
for shared-core regressions, and at graduation according to each operation's
PB0 disposition. They are not automatically required after every small leaf
repair.

`maxRSS`, sampled heap profiles, and forced-GC deltas are noisy and
platform-specific. They are report-only until a door has a PB0-ratified budget
and repeated isolated-process slope protocol. “No statistically established
growth” is not proof of absence. The controlling safety gates are deterministic
work/limit counters plus a one-sided upper confidence bound below the ratified
retained-growth budget.

### BH-12 — the proposed uplift matrix is not always factorial

A 2×2 grammar×engine matrix is causal only if both grammar snapshots compile
unchanged against both engines, expose the same operation, and reproduce the
same semantic digests. If the current grammar uses a new uplift API unavailable
in 1.0.0, the `current grammar × 1.0.0` cell does not exist. That cohort may
prove the combined new engine+grammar artifact, but it may not attribute the
gain to the engine. Compatibility shims that duplicate an engine feature in
the grammar are also confounded and forbidden as benchmark machinery.

### BH-13 — “beat regex and prior iterations” needs a finite denominator

The presently evidenced predecessor registry is exactly: LIVE regex bundle
`c73c8ff2ca2da3ed9bcd7b2016ef00aba0416a79d1787d88943fc3ab0bdccad0`,
deposed bundle
`6c62368f6b4e6c1c28e6c0542bc86004c243fba6bb6d70ac0bba8435a81618eb`,
and C14 bundle
`2b57626fff0975276f7d46d84ffc01835bc83bfe4f28ce6b5855622f6a22f0d6`.
“Every prior iteration” cannot mean unidentified or unreconstructable parser
versions. It means this owner-sealed set plus each V·π door's first and
immediately previous accepted snapshots. Later subjects append through an E-3
receipt; they do not silently change a closed gate.

The strict goal is feasible as a falsifiable close condition, not as a promised
measurement. If the idiomatic, spec-correct prototype does not win, V·π remains
performance-RED; it does not narrow semantics, add a benchmark-only fast path,
hide a losing stratum in a weighted average, or repeatedly rerun the same
candidate.

### BH-14 — no BBNF/parse-that uplift is presently admissible

The active task has not acknowledged a content-addressed exchange under the
required boundary. No package/commit/API receipt has crossed; therefore there
is no uplift candidate to fold in, benchmark, or communicate back. Once the
owner opens the boundary, only an explicitly acknowledged, fully implemented
artifact enters §2.8. A status claim, private source observation, proposal, or
performance number is not an artifact receipt.

## 2. Controlling amendments for the addendum

The E-3 writer should transpose the following as normative rows **BR-1 through
BR-10**. These identifiers are bench-local and must not consume or collide with
the semantic R-series.

### BR-1 — two immutable lanes

- **Compatibility/P-3:** preserve the recovered source, five corpus identities,
  subject bundles, order, `N=1000`, 50 warmups per sample, fifteen samples,
  five invocations, normalizer, peak statistic, and UTF-16 `.length`
  calculation. Report its throughput unit as
  `legacy_code_units_per_second`. Its output is historical continuity, not the
  strict fastest claim.
- **Qualification/V·π:** a separate runner and schema use the controls below.
  It never imports a legacy statistic or silently repairs a P-3 field.

Both lanes may execute in the same graduation wave, but their artifacts and
verdict clauses remain disjoint.

### BR-2 — sealed subject and build registry

Before the first qualification run, each subject row records:

1. stable subject ID and role;
2. complete source commit/tree or immutable package integrity plus a
   reproducible reconstruction recipe;
3. authored entry/adapter digest and public operation;
4. dependency lock and parse-that integrity where applicable;
5. esbuild version, exact flags, input source-set digest, output bundle digest,
   source-map policy, and bundle verification;
6. runtime ABI and environment receipt.

The three historical bundles in BH-13 are the initial predecessor set. They
must be copied or reproducibly rebuilt into a durable, content-addressed
prototype proof location before they participate in a new gate. Uniform fresh
builds use identical flags/tool versions; an old observed bundle is never
quietly replaced by a rebuild with a different digest. P-3's old bundle remains
available even if a reconstruction bundle is added as a separately named
subject.

Environment receipt: Node, V8, OS/release, architecture, CPU/model/core count,
machine ID, power mode, thermal state if observable without privilege, relevant
Node/V8 flags, harness/bundle/corpus digests, and start/end timestamps. Results
from different ABI/environment classes are not pooled.

### BR-3 — semantic eligibility and complete common denominator

Every atomic manifest row is correctness-checked outside timing and records
raw-result digest, diagnostic/recovery class, full-consumption rule, and an
operation-owned semantic projection digest. A subject is peer-eligible on that
row only when:

- it implements the same public operation and cost boundary;
- it satisfies the row's spec-correct accept/reject/recovery obligation;
- its projection has the same meaning without a parser/evaluator hidden in the
  adapter; and
- required serialization/result construction is either included by all
  subjects or excluded by all.

Eligibility is frozen before candidate timing. The peer denominator is **all
distinct rows eligible for both subjects** in every mandatory production,
result, maturity, and size stratum—not an author-selected subset. Approved
semantic divergences are reported as incomparable rows. If a mandatory stratum
has zero common rows, its peer result is `NO_COMMON_DENOMINATOR`, not PASS.

Accept, reject, recover, parse, exact serialization, edit serialization, and
canonical serialization are separate scenarios and verdict cells. A cheap
reject never improves successful-parse throughput. LIVE's measured
`deepFreeze` stays in its public-operation number; no estimated subtraction may
claim a faster raw parser. A separate kernel experiment is informational only.

T0 remains whole and immutable for compatibility. Qualification may publish
its mechanically classified common rows/strata alongside T0, but may not alter
the T0 hashes or present a subset as the historical five-scenario result.

### BR-4 — corpus freeze and anti-gaming law

- Freeze Phase-A T1 manifests once, independently, before any V·π timing is
  inspected. Deduplicate exact source+operation+semantic-class rows. Preserve
  ordinary accept, reject, and recovery banks separately.
- After freeze, new semantic fixtures append. Removal or reclassification
  requires an E-3 amendment and retains the superseded denominator for history.
- If a bank exceeds the addendum's explicit per-stratum tractability ceiling,
  select before implementation by ascending SHA-256 within each frozen
  production×maturity×result×size stratum using a manifest-derived seed and
  declared quota. Never sample after timing.
- T2 exists only after PB0 binds a reviewed normative/conformance occurrence to
  an operation. T3 requires path/commit/raw hash/license/provenance and an
  oracle-classified goal. T4 is deterministic stress evidence and never
  real-world or headline throughput.
- Report every stratum. A total is secondary and cannot turn a losing mandatory
  stratum GREEN. Replication changes sample duration, never corpus weight.

PB0 must publish, per operation: corpus disposition, semantic projection,
mandatory strata, limits, deterministic work counter/budget, expected
complexity class, memory-growth budget or `REPORT_ONLY` reason, join replay,
and profile disposition. These rows join the occurrence-derived Gate-2 packet.

### BR-5 — qualification execution and observer boundary

One scenario pass means one traversal of its immutable corpus. Count exact raw
UTF-8 ingress bytes, source items/operations, UTF-16 units, CSS input code
points, preprocessed units, tokens/nodes/diagnostics where applicable. File
rows retain both raw-byte and decoded-text hashes and the decode/preprocessing
receipt.

For each scenario and candidate digest:

1. perform a short round-robin pilot over all eligible subjects; choose one
   power-of-two `N` mechanically from the slowest pilot median toward 200 ms,
   bounded to a 50–1000 ms measured sample where feasible; freeze `N` before
   qualification and exclude pilot observations;
2. start a fresh process per subject/scenario/matched block;
3. rotate subject order by a manifest-seeded balanced schedule; run every
   subject in the block before the next block;
4. warm `clamp(50, ceil(1 MiB / corpusUtf8Bytes), 5000)` complete passes, then
   collect fifteen timed samples;
5. assign each actual result to an opaque module-scoped sink inside the timed
   loop, with no hash/projection/canonicalization in the clock;
6. reproduce the full semantic/result digest immediately before and after the
   samples. A mismatch is semantic RED and invalidates timing, not an outlier.

Raw samples, order, pilot, `N`, warmup, sink method, exit status, and receipts
are retained. Invalidity predicates (harness error, semantic mismatch, process
failure, predeclared thermal/power discontinuity) are machine-checkable. A
valid RED batch cannot be discarded or retried for the same candidate digest.

### BR-6 — exact statistical and verdict policy

The statistical unit is a matched block. Each subject contributes the median
of its fifteen samples; comparison uses the paired log ratio
`log(candidateMedian / baselineMedian)` for that same block.

- author smoke: one block × seven samples, explicitly non-gating;
- wave qualification: five matched blocks × fifteen samples;
- W7/PB13P/perfection: ten matched blocks × fifteen samples.

Report raw samples, per-block medians and ratios, median ratio, MAD, min/max,
and CV per subject. For comparisons, use a deterministic 10,000-resample
paired percentile bootstrap of the median log ratio; exponentiate the two-sided
95% interval. Seed = SHA-256 of schema version + scenario manifest digest +
candidate bundle digest + baseline bundle digest + literal `bootstrap-v1`;
the addendum's harness must pin the RNG algorithm. No sample- or block-level
outlier deletion occurs after inspection.

A final **FASTER** cell requires all of:

1. median paired ratio `> 1.00`;
2. 95% interval lower bound `> 1.00`;
3. at least 9 of 10 block ratios `> 1.00`;
4. semantic eligibility and every mandatory stratum complete.

Otherwise it is `INCONCLUSIVE` or `RED`; rounding never creates PASS. Because
the final claim is the conjunction “faster on every mandatory cell,” a weighted
mean cannot substitute and no multiplicity discount is taken. Exploratory
per-row claims remain non-gating.

For wave regression, compare both the immediately previous accepted bundle and
the door's fixed first-accepted anchor on the identical frozen corpus:

- ratio `< 0.95` with the 95% interval upper bound `< 1.00` is RED;
- any smaller statistically established slowdown, or point ratio `< 0.95`
  without a conclusive interval, is AMBER and requires a written root
  disposition;
- a new door establishes its anchor and makes no predecessor claim.

AMBER is performance debt, not a renewable 5% allowance. Every debt row and
both-anchor comparison reappear at W7/PB13P; perfection requires none
unresolved.

### BR-7 — proportionate continuous schedule

- At author close, run semantic gates first, then the non-gating smoke for
  changed doors.
- After repairs and E-1 semantic acceptance, run one wave qualification for the
  changed door, its fixed affected join, previous accepted bundle, and fixed
  anchor. Batch repairs by candidate digest; do not gate every edit.
- A shared scanner/lexeme/result runtime change replays all transitively
  dependent doors. A type/doc-only change whose emitted bundle digest is
  unchanged does not manufacture a timing rerun.
- Historical LIVE/deposed/C14 tournaments run at W7, not at every wave.
- W7 runs both exact P-3 compatibility and Phase-A qualification. PB13P runs
  Gate-2-ratified T2/T4 plus the inherited Phase-A cells and T3. PB13P never
  waives PB13G.

Profile separately after timing: mandatory before/after O-4 or O-5, on a
shared-core RED/AMBER, and for every graduation operation marked `PROFILE` by
its manifest. Use the aggregate's median-duration workload/N in a fresh process
with exact profiler flags; profiles explain, never replace, throughput data.

### BR-8 — allocation, retention, scaling, and deterministic work

Run memory cases in fresh `--expose-gc` processes with frozen pass counts and
identical flags. Report `memoryUsage`, heap statistics, platform-labelled
`maxRSS`, and sampled heap profiles, but do not treat RSS or one before/after
delta as an equality gate. T4 uses at least the PB0-ratified geometric size
series and repeated independent processes.

For retained growth, fit the PB0-specified quantity against completed discarded
operations/input size and gate the one-sided 95% upper slope bound against that
operation's ratified budget. For time/allocation complexity, deterministic work
counters and limit refusal are controlling; noisy wall-time slopes corroborate
them. A counter over budget, a hang, non-deterministic `limit+1`, or a slope
above the ratified class is semantic/performance RED before peer speed is
considered. Until PB0 supplies a budget, memory is explicitly `REPORT_ONLY`.

### BR-9 — exact predecessor and perfection clauses

The Phase-A strict predecessor set is LIVE regex, deposed, and C14 at the
digests in BH-13, on each subject's complete BR-3 common denominator. W7 also
compares every existing door with its fixed and immediate V·π anchors.

To close **peer-beating GREEN**, the final mirror must earn FASTER in every
mandatory eligible cell against every registered predecessor. An absent
operation is `NOT_APPLICABLE`; a semantically different operation is
`INCOMPARABLE`; neither is a peer PASS. The verdict matrix must state the exact
common denominator, so “beat LIVE/deposed/C14” means beat each on all work each
can correctly and comparably perform—not beat an invented L4 baseline.

The historical VALUE `0.0500` and SHEET `0.1000` peak/normalizer clauses remain
separate P-3 compatibility evidence and retain OC-1 until the owner explicitly
rules whether they remain an additional close gate or become a legacy report.
They are never recalibrated by this harden seat. If strict peer beating fails,
V·π remains RED; correctness, modularity, readability, and full CSS scope are
never traded for the number.

Phase-B doors with no qualified external peer close on anchor regression,
absolute throughput/latency, deterministic work/limits, allocation/retention,
and scaling matrices. They may not claim “faster than LIVE” merely because
LIVE accepts the bytes as unknown syntax.

### BR-10 — parse-that uplift admission and communication

An uplift enters only with the same acknowledged package/commit/API/batch
digest on both sides, an explicit “implemented and hardened” status receipt,
and an independently reproducible artifact. It first passes all semantic,
diagnostic, no-throw, limit, and result-digest rails.

Run the full 2×2 grammar×engine matrix only when all four cells compile without
semantic compatibility shims and pass identical projections. Then report:

- engine effect at fixed old grammar;
- engine effect at fixed current grammar;
- grammar effect at fixed 1.0.0;
- grammar effect at fixed uplift;
- interaction, raw cells, profiles, and digests.

If a new API makes a cell impossible, run the last accepted grammar on both
engines where possible for the engine-only effect, and report the uplift-API
grammar as a separate combined cohort. Do not label the cohort delta an engine
gain. Adoption still requires an E-3 pin change, no controlling regression,
and an explained profile/work delta. Retain the 1.0.0 vector after adoption.

Once the owner-mediated boundary is open, communicate only acknowledged
artifact/API/batch identities plus local scenario/result/profile digests and
classified deltas. Do not transmit private source, infer BBNF status, or accept
BBNF's measurement as local proof. At present this row is blocked for lack of
an acknowledged artifact, not waived.

## 3. Implementation/evidence boundary

After ratification, the benchmark addendum may authorize authored harness,
manifests, adapters, reconstruction recipes, and non-production evidence under
`pi/mirror/bench/` and the prototype proof tree. It does **not** authorize:

- production `src/`, `vnext/`, `scripts/dev/dev.sh`, `INBOX.md`, parse-that,
  bbnf-lang, or keyframes changes;
- grammar semantic changes merely to improve a result;
- generated grammar, duplicate scanners, evaluator logic in adapters, hidden
  fast paths, or corpus-specific dispatch;
- a parse-that pin change without its own E-3 owner gate;
- invented measurements or promotion of smoke/profile observations to a
  qualification verdict.

Harness-authored LOC and generated corpus/receipt data are accounted
separately. The recovered temporary rig is research input: durable code must be
reviewed, path-independent, deterministic, and content-addressed rather than
copied as an unexplained authority.

## 4. Required owner decisions

The addendum should surface these exact decisions before benchmark code:

1. **Continuous-rail authority:** ratify BR-1 through BR-10 as the replacement
   performance law for W7/PB13P and per-wave proof, while leaving semantics
   subordinate to G-2/PB13G.
2. **Strict close meaning:** ratify the finite predecessor set and complete
   common-semantic-denominator interpretation in BR-9; a non-win leaves the
   prototype performance-RED rather than authorizing a contrived optimization.
3. **Statistics:** ratify matched blocks, the frozen paired-bootstrap method,
   9/10 win requirement, no valid-batch retry, two-anchor regression rule, and
   AMBER debt closure.
4. **Historical floors / OC-1:** decide whether the original 0.0500/0.1000
   compatibility clauses remain an additional close gate or a separately
   reported legacy calibration after the strict peer gate lands.
5. **PB0 duty and retention:** ratify the Gate-2 benchmark fields in BR-4/BR-8
   and choose durable raw artifact retention (repository proof tree or an
   owner-named content-addressed store whose hashes/manifests remain in-tree).
6. **Uplift authority:** ratify BR-10's acknowledged-artifact and crossable-cell
   rule; no engine pin changes merely because the active BBNF task reports an
   uplift.

## 5. E-3 ruling

**YES — this must become a proper E-3 addendum.** It materially changes the
ratified W7 born-RED from deposed-relative/P-3 terminal reporting into a
continuous rail, adds LIVE and C14 as strict peers, defines new close
statistics, extends PB13P/PB0 evidence obligations, and governs future
parse-that adoption. Those are gate and scope changes, not a mechanical harness
repair. The sequence is:

`research → this harden → addendum-write → 2× independent challenge → root
gestalt → explicit owner ratification → harness prototype`.

Until that sequence closes, only the old P-3/P-4 claims and current ratified
W7/PB13P text have authority; no new V·π performance claim is GREEN.

## 6. Controlling amendment — 2026-07-21, post ADDENDA-06 challenges A/B

**Status:** controlling formation repair. This amendment supersedes every
conflicting sentence above, especially the BR-6 duration-ratio formula. It
preserves the BR-1 through BR-10 names and strict peer-beating objective. It
authorizes no harness, pilot, measurement, optimization, PB0, PB13P, or pin
change. The ADDENDA-06 writer must transpose this amendment normatively before
two fresh independent challenges.

### 6.1 Corrected speed direction and exhaustive cell law

Timed samples are elapsed durations. For each matched block, let
`baselineMedianDuration` and `candidateMedianDuration` be the medians defined in
§6.5. The only normative peer ratio is:

```text
speedRatio = baselineMedianDuration / candidateMedianDuration
pairedLogSpeedRatio = log(speedRatio)
```

Thus `speedRatio > 1` means the candidate is faster. Every earlier unqualified
“paired ratio,” confidence interval, 9-of-10 test, regression threshold, fixed
anchor, and immediate anchor uses this orientation. The earlier
`candidateMedian / baselineMedian` formula is **RETRACTED**. Throughput ratios
may be reported as corroborating values but do not define a second verdict
orientation.

Each qualification cell has exactly one state:

- `FASTER`: eligible valid evidence, median `speedRatio > 1.00`, the
  exponentiated 95% interval lower bound `> 1.00`, at least 9 of 10 final block
  ratios `> 1.00`, and every mandatory selected-frozen stratum complete;
- `SLOWER`: eligible valid evidence whose exponentiated 95% interval upper
  bound is `< 1.00`;
- `INCONCLUSIVE`: eligible valid evidence that is neither `FASTER` nor
  `SLOWER`;
- `INCOMPARABLE`: both operations exist, but same-semantics/same-cost-boundary
  eligibility cannot be established without distortion;
- `NO_COMMON_DENOMINATOR`: a mandatory peer stratum has zero eligible common
  selected-frozen rows;
- `NOT_APPLICABLE`: the registered subject does not implement that operation;
- `UNAVAILABLE_ARTIFACT`: the exact registered artifact cannot be durably
  recovered and executed;
- `UNSUPPORTED_ABI`: the exact registered artifact exists but cannot execute
  under the frozen qualification ABI;
- `INVALID`: only a predeclared runner/environment/semantic invalidity in
  §6.6 occurred and valid evidence was not obtained.

For strict close, only `FASTER` passes a mandatory eligible peer cell.
`SLOWER` and `INCONCLUSIVE` make the performance verdict RED.
`UNAVAILABLE_ARTIFACT`, `UNSUPPORTED_ABI`, `INVALID`, and a mandatory
`NO_COMMON_DENOMINATOR` make the evidence verdict BLOCKED; BLOCKED is not
GREEN. `INCOMPARABLE` and `NOT_APPLICABLE` are terminal registry dispositions,
not invented peer wins, and cannot discharge another mandatory cell. The
matrix reports performance RED separately from evidence BLOCKED.

Wave regressions use the same speed orientation. `speedRatio < 0.95` with the
interval upper bound `< 1.00` is RED; any smaller statistically established
slowdown, or point ratio `< 0.95` without a conclusive interval, is AMBER.
Both the fixed first-accepted and immediately previous accepted anchor bundles
are freshly executed in the same matched blocks on the candidate's identical
selected-frozen scenario manifest. Measurements from an older denominator are
never compared as if paired.

### 6.2 Canonical encodings and qualification identity

All normative JSON envelopes below use UTF-8 bytes of `JSON.stringify` over an
ordered JSON array: no whitespace, no object-key-order dependence, all digest
strings lowercase 64-character hexadecimal, no Unicode normalization, and no
non-finite JSON number. Raw source bytes are represented as canonical base64.
`SHA256` returns the 32 digest bytes; `hex()` is their lowercase hexadecimal
encoding; `hexToBytes()` requires exactly 64 lowercase hexadecimal characters.
Numeric schema fields are safe non-negative JSON integers.

The frozen schemas publish these digests:

- `subjectRegistryDigest`;
- `buildSchemaDigest`;
- `runtimeSchemaDigest` (Node/V8/OS/architecture and verdict-relevant flags;
  timestamps are receipts, not schema identity);
- `runnerSchemaDigest` (pilot, order, timer, sink, invalidity, and sample law);
- `statisticsSchemaDigest` (this amendment's median/bootstrap/quantile law);
- `scenarioManifestDigest` (the final selected-frozen scenario);
- `harnessBundleDigest`; and
- the candidate and every comparator bundle digest.

For a candidate×scenario batch, let `orderedSubjectArtifacts` be the JSON array
of `[subjectId,bundleDigest]` pairs sorted by subject ID's UTF-8 bytes; it
contains the candidate, every scheduled predecessor, and both scheduled anchor
artifacts. The canonical qualification identity is:

```text
qualificationId = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-qualification-id/1",
  publicOperationId,
  candidateBundleDigest,
  orderedSubjectArtifacts,
  subjectRegistryDigest,
  buildSchemaDigest,
  runtimeSchemaDigest,
  runnerSchemaDigest,
  statisticsSchemaDigest,
  scenarioManifestDigest,
  harnessBundleDigest
]))))
```

The environment receipt binds the actual machine/environment class to the
runtime schema without adding timestamps to `qualificationId`. A peer cell is
the named candidate×baseline projection of this batch. A valid batch
is controlling for this identity and semantic denominator. Administrative
metadata, report paths/layout, logging, comments that do not change emitted
bundle bytes, retry seed, subject-order requests, or an unrelated fixture
append never create a new attempt.

A superseding attempt is permitted only after one predeclared event:

1. emitted candidate bundle bytes change;
2. a verdict-affecting runtime/build/statistics field changes under a new
   versioned schema, a written pre-run causal hypothesis, and the
   pre-measurement review in §6.9; or
3. an independently frozen E-3 corpus/semantic amendment changes that cell's
   denominator and passes §6.9.

Every earlier valid verdict remains in history. The new close matrix names the
causal supersession event and both qualification identities. No nominal digest
change, favorable append, or unchanged valid loss buys another draw.

### 6.3 Exact acyclic T1 row and sampling identity

The T1 freeze uses these closed enums:

- `maturity`: `phase-a-contract`, `snapshot-2026-required`,
  `snapshot-2026-rough`, `snapshot-2026-other`, or
  `owner-experimental`;
- `resultClass`: `accept`, `reject`, or `recover`;
- `operationClass`: `parse`, `exact-serialize`, `edit-serialize`, or
  `canonical-serialize`;
- `sizeClass`, from raw ingress UTF-8 bytes: `short` for 0–63, `medium` for
  64–511, and `long` for 512 or more; and
- `cellStatus`: `mandatory` or `secondary`.

Phase-A rows use `phase-a-contract`; later snapshot/owner rows use the exact
PB0 authority mapping. An ordinary ratified semantic row is `mandatory` by
default. A `secondary` row requires a frozen root disposition and reason in the
T1 freeze; neither a feature author nor a harness author may demote it.

For each deduplicated T1 row, define:

```text
canonicalRowBytes = UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-t1-row/1",
  publicOperationId,
  grammarOwnerModuleId,
  acceptedWaveId,
  fixtureSourceId,
  productionId,
  maturity,
  resultClass,
  operationClass,
  sizeClass,
  cellStatus,
  secondaryReasonOrEmptyString,
  sourceBytesBase64,
  expectedRawResultDigest,
  expectedDiagnosticOrRecoveryDigest,
  semanticProjectionVersion,
  semanticProjectionDigest,
  sortedAffectedJoinIds,
  resourceDispositionId
]))
rowDigest = SHA256(canonicalRowBytes)
```

`sortedAffectedJoinIds` is a JSON array sorted by UTF-8 byte order. Exactly one
non-registry grammar owner is required; joins are thin consumers and cannot own
the row. Deduplicate identical `rowDigest` values. A digest collision with
different `canonicalRowBytes` is structural RED.

For each exact
`publicOperationId×productionId×maturity×resultClass×operationClass×sizeClass`
stratum, sort all distinct `rowDigest` byte strings ascending and define the
acyclic complete-bank envelope:

```text
bankBytes = UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-t1-bank/1",
  publicOperationId,
  productionId,
  maturity,
  resultClass,
  operationClass,
  sizeClass,
  sortedRowDigestHexes
]))
bankDigest = SHA256(bankBytes)
```

The selection key is:

```text
selectionKey = SHA256(
  UTF8("t1-select-v1\0") || bankDigest || rowDigest
)
```

Sort rows by unsigned `selectionKey` bytes, then unsigned `rowDigest` bytes,
then unsigned `canonicalRowBytes`; retain every row if the bank has at most
1,024, otherwise exactly the first 1,024. The impossible-but-defined final
tie is structural RED rather than author choice. Seal both `bankDigest` and:

```text
selectedBankDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-t1-selected-bank/1",
  hex(bankDigest),
  selectedRowDigestHexesInSelectionOrder
]))))
```

The scenario manifest is built only after all bank selections and therefore
does not seed itself. BR-3's peer denominator means every distinct **selected
frozen** common row when the ceiling applies; the complete bank remains
coverage evidence and its digest is never discarded. Later additions append
through E-3 and preserve the superseded complete and selected bank identities.

### 6.4 T1 owner lattice and Phase-A resource dispositions

Before any pilot, one versioned T1 freeze artifact binds every Phase-A row and
cell to:

1. the canonical public operation and exactly one grammar-owner module;
2. accepted wave and fixture source;
3. the closed maturity/result/operation/size enums in §6.3;
4. mandatory/secondary status and root-owned reason;
5. affected thin joins;
6. semantic projection version/digest;
7. complete-bank, selected-bank, and scenario digests; and
8. one Phase-A `resourceDispositionId` record.

The resource record, derived from accepted Phase-A wave contracts, states the
deterministic work counter, limit and `limit+1` behavior, expected complexity
class, scale recipe, profile disposition, and retained-memory disposition for
the public parsing operation. Deterministic work/limit/complexity rows are
mandatory at perfection. Retained memory may be `REPORT_ONLY` only with an
operation-specific frozen reason and must be reported as such, never memory
GREEN. A retained-slope gate exists only when quantity, independent-process
count, fit model, confidence method, and numeric budget were all frozen before
observations.

This T1 artifact owns Phase-A resource closure. PB0 owns corresponding
occurrence-derived T2/T4 Phase-B rows and Gate-2 duties; PB0 does not
retroactively own Phase A. Bench adapters follow the frozen grammar-owner
lattice and thin joins—no omnibus CSS adapter, barrel-owned denominator,
duplicate scanner, or cosmetic BBNF directory claim.

The entire T3 registry, all mandatory T3 strata, provenance, goals, and oracle
classifications also freeze under §6.9 before any candidate pilot. An
owner-approved source is not timing-independent merely because it is real.

### 6.5 Exact pilot `N`, median, order, RNG, and interval

#### Pilot and common `N`

Pilot observations never enter qualification. For each eligible subject in a
fresh pilot process, perform the ordinary semantic check and frozen warmup,
then begin with `pilotN=1`. At each step collect exactly seven elapsed-duration
observations for `pilotN` complete scenario passes. Define their median by the
rule below. If the median is finite, positive, and at least 25 ms, stop for that
subject; otherwise double `pilotN`. `pilotN > 2^30`, a non-finite duration, or
a non-positive duration is `PILOT_INVALID` and no qualification begins.

For each subject compute
`pilotDurationPerPass = medianPilotDuration / pilotN`. Let `dMax` be the
largest such value across the candidate and every comparator scheduled in the
cell. Let `x = 200 / dMax`. Select the qualification `N` from integer powers of
two `2^k`, `0 ≤ k ≤ 30`, minimizing `abs(2^k - x)`; ties select the smaller
power. All subjects use that same `N`. If `N*dMax > 1000 ms`, retain the same
`N`, record `PILOT_TOO_SLOW`, and proceed unless the public operation itself
returns its frozen limit/refusal result; the runner neither divides the corpus
nor invents throughput. If another subject's
predicted `N*pilotDurationPerPass < 50 ms`, retain the same `N`, record
`PILOT_SHORT_WINDOW`, and proceed; the runner may not choose a different `N`
after seeing which subject is fast. These flags and all pilot observations are
part of the receipt, never the verdict sample.

#### Median and numeric validity

Sort finite numeric observations ascending. For odd count `n`, median is
position `(n+1)/2` in one-based notation. For even `n`, median is the
arithmetic mean of positions `n/2` and `n/2+1`. A timed duration or derived
ratio that is not a finite IEEE-754 number, or a duration `≤0`, invalidates the
complete matched block under §6.6. `log` and `exp` use the host's pinned
ECMAScript/Node runtime recorded by `runtimeSchemaDigest`; a non-finite result
is invalid.

#### Seeded balanced subject order

The order seed is:

```text
orderSeed = SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-order/1",
  qualificationId,
  orderedSubjectIds
])))
```

`orderedSubjectIds` is exactly the subject-ID projection of the batch's
`orderedSubjectArtifacts`, initially sorted by UTF-8 byte order. Re-label it by
sorting subjects on
`SHA256(UTF8("v-pi-order-label/1\0") || orderSeed ||
UTF8(subjectId))`, then subject ID as tie-breaker. For `m≥2`, define Williams
base indices `0,1,m-1,2,m-2,3,m-3,...`, emitting each index exactly once. Row
`r` adds `r mod m` to every index modulo `m`. For even `m`, the schedule cycle
is rows `r=0..m-1`; for odd `m`, it is those rows followed by each row's exact
reverse in the same `r` order. Qualification block `b` uses cycle row
`b mod cycleLength`. The runner supports `2≤m≤16`; another count is schema RED,
not author discretion. Candidate and all baselines scheduled for the cell run
once in that row before the next block.

#### Deterministic paired bootstrap

The bootstrap seed is:

```text
bootstrapSeed = SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-bootstrap-seed/1",
  qualificationId,
  statisticsSchemaDigest,
  buildSchemaDigest,
  runtimeSchemaDigest,
  runnerSchemaDigest,
  scenarioManifestDigest,
  candidateBundleDigest,
  baselineBundleDigest,
  "bootstrap-v1"
])))
```

For zero-based resample `r`, draw `d`, and rejection counter `j`, obtain a
64-bit unsigned word from the first eight bytes, big-endian, of:

```text
SHA256(
  UTF8("br6-rng-v1\0") || bootstrapSeed ||
  u32be(r) || u32be(d) || u32be(j)
)
```

For `n` matched blocks, let `limit=floor(2^64/n)*n`. Reject a word `≥limit`,
increment `j`, and hash again; otherwise the selected zero-based block index is
`word mod n`. This is the normative unbiased stream; no library RNG is used.

Produce exactly 10,000 resamples. Each draws `n` paired log-speed ratios with
replacement and takes the median defined above. Sort the 10,000 finite
bootstrap medians. The two-sided 95% type-1 percentile interval is one-based
positions 250 and 9,750, equivalent to
`Q(p)=x[ceil(10000p)]`. Select bounds in log space, then exponentiate both.
Wave qualification uses its five matched blocks (`n=5`); final qualification
uses ten (`n=10`). The 9-of-10 clause applies only to final qualification.

Changing any rule in §§6.2–6.5 creates a new statistics/runner schema cohort,
requires the causal supersession law, and cannot overwrite an old result.

### 6.6 Exact invalidation and no optional stopping

The pre-measurement environment schema freezes numeric power/thermal
availability fields and thresholds supported without privilege. If a field is
unobservable, it is `UNOBSERVABLE`; no post-result human thermal judgment may
invalidate a block. Predeclared invalidities are only: process nonzero exit or
signal; harness schema/digest mismatch; semantic pre/post digest mismatch;
timer/duration/log non-finite or non-positive; machine/environment-class
change inside a block; or a frozen observable threshold breach.

An invalidity discards no individual subject. Retain the failed raw receipt
and rerun the complete matched block at most once under the same scheduled
order. A second invalidity makes the cell `INVALID`. A valid RED,
INCONCLUSIVE, or SLOWER batch is never retried under the same
`qualificationId`. No inspected outlier is deleted.

### 6.7 Exact predecessor durability, ABI, and P-4 wording

Before the first pilot, copy and hash-verify each exact observed LIVE,
deposed, and C14 bundle into the owner-selected durable content-addressed
prototype store, or prove a byte-identical reconstruction. A differently
digested rebuild is an appended peer and cannot discharge the observed peer.
Loss yields `UNAVAILABLE_ARTIFACT`; inability to execute under the frozen ABI
yields `UNSUPPORTED_ABI`. Neither permits substitution, dropping the subject,
nor strict close.

P-3 has the durable aggregate identity already stated. P-4 is more limited:
`../parser-proof/PROFILE-ANALYSIS.md`, SHA-256
`437a7f5f305b286e19dd1da79d3a245ff9e39acd62c91cc4eee165468ee757d0`,
is a durable **historical diagnostic report backed by currently recoverable
temporary inputs**, not a sealed or reproducible profile baseline. Its driver,
analyzer, six raw profiles, exact commands, and manifest are not durable; its
global 4–6.5-second/30k–48k summary is not exact for every file. Historical
self-time percentages and estimated O-1–O-4 gains may motivate new profiles
but are never close gates. Any later admission content-addresses driver,
analyzer, command, flags, bundle/corpus, raw profile, and derived report under
BR-2.

Accordingly, the earlier shorthand that P-3/P-4 are equivalent durable claims
is retracted: only P-3's aggregate is sealed as stated; P-4 has the narrower
status above.

### 6.8 Closed OC-1 choices and local uplift attribution

The owner must select exactly one mutually exclusive historical-floor choice:

- **OC-1A — RETAIN:** strict BR-9 peer-beating GREEN **and** the unchanged P-3
  VALUE `0.0500` and SHEET `0.1000` peak/normalizer clauses are conjunctive

- **OC-1B — LEGACY-CALIBRATION:** the unchanged P-3 compatibility lane still

Neither choice recalibrates a floor, renames legacy code units as bytes, omits
the compatibility run, weakens semantic eligibility, or treats existence of a
peer gate as a GREEN peer result. Silence authorizes neither choice.
Performance law remains subordinate to G-2/PB13G semantics.

For a future acknowledged parse-that uplift, engine attribution is local to a
crossable grammar row:

- an engine effect may be attributed only where old-engine and uplift-engine

- if the current grammar cannot cross to 1.0.0, no engine effect at current

  old-versus-uplift engine effect, but that effect cannot be imputed to an
  uplift-only current grammar; and
- the uplift-API current grammar remains a separately named combined cohort.

This does not weaken BR-10's acknowledged-artifact, independent local replay,
E-3 pin, 1.0.0-retention, or owner-mediated communication laws. No current
uplift is admissible.

### 6.9 Pre-measurement freeze boundary

Owner ratification of the repaired ADDENDA-06 may authorize construction of
prototype benchmark machinery, but **not even a pilot**. Before the first
pilot, one finite freeze packet must contain:

1. the path-independent runner and statistics schemas implementing §§6.2–6.6;
2. the complete BR-2 predecessor registry and durable exact artifacts/ABI
   dispositions;

   thresholds; and

The packet receives one mechanical structural/digest check, then **two
independent semantic/anti-gaming challenges**, then root disposition. Any
normative byte change after a challenge resets both challenges. Only a GREEN
packet opens Phase-A smoke/pilot/qualification. This is a one-time shared
freeze plus append-only E-3 amendments, not a new ceremony per door.

PB0/Gate 2 remains the corresponding Phase-B T2/T4 occurrence/resource freeze
and is not duplicated here. PB0 has no authority before Gate 1; PB13P has no
authority before Gate 2 and cannot waive PB13G.

## 7. Second controlling amendment — 2026-07-21, post fresh B2 challenge

**Status:** controlling formation repair for ADDENDA-06 B2-01 through B2-04.
This section appends to and supersedes only conflicting attempt-identity,
environment, block-count, aggregate, and wave/final-state sentences in §6. All
other §6 algorithms and strictness remain normative. It grants no harness,
pilot, measurement, optimization, PB0, PB13P, or pin-change authority.

### 7.1 Reviewed causal candidate supersession

Changed emitted candidate bytes do not alone open another attempt. Every
initial candidate and every superseding candidate needs a reviewed,
content-addressed causal source receipt.

For a proposed supersession, define `changedPaths` as ordered arrays
`[repoRelativePosixPath,oldSourceSha256OrAbsent,newSourceSha256OrAbsent]`, sorted
by path UTF-8 bytes. Paths are workspace-relative POSIX strings with no empty,
`.` or `..` segment. `affectedOperationIds` is sorted by UTF-8 bytes.
`causalClasses` is a non-empty sorted subset of `semantic`,
`deterministic-work`, `allocation`, and `timing`. The causal hypothesis is a
pre-pilot UTF-8 document explaining the reachable operation path and why the
delta can affect at least one named class. Define:

```text
causalSourceDeltaDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-causal-source-delta/1",
  priorCandidateSourceSetDigestOrGenesis,
  newCandidateSourceSetDigest,
  priorCandidateBundleDigestOrGenesis,
  newCandidateBundleDigest,
  changedPaths,
  affectedOperationIds,
  causalClasses,
  causalHypothesisDigest
]))))
```

The exact delta manifest, source-set receipts, source→bundle reconstruction,
and hypothesis enter the §6.9 pre-measurement review class: one mechanical
content/digest/reachability check, two independent semantic/anti-gaming
challenges, and root disposition, all before a pilot. After GREEN, define:

```text
candidateCausalReceiptDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-candidate-causal-receipt/1",
  causalSourceDeltaDigest,
  mechanicalCheckDigest,
  challengeADigest,
  challengeBDigest,
  rootDispositionDigest
]))))
```

The initial candidate uses `"GENESIS"` for both prior fields and receives the
same review inside the initial freeze packet. A bundle-only, unreachable,
administrative, formatting-only, or reviewed performance-neutral delta does
not receive a new `candidateCausalReceiptDigest`, does not open a pilot, and
does not reseed subject order or bootstrap. A reviewed causal delta preserves
the prior valid verdict and binds its old/new candidate, qualification, and run
identities in the close history. This supersedes §6.2 event 1; all three
supersession events now require content-addressed pre-measurement review.

### 7.2 Portable qualification, environment-bound run, and no-retry scope

The portable `qualificationId` in §6.2 remains the semantic/build/runner/
statistics/scenario cohort shared across eligible environments, but its
canonical ordered array gains, immediately after `candidateBundleDigest`:

```text
candidateCausalReceiptDigest,
closeEnvironmentVectorDigest,
```

`runtimeSchemaDigest` identifies the supported runtime/ABI schema and flags,
not one physical machine. This insertion is normative for
`v-pi-qualification-id/1`; an ID computed without it is not a repaired-law ID.

Before any run, define each environment class from this exact ordered array:

```text
environmentClassDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-environment-class/1",
  ownerAssignedOpaqueMachineId,
  cpuVendor,
  cpuModel,
  cpuMicroarchitectureOrUnobservable,
  physicalCoreCount,
  logicalCoreCount,
  installedMemoryBytes,
  osName,
  osRelease,
  architecture,
  nodeVersion,
  v8Version,
  sortedRelevantRuntimeFlags,
  powerModeOrUnobservable,
  thermalPolicySchemaDigest
]))))
```

Strings use §6.2 canonical JSON rules; flag arrays sort by UTF-8 bytes. The
opaque machine ID is stable within the proof program but need not disclose a
hardware serial. Timestamps and instantaneous thermal/power readings are run
receipts, not class identity. Their frozen invalidity thresholds remain owned
by the runtime/environment schema.

Before the first pilot, the §6.9 packet freezes a non-empty ordered
`requiredEnvironmentClassDigests` array, sorted by digest bytes, and one member
as `machineOfRecordEnvironmentClassDigest`. Define:

```text
closeEnvironmentVectorDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-close-environment-vector/1",
  machineOfRecordEnvironmentClassDigest,
  requiredEnvironmentClassDigests
]))))
```

The array cannot be changed after observations except by an E-3 environment-
cohort amendment that preserves all old vectors and cannot rescue their loss.
An environment not in the required array is a separately named report-only
vector.

For each required or report-only environment, define:

```text
qualificationRunId = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-qualification-run/1",
  qualificationId,
  environmentClassDigest
]))))
```

The one-valid-batch/no-retry law governs `qualificationRunId`: at most one
valid batch exists per portable qualification and environment class, subject
only to §6.6's one whole-block replacement for a predeclared invalidity. A
different environment class may run the same portable qualification once; its
samples and verdicts are never pooled with, averaged against, allowed to
overwrite, or made to compete with another class. Order and bootstrap seeds
use `qualificationRunId` wherever §6.5 previously used `qualificationId`.

Strict close is the conjunction of the independently reported final vectors
for every frozen required environment class. A missing required class is
`UNAVAILABLE_ENVIRONMENT`, an evidence-BLOCKED non-PASS. Report-only classes
cannot cure a required RED/BLOCKED vector. The machine-of-record vector is the
named continuity vector, not a post-result winner selection. Every close row
records `qualificationId`, `qualificationRunId`, environment-class digest,
causal receipt, aggregate, interval, block consistency, and cell/disposition
state.

### 7.3 Complete Williams cycles and exact evidence counts

Let `m` be the number of scheduled subject artifacts in the batch,
`2 ≤ m ≤ 16`. The Williams schedule itself remains exactly §6.5. Its cycle
length is:

```text
cycleLength = m       when m is even
cycleLength = 2 * m   when m is odd
```

Replace fixed five/ten block counts with the smallest complete positive cycle
multiples meeting the old minima:

```text
waveBlockCount  = cycleLength * ceil(5  / cycleLength)
finalBlockCount = cycleLength * ceil(10 / cycleLength)
```

Every block in the resulting full cycles executes once; none is truncated.
Thus the wave always has at least five blocks and the final always has at
least ten, while each subject receives exact Williams position and first-order
carryover balance for the supported `m`. Smoke remains the previously named
non-gating one-block/seven-sample observation and makes no balance or verdict
claim.

The paired bootstrap uses `n=waveBlockCount` or `n=finalBlockCount` without any
other RNG/median/quantile change. Final block consistency is at least 90%:

```text
requiredWinningBlocks = ceil(0.90 * finalBlockCount)
```

This equals the prior 9-of-10 rule when the final count is ten and never
weakens it for a larger complete cycle. All scheduled subject identities and
the resulting `m`, cycle length, block count, and full order sequence are
sealed in `runnerSchemaDigest`/the batch receipt before timing.

### 7.4 One aggregate and distinct wave/final laws

For each candidate×baseline×scenario×environment comparison, compute the
per-block `pairedLogSpeedRatio` exactly as §6.1, then define the only
cross-block point aggregate:

```text
aggregateLogSpeedRatio = median(all pairedLogSpeedRatio values)
aggregateSpeedRatio = exp(aggregateLogSpeedRatio)
```

The median is exactly §6.5, including the arithmetic mean of the two center
**log** values for even block counts. No arithmetic median of raw block ratios,
ratio of separately aggregated durations, or bare cross-block `speedRatio` is
normative. Every point estimate, `0.95` anchor comparison, report, cell state,
and close clause uses `aggregateSpeedRatio`. The bootstrap remains the
distribution of resampled medians of paired log-speed ratios and produces the
same named exponentiated interval.

Wave qualification is regression control, not a final fastest claim. After
semantic/artifact/environment states are applied, each fixed-anchor and
immediate-anchor wave comparison receives exactly one disposition:

- `WAVE_RED`: `aggregateSpeedRatio < 0.95` **and** interval upper bound
  `< 1.00`;
- `WAVE_AMBER`: either (a) interval upper bound `< 1.00` while
  `aggregateSpeedRatio ≥ 0.95`, or (b) `aggregateSpeedRatio < 0.95` while the
  interval upper bound is `≥ 1.00`;
- `WAVE_GREEN`: neither RED nor AMBER; or
- `WAVE_BLOCKED`: the common evidence state is
  `NO_COMMON_DENOMINATOR`, `UNAVAILABLE_ARTIFACT`, `UNSUPPORTED_ABI`,
  `UNAVAILABLE_ENVIRONMENT`, or `INVALID`.

`INCOMPARABLE` and `NOT_APPLICABLE` remain terminal registry dispositions and
cannot disguise an anchor regression. Wave cells do not emit `FASTER`,
`SLOWER`, or `INCONCLUSIVE`, and have no winning-block clause. AMBER remains
debt and must be resolved before perfection.

Final W7/PB13P/perfection peer cells use the §6.1 exhaustive semantic/artifact
states with these exact performance predicates:

- `FASTER`: `aggregateSpeedRatio > 1.00`, interval lower bound `> 1.00`, at
  least `requiredWinningBlocks` individual block ratios `> 1.00`, and every
  mandatory selected-frozen stratum complete;
- `SLOWER`: interval upper bound `< 1.00`; and
- `INCONCLUSIVE`: valid eligible evidence that is neither.

Only final `FASTER` passes strict peer beating. This final state law is not
used to accept a wave; the wave law cannot be used to relax the final state.
At perfection, fixed/immediate anchors retain their WAVE disposition/debt
rows, while registered predecessor cells require final `FASTER` independently.

5. split wave `WAVE_GREEN/AMBER/RED/BLOCKED` regression dispositions from the

## 8. Third controlling amendment — 2026-07-21, post second-amendment A3/B3 challenges

**Status:** controlling formation repair for A6-2A-1/A6-2A-2 and B3-01/B3-02.
This section appends to and supersedes only conflicting causal-delta,
peer-denominator, qualification/batch-plan, schedule-identity, and freeze-
packet sentences in §§6–7. All other §6–§7 algorithms, semantic gates,
strictness, and boundaries remain normative. It grants no harness, pilot,
measurement, optimization, PB0, PB13P, coordination, or pin-change authority.

### 8.1 Canonical causal-source bytes, genesis, and transitions

In `changedPaths`, each row has the exact JSON shape:

```text
[repoRelativePosixPath, oldSourceSha256OrNull, newSourceSha256OrNull]
```

JSON `null` is the sole `ABSENT` sentinel. A non-null source digest is the
lowercase 64-hex SHA-256 of that path's exact raw source bytes. This nullable
union is an explicit exception to §6.2's digest-string rule; `"ABSENT"`, an
empty string, an omitted array member, and any other sentinel are invalid.

The path is a non-empty workspace-relative POSIX string. It has no leading
slash, backslash, NUL, empty segment, `.` segment, or `..` segment. Its bytes
receive no Unicode normalization. `changedPaths` is in strictly ascending
order by the paths' raw UTF-8 bytes and contains exactly one row for each exact
path. Duplicate, out-of-order, or non-canonical paths are structural RED.

A valid transition has at least one non-null side and changes identity:

- addition: `[path,null,newDigest]`;
- deletion: `[path,oldDigest,null]`; or
- modification: `[path,oldDigest,newDigest]` with `oldDigest != newDigest`.

Both-null and equal-digest transitions are invalid. Define the hypothesis from
the exact stored raw bytes of a document that strictly decodes as UTF-8:

```text
causalHypothesisDigest = hex(SHA256(causalHypothesisUtf8Bytes))
```

No newline, BOM, Unicode, or serialization normalization occurs; if a BOM is
present, it is hashed as data. The mechanical check receives the same raw
bytes and reproduces this digest.

The prior source-set and prior bundle fields use the closed union `digest |
"GENESIS"`; literal `"GENESIS"` is the sole non-digest exception. For an
initial candidate, both prior fields are exactly `"GENESIS"`, every
`changedPaths` row is an addition, and at least one row exists. For a
non-genesis candidate, both prior fields are lowercase 64-hex digests and
`"GENESIS"` is forbidden. New source-set and bundle fields are always
lowercase 64-hex digests. These type and transition rules are part of the
versioned causal-delta schema and the pre-GREEN mechanical review. Failure
opens no causal receipt, qualification, or pilot.

### 8.2 Exact complete pairwise denominator ownership

BR-3 and BR-9 are pairwise laws. A candidate's denominator against baseline
`B1` is never the multiway intersection of candidate, `B1`, `B2`, anchors, or
any other scheduled subject. Before a batch plan is frozen, construct one
complete candidate×baseline denominator independently for every registered
predecessor, fixed anchor, and immediate anchor.

For every selected-frozen bank stratum, mechanically enumerate **all and only**
rows on which that candidate and that baseline satisfy BR-3's same-operation,
same-cost-boundary, spec-correct semantic eligibility. There is no second
sampling step after the §6.3 selected-bank freeze. Retain every mandatory
stratum in the manifest, including an empty row list, so an empty mandatory
pairwise stratum deterministically yields `NO_COMMON_DENOMINATOR` instead of
disappearing.

For **every** row in every relevant selected bank—not only included rows—an
ordered eligibility receipt binds the frozen `rowDigest`, stratum fields and
mandatory/secondary disposition, exact input/source identity, candidate and
baseline raw-result/diagnostic receipts, both subject projection digests, the
common semantic-projection version/digest when one exists, the candidate and
baseline cost-boundary digests, the common cost-boundary digest when one
exists, sorted affected-join IDs, and the BR-3 inclusion or closed exclusion
disposition. Omitting a selected-bank row is structural RED. Sort receipts
within a stratum by unsigned `rowDigest` bytes and strata by the UTF-8 bytes of
their canonical §6.3 stratum tuple. Define:

```text
pairwiseEligibilityReceiptsDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-pairwise-eligibility-receipts/1",
  candidateSubjectId,
  candidateBundleDigest,
  baselineSubjectId,
  baselineBundleDigest,
  orderedCompleteSelectedBankEligibilityReceipts
]))))

pairwiseDenominatorDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-pairwise-denominator/1",
  publicOperationId,
  commonCostBoundaryDigest,
  orderedPairwiseStrataIncludingEmptyMandatoryStrata,
  orderedIncludedRowDigests,
  orderedInputSourceDigests,
  commonSemanticProjectionVersion,
  commonSemanticProjectionDigest,
  sortedAffectedJoinIds
]))))

peerCellManifestDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-peer-cell-manifest/1",
  candidateSubjectId,
  candidateBundleDigest,
  baselineSubjectId,
  baselineBundleDigest,
  pairwiseDenominatorDigest,
  pairwiseEligibilityReceiptsDigest
]))))
```

`pairwiseEligibilityReceiptsDigest` content-addresses the ordered full
candidate/baseline eligibility and semantic/result receipts described above;
it cannot be a bare PASS flag. The denominator lists the exact included row
and input identities in the same order used by the scenario traversal; its
stratum list is complete and records empty mandatory strata. Thus every peer/
anchor cell has its own immutable `peerCellManifestDigest`, while
`pairwiseDenominatorDigest` independently names the exact common bytes,
strata, joins, common projection, and common cost boundary that may be shared
by a physical batch. Subject-specific projection and eligibility receipts are
bound by the peer-cell identity without falsely making equal common workloads
different denominators.

Multiple baselines may share one Williams batch only when a mechanical check
proves their `pairwiseDenominatorDigest`, `publicOperationId`, common-cost-
boundary digest, ordered input rows, stratum dispositions, join set, and
common-projection version/digest are byte-identical. Otherwise each distinct
pairwise workload receives a separate cohort and schedule. Fixed and
immediate anchors follow the same rule; their status never grants an exception.
Grouping changes execution economy only. Each close/wave cell retains its own
baseline artifact, `pairwiseDenominatorDigest`, and `peerCellManifestDigest`,
and no grouped subject can
shrink another cell's complete pairwise denominator.

### 8.3 Acyclic batch plan, qualification, schedule, and activation

`runnerSchemaDigest` identifies only invariant runner algorithms and schema
constants: pilot, Williams construction, complete-cycle formulas, timer,
sink, invalidity, samples, and receipt law. It contains no candidate or
subject artifact, peer denominator, qualification/run ID, derived relabelling,
block order, batch receipt, or schedule receipt.

`qualificationKind` is the closed enum `wave` or `final`. Smoke remains a
separate non-gating observation and is not a qualification kind. For one
candidate and one set of mechanically groupable pairwise workloads, define
`orderedPeerCells` as
`[baselineSubjectId,baselineBundleDigest,pairwiseDenominatorDigest,
peerCellManifestDigest]` rows sorted by baseline subject ID's UTF-8 bytes.
Define the pre-review batch plan:

```text
batchPlanDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-batch-plan/1",
  qualificationKind,
  publicOperationId,
  candidateSubjectId,
  candidateBundleDigest,
  orderedPeerCells,
  pairwiseDenominatorDigest,
  commonCostBoundaryDigest,
  orderedSubjectArtifacts,
  m,
  cycleLength,
  blockCount
]))))
```

`blockCount` is `waveBlockCount` for `wave` and `finalBlockCount` for `final`.
`m`, `cycleLength`, and the count are mechanically evaluated from the frozen
subject set and §7.3 formulas. The batch plan contains no derived relabelling
or order sequence. A different qualification kind, pairwise denominator,
grouping, subject artifact, or block count has a different plan and cannot
reuse another kind's run.

The canonical qualification identity is now version 2. It replaces the old
single `scenarioManifestDigest`/underspecified peer projection with the exact
batch plan and pairwise-cell mapping:

```text
qualificationId = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-qualification-id/2",
  candidateCausalReceiptDigest,
  closeEnvironmentVectorDigest,
  batchPlanDigest,
  orderedPeerCells,
  subjectRegistryDigest,
  buildSchemaDigest,
  runtimeSchemaDigest,
  runnerSchemaDigest,
  statisticsSchemaDigest,
  harnessBundleDigest
]))))
```

`qualificationRunId` remains the §7.2 hash of this `qualificationId` and one
exact `environmentClassDigest`. Its one-valid-run/no-retry law is unchanged.
Order and bootstrap remain environment-bound through `qualificationRunId`.

The exact dependency order is normative:

1. **T0 — freeze inputs:** freeze causal delta/hypothesis, source/build
   reconstruction, invariant schema digests, subject artifacts, independent
   peer-denominator manifests, mechanically valid grouping/batch plans,
   qualification kind, T1/T3 data, and the environment cohort/vector.
2. **T1 — review:** run the one mechanical check, two independent semantic/
   anti-gaming challenges, and root disposition on those bytes.
3. **T2 — causal receipt:** form `candidateCausalReceiptDigest` from T0/T1.
4. **T3 — portable identity:** form `qualificationId` version 2.
5. **T4 — environment run:** form `qualificationRunId`.
6. **T5 — schedule:** derive `orderSeed`, subject relabelling, and the complete
   Williams block sequence mechanically from the reviewed plan and T4 ID.
7. **T6 — schedule receipt:** content-address the derived sequence as below.
8. **T7 — activation:** reproduce all prior identities and algorithms, seal a
   mechanical activation receipt, then—and only then—permit the pilot.

No value first formed at T2–T7 may feed T0/T1, `batchPlanDigest`,
`runnerSchemaDigest`, any challenge/root digest, or any input digest used to
form `candidateCausalReceiptDigest` or `qualificationId`. In particular, the
pre-GREEN packet contains the sorted subject artifacts, pairwise manifests,
`m`, cycle length/count, qualification kind, and invariant order algorithm,
but not `qualificationId`, `qualificationRunId`, an ID-derived relabelling,
complete order sequence, `batchScheduleDigest`, or activation receipt.

After T5, define:

```text
batchScheduleDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-batch-schedule/1",
  qualificationRunId,
  batchPlanDigest,
  qualificationKind,
  orderedSubjectArtifacts,
  m,
  cycleLength,
  blockCount,
  completeOrderSequence
]))))
```

`completeOrderSequence` contains exactly `blockCount` arrays of subject IDs
and is the sole §6.5 Williams derivation; it accepts no caller order. A
mechanical verifier recomputes the causal receipt, both IDs, plan, counts,
seed, relabelling, sequence, and schedule digest from frozen bytes. Define:

```text
mechanicalScheduleVerificationDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-schedule-verification/1",
  batchPlanDigest,
  qualificationRunId,
  batchScheduleDigest,
  "PASS"
]))))

batchActivationReceiptDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-batch-activation/1",
  candidateCausalReceiptDigest,
  qualificationId,
  qualificationRunId,
  runnerSchemaDigest,
  batchScheduleDigest,
  mechanicalScheduleVerificationDigest
]))))
```

The activation receipt and full sequence enter the pre-pilot batch receipt,
not the pre-GREEN freeze packet or `runnerSchemaDigest`. A mismatch is
structural RED and opens no pilot. This deterministic post-GREEN derivation is
not a new draw, retry seed, human order choice, or semantic amendment. A
normative source, schema, denominator, grouping, environment-vector, or plan
change returns through its governing causal/E-3 review and preserves history.
