## 9. Fourth controlling amendment — 2026-07-21, post third-amendment B4 challenge

**Status:** controlling formation repair for B4-01 and B4-02. This section
appends to and supersedes only the conflicting §8.3 no-backedge sentence and
the pilot attempt, identity, invalidity, receipt, and bootstrap inputs in
§§6–8. Every non-conflicting causal, pairwise-denominator, peer-cell, batch-
plan, environment, schedule, statistics, verdict, authority, and scope rule
remains normative. It authorizes no harness, pilot, measurement, optimization,
PB0, PB13P, coordination, or pin change.

### 9.1 Exact forward-only stage exclusions

The §8.3 sentence beginning “No value first formed at T2–T7” is **RETRACTED**.
It incorrectly prohibited the required T2→T3 edge. The following exclusions
are the sole controlling no-backedge law:

- no T2-or-later value feeds T0 freeze bytes, T1 mechanical/challenge/root
  bytes, `batchPlanDigest`, or `runnerSchemaDigest`;
- no T3-or-later value feeds the T2 `candidateCausalReceiptDigest`;
- no T4-or-later value feeds the T3 `qualificationId`;
- no T5-or-later value feeds the T4 `qualificationRunId`;
- no T6-or-later value feeds the T5 order seed, relabelling, or derived
  Williams sequence;
- no T7-or-later value feeds the T6 `batchScheduleDigest` or mechanical
  schedule-verification digest;
- no T8-or-later value feeds the T7 `batchActivationReceiptDigest`; and
- no T9 qualification observation or result feeds the T8 pilot attempt,
  observations, selected common `N`, flags, status, or panel digest.

The required forward chain is exact and permitted:

```text
T0 frozen inputs and batch plans
  -> T1 reviews/root
  -> T2 candidateCausalReceiptDigest
  -> T3 qualificationId
  -> T4 qualificationRunId
  -> T5 derived schedule
  -> T6 schedule/verification receipts
  -> T7 activation receipt
  -> T8 one-shot pilot panel and common N or terminal PILOT_INVALID
  -> T9 qualification samples and verdict, only after a valid T8 panel
```

Every arrow points strictly forward. A later receipt may content-address prior
stage identities but can never alter or reseed them.

### 9.2 One durable pilot attempt per activated run

Each exact `qualificationRunId` plus `batchActivationReceiptDigest` owns one
and only one pilot attempt:

```text
pilotAttemptId = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-pilot-attempt/1",
  qualificationRunId,
  batchActivationReceiptDigest
]))))

pilotLaunchReceiptDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-pilot-launch/1",
  pilotAttemptId,
  qualificationRunId,
  batchActivationReceiptDigest,
  environmentClassDigest,
  runnerSchemaDigest,
  "LAUNCHED"
]))))
```

Before any pilot clock read, warmup, semantic observation, or subject process
launch, the executor must atomically create-if-absent a durable launch record
keyed by `pilotAttemptId` and containing the exact launch receipt above. Only
the successful creator may proceed. If the key already exists in `LAUNCHED`,
`PILOT_VALID`, or `PILOT_INVALID` state, no executor may start or restart a
pilot. A store failure before the create commits produces no observation and
no launch; once the create commits, the attempt is irrevocably consumed.

Timestamps and executor/process metadata may accompany the record as report
fields but do not change either deterministic identity. A process, executor,
machine, or controller crash after durable launch cannot reopen the attempt.
The supervisor or later proof resumption finalizes any launched attempt that
did not atomically commit one complete valid panel as `PILOT_INVALID` with the
retained launch and failure receipt. It may not resume the adaptive sequence,
replace the panel, or choose a new pilot under the same run/activation.

### 9.3 Canonical pilot subject, round, and panel receipts

`pilotSubjectOrder` is exactly the first subject-ID row of the T6
`completeOrderSequence`. It contains every `orderedSubjectArtifacts` subject
exactly once. Subjects execute sequentially in that order. Each subject gets
one fresh process and one internal adaptive sequence; a round, subject, or
process is never restarted.

For subject order index `s`, start at round `k=0` with `pilotN=2^k`. Perform
the frozen semantic check and warmup once in that subject process, then collect
the fixed sequence of seven observations for `pilotN` complete scenario
passes; a terminal invalidity ends that array as a shorter ordered prefix. A
pilot observation is the
ordered JSON union `["OK",elapsedDurationMilliseconds]`,
where the duration is a finite positive JSON number, or
`["INVALID",pilotInvalidityCode]`. The panel invalidity code is exactly one of:

```text
PROCESS_NONZERO_EXIT
PROCESS_SIGNAL
HARNESS_SCHEMA_DIGEST_MISMATCH
SEMANTIC_PRE_POST_DIGEST_MISMATCH
TIMER_OR_DURATION_NONFINITE
DURATION_NONPOSITIVE
ENVIRONMENT_CLASS_CHANGE
FROZEN_THRESHOLD_BREACH
PILOT_N_EXHAUSTED
PILOT_DERIVATION_INVALID
CRASH_OR_INCOMPLETE
```

An invalid observation stops the subject and panel immediately. Otherwise
compute the seven-observation median by §6.5. If it is at least 25 ms, the
subject is `SUBJECT_SELECTED`. If it is below 25 ms and `k<30`, the round is
`DOUBLE` and the same process continues once with `k+1`. If it is below 25 ms
at `k=30`, the next `pilotN` would exceed `2^30`; record
`PILOT_N_EXHAUSTED` in the failure receipt and stop `PILOT_INVALID`. Thus each
subject has one finite
prefix of the predetermined `1,2,4,...,2^30` sequence and no author-selected
round, process, or stopping point.

For every begun round, retain its ordered observation prefix, including an
invalid terminal observation when present, and define:

```text
pilotRoundDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-pilot-round/1",
  pilotAttemptId,
  subjectId,
  subjectOrderIndex,
  roundIndex,
  pilotN,
  orderedObservationUnionArray,
  medianDurationMillisecondsOrNull,
  roundStatus
]))))
```

Each observation union is durably appended to the attempt log before the next
observation begins, and each round digest is durably appended before the next
round or subject begins. Therefore a crash has one reconstructible prefix; it
cannot erase an inspected observation and begin again.

Here JSON `null` is the sole absent median, and `roundStatus` is exactly
`DOUBLE`, `SUBJECT_SELECTED`, or `PILOT_INVALID`. A completed subject receipt
is:

```text
pilotSubjectReceiptDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-pilot-subject/1",
  pilotAttemptId,
  subjectId,
  subjectOrderIndex,
  orderedPilotRoundDigests,
  selectedPilotNOrNull,
  pilotDurationPerPassOrNull,
  subjectStatus
]))))
```

The nullable fields are non-null exactly for `subjectStatus =
"SUBJECT_SELECTED"`; otherwise the status is `"PILOT_INVALID"`. For a
selected subject, `pilotDurationPerPass = selectedMedianDuration / selectedPilotN`
is the exact finite positive host result under the frozen runtime schema.

After every subject is selected, derive `dMax`, `x=200/dMax`, and common
qualification `N` by the existing §6.5 nearest-power-of-two/tie-smaller rule.
If any selected median, `pilotDurationPerPass`, `dMax`, `x`, or common-`N`
derivation is non-finite, non-positive, or outside that closed power-of-two
rule, finalize `PILOT_DERIVATION_INVALID`; no implementation may clamp or
choose a fallback `N`.
In pilot-subject order, record `[subjectId,"PILOT_SHORT_WINDOW"]` for every
subject whose predicted `N*pilotDurationPerPass < 50 ms`; record the single
row `["","PILOT_TOO_SLOW"]` first when `N*dMax > 1000 ms`. No other flag or
flag order exists. Those flags do not authorize a different `N`.

For any invalid terminal, define the exact failure receipt from the completed
subject-receipt prefix plus the begun subject/round/observation prefix:

```text
pilotFailureReceiptDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-pilot-failure/1",
  pilotAttemptId,
  pilotInvalidityCode,
  failingSubjectIdOrEmptyString,
  failingSubjectOrderIndexOrNull,
  failingRoundIndexOrNull,
  failingObservationIndexOrNull,
  orderedCompletedSubjectReceiptDigests,
  orderedFailingSubjectRoundDigests,
  failingSubjectReceiptDigestOrEmptyString,
  terminalPilotRoundDigestOrEmptyString
]))))
```

JSON `null` is the sole absent numeric index. A crash before a round receipt
uses empty subject/digest strings and an empty failing-round array where
unavailable. `orderedCompletedSubjectReceiptDigests` contains only fully
selected subjects preceding the failure; the failing subject's complete
durable round prefix is bound separately and its subject receipt is included
when it could be finalized. A failure after all subjects selected uses the
complete selected-subject array and empty failing-subject fields. All use
`CRASH_OR_INCOMPLETE`; durable raw process/controller metadata remains beside
the receipt. Define exactly one atomically finalized panel:

```text
pilotPanelDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-pilot-panel/1",
  pilotAttemptId,
  pilotLaunchReceiptDigest,
  pilotSubjectOrder,
  orderedCompletedSubjectReceiptDigests,
  pilotPanelStatus,
  selectedCommonNOrNull,
  orderedPilotFlags,
  pilotFailureReceiptDigestOrEmptyString
]))))
```

`pilotPanelStatus` is exactly `PILOT_VALID` or `PILOT_INVALID`. A valid panel
contains one completed selected-subject receipt for every subject in order, a
non-null common `N`, canonical flags, and an empty failure string. An invalid
panel contains JSON `null` for common `N`, no qualification flags, and one
failure digest. The durable state transition from `LAUNCHED` to either final
status is atomic and exclusive. Both valid and invalid panels are terminal:
there is no panel replacement, invalid-pilot replacement, re-pilot, extra
round, or second choice of `N` for that attempt, run, or activation.

### 9.4 Qualification identity, bootstrap, receipts, and exhaustive failure

Only a `PILOT_VALID` panel opens T9. Before any T9 sample, define:

```text
qualificationExecutionId = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-qualification-execution/1",
  qualificationRunId,
  batchActivationReceiptDigest,
  pilotAttemptId,
  pilotPanelDigest,
  selectedCommonN
]))))
```

The T9 runner uses exactly that `selectedCommonN`; it cannot recompute or
replace it. The bootstrap seed in §6.5/BR-6 is superseded for this cohort by:

```text
bootstrapSeed = SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-bootstrap-seed/2",
  qualificationExecutionId,
  pilotPanelDigest,
  selectedCommonN,
  statisticsSchemaDigest,
  buildSchemaDigest,
  runtimeSchemaDigest,
  runnerSchemaDigest,
  batchPlanDigest,
  pairwiseDenominatorDigest,
  candidateBundleDigest,
  baselineBundleDigest,
  "bootstrap-v2"
])))
```

Every pilot raw/round/subject/panel artifact is retained. Every T9 raw sample,
matched-block, batch, bootstrap, aggregate, wave/final cell, history,
supersession, and close receipt binds `pilotAttemptId`,
`pilotLaunchReceiptDigest`, `pilotPanelDigest`, `pilotPanelStatus`, common `N`
or JSON `null`, canonical flags, `qualificationExecutionId` or the empty
string, and the already required plan/schedule/peer/environment identities.
No report may substitute another panel or merely cite `qualificationRunId`.

`PILOT_INVALID` is now an exact predeclared BR-5/§6.6 invalidity cause and
maps, without adding a new cell vocabulary, to existing `INVALID` for every
peer/anchor cell in that activated batch/environment. No T9 qualification
begins. Every affected wave cell is therefore `WAVE_BLOCKED`; every affected

replacement applies only after a valid pilot has opened T9. A crash or
incomplete state after durable pilot launch has this same terminal mapping and

## 10. Fifth controlling amendment — 2026-07-21, post fourth-amendment A5/B5 challenges

**Status:** controlling formation repair for A6-4A-1 and B5-01 through B5-03.
This section appends to and supersedes only conflicting pilot terminal-
serialization, equal-work grouping, normative-number encoding, and T9
execution-lifecycle sentences in §§6–9. Every non-conflicting causal,
pairwise, environment, schedule, statistical, verdict, scope, and authority
law remains normative. It authorizes no harness, smoke, pilot, measurement,
optimization, PB0, PB13P, coordination, or pin change.

### 10.1 Canonical integer and measurement-scalar encodings

The earlier unqualified sentence “Numeric schema fields are safe non-negative
JSON integers” is narrowed as follows.

- A declared schema/control count, zero-based index, enum ordinal, subject
  count, block count, sample count, exponent, `N`, `pilotN`, or other
  non-observational mathematically integral schema field is a JSON number only when it is a non-negative safe
  integer (`0 ≤ x ≤ 2^53-1`); overflow is structural RED.
- Every exact observed unsigned integer counter—including raw monotonic clock
  ticks/nanoseconds, ingress bytes, UTF units/code points, tokens, nodes,
  diagnostics, allocations, retained bytes, and deterministic-work counts—uses
  exactly `u64be:` followed by sixteen lowercase hexadecimal digits encoding
  the unsigned 64-bit big-endian value, even when it is below `2^53`. Overflow
  is invalid evidence. A duration receipt may retain raw `u64be` start/end/delta
  counters beside the required derived `f64be` duration; statistics consume
  only the declared `f64be` duration. Decode `u64be` to an exact unsigned
  integer/BigInt and perform counter arithmetic exactly; conversion to
  ECMAScript `Number` is permitted only after proving the value is a safe
  integer, otherwise the frozen limit/invalidity law controls.
- Every finite duration, timestamp delta, median, per-pass value, sample,
  throughput, ratio, logarithm, exponential, aggregate, deviation, coefficient,
  slope, bound, interval endpoint, quantile, derived memory/allocation
  statistic, and every other continuous or derived numeric scalar is an exact IEEE-754 binary64
  **bit string**, serialized as literal `f64be:` followed by exactly sixteen
  lowercase hexadecimal digits in big-endian byte order. Call this string
  `f64beScalar`.
- Encode by converting the pinned ECMAScript `Number` to its raw big-endian
  IEEE-754 binary64 bytes, lower-hex encoding all eight bytes, and prepending
  `f64be:`. Decode the sixteen digits after that exact prefix to `Number`;
  perform arithmetic, comparisons, `log`, and `exp`
  only under the pinned Node/V8 runtime; then encode the resulting bits again.
  No decimal parsing convention, rounding, fixed precision, locale, or JSON
  number spelling participates. The sign bit of zero is preserved.
- A NaN or positive/negative infinity has no scalar encoding. Raw pilot timer/
  duration failure maps to `TIMER_OR_DURATION_NONFINITE`; pilot derived-value
  failure maps to `PILOT_DERIVATION_INVALID`; T9 raw or derived measurement
  failure maps to the predeclared `MEASUREMENT_NONFINITE` invalidity. No
  implementation may clamp, coerce, omit, or stringify a non-finite value.

Critical constants have these exact binary64 encodings:

```text
25 ms   = f64be:4039000000000000
50 ms   = f64be:4049000000000000
200 ms  = f64be:4069000000000000
1000 ms = f64be:408f400000000000
0.90    = f64be:3feccccccccccccd
0.95    = f64be:3fee666666666666
1.00    = f64be:3ff0000000000000
```

Thresholds are decoded from those bits; all other frozen numeric thresholds
publish the same `f64be:` plus sixteen-hex representation. Define:

```text
numericEncodingSchemaDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-numeric-encoding/1",
  "safe-nonnegative-json-integer-for-schema-and-control-fields",
  "u64be-colon-big-endian-lowercase-hex16-for-all-observed-unsigned-counters",
  "f64be-colon-ieee754-binary64-big-endian-lowercase-hex16-for-all-scalars",
  "preserve-signed-zero",
  "nonfinite-is-invalid"
]))))
```

This digest is frozen at T0 and enters every batch plan, qualification,
activation, pilot, T9, statistics/bootstrap, raw evidence, history, and close
receipt. Every earlier pilot example containing a finite measurement as a JSON
number is superseded: the exact observation union is
`["OK",elapsedDurationF64beScalar]`; median and per-pass fields are
`f64beScalar | null`. Every T9 sample and every BR-6 point/interval field uses
the same scalar string. Existing integer formulas remain mathematical formulas;
their declared integral outputs retain the safe-integer representation.

### 10.2 One append-only pilot WAL and total terminal serialization

The §9 pilot-launch tag is superseded by:

```text
pilotLaunchReceiptDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-pilot-launch/2",
  pilotAttemptId,
  qualificationRunId,
  batchActivationReceiptDigest,
  environmentClassDigest,
  runnerSchemaDigest,
  numericEncodingSchemaDigest,
  "LAUNCHED"
]))))
```

The pilot's sole authority is one append-only, hash-chained write-ahead log
(`pilotWAL`) keyed by `pilotAttemptId`. Event index `i` is the zero-based
position in that log. Event zero is the already atomically created launch. For
every event:

```text
pilotWalEventDigest[i] = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-pilot-wal-event/1",
  pilotAttemptId,
  i,
  i == 0 ? "GENESIS" : pilotWalEventDigest[i-1],
  numericEncodingSchemaDigest,
  eventType,
  eventPayload
]))))
```

`"GENESIS"` is the sole previous-event sentinel. Append is atomic and durable;
an action cannot begin until its intent event is durable, and its completion
event is durable before the next action or intent. The closed event/payload
schemas are:

```text
PILOT_LAUNCH          [pilotLaunchReceiptDigest]
SUBJECT_INTENT        [s,subjectId]
ROUND_INTENT          [s,k,pilotN]
SUBJECT_READY         [s,k,subjectId,semanticPreDigest,warmupReceiptDigest,
                       subjectProcessReceiptDigest]
OBSERVATION_INTENT    [s,k,o]
OBSERVATION_OK        [s,k,o,elapsedDurationF64beScalar]
OBSERVATION_INVALID   [s,k,o,pilotInvalidityCode]
ROUND_COMPLETE        [s,k,pilotRoundDigest,roundStatus]
SUBJECT_COMPLETE      [s,pilotSubjectReceiptDigest,subjectStatus]
FAILURE_DECISION      [pilotInvalidityCode,sOrNull,kOrNull,oOrNull,
                       decidedPilotWalPrefixDigest]
PILOT_FAILURE         [pilotFailureReceiptDigest]
PANEL_COMPLETE        [pilotPanelStatus,pilotPanelDigest]
```

Indices and derivations are exact:

- `s` is the zero-based position of `subjectId` in `pilotSubjectOrder`;
- `k` is the zero-based adaptive round index and `pilotN = 2^k`, `0≤k≤30`;
- `o` is the zero-based position in the round, `0≤o≤6`;
- `SUBJECT_INTENT` values must advance in subject order; `ROUND_INTENT` values
  advance in `k`; and observation intents advance in `o`;
- for `k=0`, `SUBJECT_INTENT` then `ROUND_INTENT` are durable before subject
  process launch, semantic precheck, or warmup, and `SUBJECT_READY` completes
  that setup; later rounds do not repeat subject setup; and
- a payload/index/order mismatch is
  `HARNESS_SCHEMA_DIGEST_MISMATCH`, never an alternate interpretation.

At any instant there is at most one unmatched intent because subjects, rounds,
and observations are sequential. When several invalid predicates are proven
by the same durable prefix, choose the **first** matching code in this exact
precedence list:

```text
PROCESS_SIGNAL
PROCESS_NONZERO_EXIT
HARNESS_SCHEMA_DIGEST_MISMATCH
SEMANTIC_PRE_POST_DIGEST_MISMATCH
ENVIRONMENT_CLASS_CHANGE
FROZEN_THRESHOLD_BREACH
TIMER_OR_DURATION_NONFINITE
DURATION_NONPOSITIVE
PILOT_N_EXHAUSTED
PILOT_DERIVATION_INVALID
CRASH_OR_INCOMPLETE
```

Only predicates established by durable bytes participate. Thus a recorded
signal wins over every other simultaneous predicate; a recorded nonzero exit
wins over schema/semantic/timer failure; and `CRASH_OR_INCOMPLETE` is used only
when no earlier cause is provable. `PILOT_N_EXHAUSTED` applies only after seven durable OK values at
`k=30` have a decoded median below 25 ms. `PILOT_DERIVATION_INVALID` applies
only after every subject is selected and common-`N` derivation is invalid.

#### Deterministic live close and crash recovery

Given the longest structurally valid durable WAL prefix, the following is a
total function; live failure and recovery execute the same steps:

Recovery begins only after a durable controller/process termination receipt or
proof-program restart with no live owner of the atomic attempt record; no
elapsed-time guess or result inspection may declare a running pilot abandoned.

1. If an exclusive `PANEL_COMPLETE` exists, that terminal is authoritative and
   no event may append.
2. Otherwise choose the invalidity code and derived indices by the precedence
   above. WAL corruption
   or an impossible event transition selects
   `HARNESS_SCHEMA_DIGEST_MISMATCH` and content-addresses the corrupt suffix as
   adjacent evidence; it never discards the valid prefix.
   Compute `decidedPilotWalPrefixDigest` from that exact valid prefix.
3. Atomically compare-and-append exactly one `FAILURE_DECISION` against the
   current last valid event digest. Its code, indices, and decided-prefix digest
   are thereafter authoritative. A competing finalizer must read and follow
   the committed decision. If recovery itself stops, a later finalizer appends
   only the still-missing deterministic suffix; it never re-decides or executes
   pilot work.
4. If an `OBSERVATION_INTENT(s,k,o)` lacks completion, append exactly
   `OBSERVATION_INVALID[s,k,o,code]`. If no observation intent is unmatched,
   no observation completion is synthesized.
5. If `ROUND_INTENT(s,k,pilotN)` exists without `ROUND_COMPLETE`, synthesize
   exactly one version-2 terminal `pilotRoundDigest` from **all** durable
   observation completions for that round, in `o` order, with median `null`
   and status `PILOT_INVALID`, then append `ROUND_COMPLETE`. This occurs even
   when the observation array is empty. When the committed failure locus has a
   non-null `k`, `terminalPilotRoundDigest` is exactly that round's preexisting
   or synthesized `ROUND_COMPLETE` digest. It is empty if and only if `k` is
   null.
6. If `SUBJECT_INTENT(s,subjectId)` exists without `SUBJECT_COMPLETE`, synthesize
   exactly one version-2 invalid `pilotSubjectReceiptDigest` from every durable
   round digest for that subject, selected-N/per-pass `null`, and status
   `PILOT_INVALID`, then append `SUBJECT_COMPLETE`. If no unmatched subject
   intent exists, no subject is synthesized. When the committed locus has a
   non-null `s`, `failingSubjectReceiptDigest` is exactly that subject's
   preexisting or synthesized `SUBJECT_COMPLETE` digest. It is empty if and
   only if `s` is null.
7. Compute `panelPilotWalPrefixDigest` from the prefix after all deterministic
   synthesis. Append the version-2 failure receipt, compute the version-2
   invalid panel, and append the
   exclusive `PILOT_FAILURE` then `PANEL_COMPLETE` events. No process, timer,
   semantic check, warmup, round, observation, or derivation is rerun.

The zero-based failure indices are derived, never selected:

- an observation failure encodes its exact `s,k,o`;
- a failure after a round's seven observations (including exhaustion) encodes
  `s,k,null`;
- setup/precheck/process/warmup failure after the required first round intent
  encodes `s,0,null`; only a crash between durable `SUBJECT_INTENT` and
  `ROUND_INTENT` encodes `s,null,null`;
- common-panel derivation failure after all subjects encodes
  `null,null,null`; and
- an unmatched intent from a crash uses that intent's exact indices under the
  same rules.

For any named prefix, define:

```text
pilotWalPrefixDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-pilot-wal-prefix/1",
  pilotAttemptId,
  eventCount,
  orderedPilotWalEventDigests
]))))
```

The controlling version-2 terminal envelopes are:

```text
pilotRoundDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-pilot-round/2",
  pilotAttemptId,s,k,pilotN,
  orderedObservationUnions,
  medianDurationF64beScalarOrNull,
  roundStatus,
  numericEncodingSchemaDigest
]))))

pilotSubjectReceiptDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-pilot-subject/2",
  pilotAttemptId,s,subjectId,
  orderedPilotRoundDigests,
  selectedPilotNOrNull,
  pilotDurationPerPassF64beScalarOrNull,
  subjectStatus,
  numericEncodingSchemaDigest
]))))

pilotFailureReceiptDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-pilot-failure/2",
  pilotAttemptId,
  pilotInvalidityCode,
  failingSubjectIdOrEmptyString,
  failingSubjectOrderIndexOrNull,
  failingRoundIndexOrNull,
  failingObservationIndexOrNull,
  orderedCompletedSelectedSubjectReceiptDigests,
  orderedFailingSubjectRoundDigests,
  failingSubjectReceiptDigestOrEmptyString,
  terminalPilotRoundDigestOrEmptyString,
  decidedPilotWalPrefixDigest,
  panelPilotWalPrefixDigest,
  failureDecisionEventDigest,
  corruptWalSuffixDigestOrEmptyString,
  numericEncodingSchemaDigest
]))))

pilotPanelDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-pilot-panel/2",
  pilotAttemptId,
  pilotLaunchReceiptDigest,
  pilotSubjectOrder,
  orderedCompletedSubjectReceiptDigests,
  pilotPanelStatus,
  selectedCommonNOrNull,
  orderedPilotFlags,
  pilotFailureReceiptDigestOrEmptyString,
  panelPilotWalPrefixDigest,
  numericEncodingSchemaDigest
]))))
```

For an invalid panel,
`orderedCompletedSelectedSubjectReceiptDigests` contains exactly the selected
subjects strictly before the failure; `orderedFailingSubjectRoundDigests` and
the failing-subject receipt obey steps 5–6, with no “when available” choice.
`corruptWalSuffixDigestOrEmptyString` is
`hex(SHA256(exactRawCorruptSuffixBytes))` only for a structural WAL failure and
is otherwise empty. In an
invalid panel, `orderedCompletedSubjectReceiptDigests` is exactly the receipt
projection of every durable `SUBJECT_COMPLETE` event in WAL order, including
the deterministic synthesized failing-subject receipt when one exists. For a
valid panel it contains every selected subject in order, and
`panelPilotWalPrefixDigest` is computed immediately before
`PANEL_COMPLETE`; `decidedPilotWalPrefixDigest`, decision event, and failure
receipt do not exist. After
`PANEL_COMPLETE`, define the final WAL and terminal identities without a
digest cycle:

```text
pilotWalDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-pilot-wal/1",
  pilotAttemptId,
  finalEventCount,
  orderedFinalPilotWalEventDigests
]))))

pilotTerminalReceiptDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-pilot-terminal/1",
  pilotAttemptId,
  pilotPanelDigest,
  pilotPanelStatus,
  pilotWalDigest,
  numericEncodingSchemaDigest
]))))
```

Version-2 `qualificationExecutionId` replaces §9.4's version 1 and binds
`pilotTerminalReceiptDigest` plus the numeric schema:

```text
qualificationExecutionId = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-qualification-execution/2",
  qualificationRunId,
  batchActivationReceiptDigest,
  pilotAttemptId,
  pilotPanelDigest,
  pilotTerminalReceiptDigest,
  selectedCommonN,
  numericEncodingSchemaDigest
]))))
```

Only a valid pilot terminal can instantiate that ID. Pilot crash recovery is
still terminal INVALID/BLOCKED and never reopens T8.

### 10.3 Canonical maximal equal-work grouping and chunking

For each candidate and `qualificationKind`, every peer/anchor cell belongs to
exactly one workload equivalence class keyed by the canonical tuple:

```text
[qualificationKind,
 publicOperationId,
 candidateSubjectId,
 candidateBundleDigest,
 pairwiseDenominatorDigest,
 commonCostBoundaryDigest,
 orderedInputSourceDigests,
 orderedPairwiseStrataIncludingEmptyMandatoryStrata,
 sortedAffectedJoinIds,
 commonSemanticProjectionVersion,
 commonSemanticProjectionDigest]
```

Its exact bytes define:

```text
workloadEquivalenceClassDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-workload-equivalence-class/1",
  workloadEquivalenceClassTuple
]))))
```

Within that class, one peer cell has total-order bytes
`UTF8(JSON.stringify([baselineSubjectId,baselineBundleDigest,
pairwiseDenominatorDigest,peerCellManifestDigest]))`. Sort all cells by unsigned lexicographic comparison
of those bytes. Equal order bytes or a repeated peer cell are structural RED.
`baselineSubjectId` is also unique within the equivalence class. Two baseline
artifacts that would otherwise share an ID must receive distinct registry-
versioned subject IDs before T0; different bundle digests do not excuse an ID
collision because `orderedSubjectArtifacts` and Williams scheduling require
unique IDs.
No role, expected speed, author order, registry insertion order, or prior
result participates.

Let `q` be the number of ordered baselines. `q≥1`. Partition mechanically into
the consecutive slices `orderedPeerCells[15*g .. min(15*(g+1),q))` for
zero-based `g=0..ceil(q/15)-1`. The candidate is inserted exactly once at the
group level, then the candidate plus that group's baselines are sorted by
subject ID's raw UTF-8 bytes to form the existing `orderedSubjectArtifacts`.
Thus every resulting
`m = 1 + groupBaselineCount` satisfies `2≤m≤16`. A class of at most fifteen
baselines has exactly one maximal group. Above fifteen, only those consecutive
maximal 15-baseline chunks plus the final remainder exist; optional split,
merge, rebalance, or singleton execution is structural RED.

For each group:

```text
canonicalGroupDigest[g] = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-canonical-peer-group/1",
  workloadEquivalenceClassDigest,
  g,
  orderedPeerCellsInGroup
]))))

canonicalGroupPartitionDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-canonical-group-partition/1",
  workloadEquivalenceClassDigest,
  q,
  16,
  15,
  orderedAllPeerCells,
  orderedArraysOfGroupIndexDigestAndCells
]))))
```

`orderedArraysOfGroupIndexDigestAndCells` has the exact row shape
`[g,canonicalGroupDigest[g],orderedPeerCellsInGroup]` in ascending `g`.
Let `groupCount = ceil(q/15)`; it is a safe non-negative integer and the array
has exactly that many rows.

T0 freezes and mechanically checks the full equivalence-class census,
`canonicalGroupPartitionDigest`, every zero-based group index/digest, and proof that
the groups concatenate to the total ordered cell list exactly once. The
version-1 batch-plan tag is superseded. Each canonical group uses:

```text
batchPlanDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-batch-plan/2",
  qualificationKind,
  publicOperationId,
  candidateSubjectId,
  candidateBundleDigest,
  workloadEquivalenceClassDigest,
  canonicalGroupPartitionDigest,
  groupIndex,
  groupCount,
  canonicalGroupDigestForGroupIndex,
  orderedPeerCellsInGroup,
  pairwiseDenominatorDigest,
  commonCostBoundaryDigest,
  orderedSubjectArtifacts,
  m,
  cycleLength,
  blockCount,
  numericEncodingSchemaDigest
]))))
```

Every plan, qualification, run, schedule, activation, pilot, T9 execution,
history, and close cell binds the full grouping digest plus its group index and
digest. Group membership is decided before any pilot or performance
observation and cannot vary between environments. A peer cell appears in one
and only one canonical group for that candidate/kind/workload class.

### 10.4 Durable one-shot T9 execution WAL

After a valid T8 terminal creates `qualificationExecutionId`, T9 owns one
append-only execution and no redraw. Define:

```text
t9LaunchReceiptDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-t9-launch/1",
  qualificationExecutionId,
  qualificationRunId,
  batchActivationReceiptDigest,
  pilotTerminalReceiptDigest,
  batchScheduleDigest,
  batchPlanDigest,
  numericEncodingSchemaDigest,
  "LAUNCHED"
]))))
```

Before any T9 semantic precheck, warmup, process launch, clock read, or sample,
atomically create-if-absent one durable state record keyed by
`qualificationExecutionId` containing that launch receipt. Only the successful
creator executes. An existing `LAUNCHED`, `T9_VALID`, or `T9_INVALID` record
can never launch, redraw, resample, or restart T9.

T9 uses its own hash-chained WAL. Event indices are zero-based; event zero is
the atomic launch; every event includes the numeric schema:

```text
t9WalEventDigest[i] = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-t9-wal-event/1",
  qualificationExecutionId,
  i,
  i == 0 ? "GENESIS" : t9WalEventDigest[i-1],
  numericEncodingSchemaDigest,
  eventType,
  eventPayload
]))))
```

The closed event/payload schemas are:

```text
T9_LAUNCH                 [t9LaunchReceiptDigest]
BLOCK_INTENT              [b,a,scheduledSubjectIds]
SUBJECT_INTENT            [b,a,p,subjectId]
SUBJECT_PRECHECK_COMPLETE [b,a,p,semanticPreDigest,warmupReceiptDigest,
                           subjectProcessReceiptDigest]
SAMPLE_INTENT             [b,a,p,j]
SAMPLE_OK                 [b,a,p,j,durationF64beScalar]
SAMPLE_INVALID            [b,a,p,j,t9InvalidityCode]
SUBJECT_COMPLETE          [b,a,p,subjectReceiptDigest]
BLOCK_INVALID_DETECTED    [b,a,pOrNull,jOrNull,t9InvalidityCode,
                           failedBlockReceiptDigest]
BLOCK_REPLACEMENT_INTENT  [b,1,failedBlockReceiptDigest]
BLOCK_COMPLETE            [b,a,blockReceiptDigest,blockStatus]
STATISTICS_COMPLETE       [statisticsArtifactDigest]
T9_FAILURE_DECISION       [t9InvalidityCode,bOrNull,aOrNull,pOrNull,jOrNull,
                           decidedT9WalPrefixDigest]
T9_FAILURE                [t9FailureReceiptDigest]
T9_TERMINAL               [t9TerminalStatus,t9TerminalReceiptDigest]
```

`b` is the zero-based logical block index in the complete T6 schedule;
`a=0` is its primary execution and `a=1` its sole possible replacement; `p`
is the zero-based subject position in that block's exact scheduled row; and
`j` is the zero-based timed-sample index, `0≤j<15`. Intents and completions
must advance exactly through the frozen schedule. Every intent is durable
before its action; every sample scalar/completion and receipt is durable
before the next intent. Any mismatch is
`HARNESS_SCHEMA_DIGEST_MISMATCH` and terminal, never an alternate prefix.

`t9InvalidityCode` is exactly one of the following; simultaneous durable
predicates use the first matching code:

```text
PROCESS_SIGNAL
PROCESS_NONZERO_EXIT
HARNESS_SCHEMA_DIGEST_MISMATCH
SEMANTIC_PRE_POST_DIGEST_MISMATCH
ENVIRONMENT_CLASS_CHANGE
FROZEN_THRESHOLD_BREACH
TIMER_OR_DURATION_NONFINITE
DURATION_NONPOSITIVE
MEASUREMENT_NONFINITE
CONTROLLER_CRASH_OR_INCOMPLETE
```

The first nine are predeclared live subject/block invalidities when detected
inside a running block. `MEASUREMENT_NONFINITE` detected only during post-block
aggregate/bootstrap derivation is terminal and not replacement-eligible.
`CONTROLLER_CRASH_OR_INCOMPLETE` is last, applies only when no earlier durable
predicate is proven, and is never replacement-eligible.

The existing whole-block replacement is retained with this exact scope:

1. Only the still-live original executor may use it, and only after a
   predeclared subject/block invalidity is detected and durably recorded in
   `BLOCK_INVALID_DETECTED` and the complete failed-block receipt.
   `SAMPLE_INVALID` is required if and only if a `SAMPLE_INTENT` exists for the
   failure; setup, precheck, warmup, process, or semantic-postcheck failure with
   no sample intent uses the exact subject position `p` and JSON `null` for
   `j`; a block-level failure outside a subject uses JSON `null` for both. No
   such failure invents a sample event.
2. At most one replacement occurs in the entire T9 execution. Before any
   replacement work, append `BLOCK_REPLACEMENT_INTENT`; then execute the whole
   same scheduled block with `a=1`, every subject, precheck, warmup, and sample.
   No primary observation is reused.
3. An invalid replacement, a second invalid primary block, or an invalidity
   after the one replacement is terminal `T9_INVALID`.
4. Controller/executor crash, abandonment, or an incomplete durable prefix is
   never a subject-block invalidity and never earns a replacement.

Define the content-addressed current WAL prefix whenever a block, failure, or
statistics receipt is sealed:

```text
t9WalPrefixDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-t9-wal-prefix/1",
  qualificationExecutionId,
  eventCount,
  orderedT9WalEventDigests
]))))
```

The receipt formulas use the WAL prefix immediately before the event that
publishes the receipt:

```text
t9SubjectReceiptDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-t9-subject/1",
  qualificationExecutionId,
  b,a,p,subjectId,
  selectedCommonN,
  semanticPreDigestOrEmptyString,
  warmupReceiptDigestOrEmptyString,
  subjectProcessReceiptDigestOrEmptyString,
  orderedSampleEventDigests,
  semanticPostDigestOrEmptyString,
  processExitReceiptDigestOrEmptyString,
  subjectStatus,
  t9InvalidityCodeOrEmptyString,
  subjectWalPrefixDigest,
  numericEncodingSchemaDigest
]))))

t9BlockReceiptDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-t9-block/1",
  qualificationExecutionId,
  b,a,
  scheduledSubjectIds,
  orderedSubjectReceiptDigests,
  orderedRawSampleEventDigests,
  blockStatus,
  t9InvalidityCodeOrEmptyString,
  blockWalPrefixDigest,
  numericEncodingSchemaDigest
]))))

t9ReplacementReceiptDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-t9-replacement/1",
  qualificationExecutionId,
  b,
  failedPrimaryBlockReceiptDigest,
  replacementBlockReceiptDigest,
  numericEncodingSchemaDigest
]))))

t9FailureReceiptDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-t9-failure/1",
  qualificationExecutionId,
  t9InvalidityCode,
  failingBlockIndexOrNull,
  failingAttemptIndexOrNull,
  failingSubjectPositionOrNull,
  failingSampleIndexOrNull,
  orderedControllingBlockReceiptDigests,
  partialBlockReceiptDigestOrEmptyString,
  decidedT9WalPrefixDigest,
  terminalizedT9WalPrefixDigest,
  t9FailureDecisionEventDigest,
  corruptT9WalSuffixDigestOrEmptyString,
  numericEncodingSchemaDigest
]))))
```

`subjectStatus` and `blockStatus` are each exactly `VALID` or `INVALID`.
Each subject receipt `OrEmptyString` field is nonempty if and only if its
corresponding completion evidence exists in the WAL; a valid subject requires
all required fields nonempty, while deterministic invalid synthesis copies the
exact completed prefix and uses empty strings only for uncompleted phases.
Failure indices are zero-based and derive from the earliest unmatched or
invalid durable intent: sample failure uses `b,a,p,j`; subject failure before a
sample uses `b,a,p,null`; block failure outside a subject uses `b,a,null,null`;
post-block/statistical failure uses `null,null,null,null`. A controller crash
uses the same locus when an intent is unmatched and all-null only between
blocks. `partialBlockReceiptDigestOrEmptyString` is mandatory whenever a block
intent is currently open without `BLOCK_COMPLETE`, is synthesized solely from
its durable prefix with status `INVALID`, and is empty if and only if no block
is open. This is a total mapping;
there is no “available” or implementation-choice branch.
`corruptT9WalSuffixDigestOrEmptyString` is
`hex(SHA256(exactRawCorruptSuffixBytes))` only for a malformed/gapped WAL and
is empty otherwise.

Live terminal failure and crash recovery validate the longest hash-correct WAL
prefix, record it as `decidedT9WalPrefixDigest`, derive the first invalidity
code and exact locus above, and atomically
compare-and-append one `T9_FAILURE_DECISION`. That committed event controls all
later failure/block/terminal synthesis. A competing or later finalizer follows
it and appends only the uniquely missing deterministic suffix; it never
re-decides or executes T9 work. A malformed/gapped suffix selects
`HARNESS_SCHEMA_DIGEST_MISMATCH`, is content-addressed beside the failure
receipt, and cannot erase the longest valid prefix. After any mechanically
required terminalization events are appended, compute
`terminalizedT9WalPrefixDigest` immediately before `T9_FAILURE`; those are the
two and only two prefix identities in the failure envelope.

After the committed decision, terminalization is exact and idempotent:

1. Append `SAMPLE_INVALID[b,a,p,j,code]` if and only if a
   `SAMPLE_INTENT[b,a,p,j]` is the outstanding intent. A completed sample, or a
   failure before/between samples, creates no sample event.
2. If and only if `SUBJECT_INTENT[b,a,p,subjectId]` is open without
   `SUBJECT_COMPLETE`, synthesize one invalid `t9SubjectReceiptDigest` from its
   complete durable sample-event prefix, with semantic-post/exit fields empty
   exactly when their completion evidence does not exist, append
   `SUBJECT_COMPLETE`, and use that exact subject position. No open subject
   means no synthesized subject receipt and `p/j` follow the failure-locus law.
3. If and only if `BLOCK_INTENT[b,a,...]` is open without `BLOCK_COMPLETE`,
   synthesize one `t9BlockReceiptDigest` with status `INVALID` from the exact
   scheduled row, every durable/synthesized subject receipt, every raw sample
   event, the committed code/locus, and its prefix; append
   `BLOCK_INVALID_DETECTED` then `BLOCK_COMPLETE`. This digest is
   `partialBlockReceiptDigest`. No open block means the field is the empty
   string and `b/a/p/j` are all null unless a post-block failure supplies a
   completed block locus.
4. Append the failure receipt, `T9_FAILURE`, terminal receipt, and exclusive
   `T9_TERMINAL`. A crash during terminalization can only resume this derived
   suffix; it never makes replacement eligible and never executes work.

Every subject receipt binds its exact event/sample prefix, scalar strings,
semantic pre/post digests, process/exit receipt, `N`, and numeric schema. Every
failed or valid block receipt binds `b`, `a`, scheduled row, every subject
receipt, raw sample-event digests, invalidity/status, and the corresponding WAL
prefix. Failed primary/replacement bytes remain permanently present.

#### Exclusive T9 terminal and crash law

The live executor may finalize `T9_VALID` only after every logical block has
one controlling valid block receipt (primary or the one permitted replacement),
the durable rows concatenate to the exact complete schedule, semantic post-
checks are GREEN, and deterministic BR-6 statistics/bootstrap have produced
one `STATISTICS_COMPLETE` receipt. It otherwise finalizes `T9_INVALID` with an
exact failure receipt.

After controller crash or later proof resumption, **no execution action may
resume**. Mechanical finalization to `T9_VALID` is allowed only when the
already durable prefix contains every scheduled sample completion, subject
semantic postcheck, and block required for a complete valid execution. Missing
purely derived block/statistics receipts may then be synthesized from those
complete bytes without a process, clock, warmup, sample, or observation. If
even one required action/sample/postcheck is absent, or an intent is unmatched,
the sole result is `CONTROLLER_CRASH_OR_INCOMPLETE` and `T9_INVALID`; no sample,
block, or execution restarts.

Recovery authority likewise requires a durable controller/process termination
receipt or proof-program restart with no live owner of the atomic execution
record; no wall-clock timeout or observed result may manufacture abandonment.

Before `T9_TERMINAL`, seal:

```text
t9EvidencePrefixDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-t9-evidence-prefix/1",
  qualificationExecutionId,
  eventCount,
  orderedT9WalEventDigests,
  orderedControllingBlockReceiptDigests,
  t9ReplacementReceiptDigestOrEmptyString,
  statisticsArtifactDigestOrEmptyString
]))))

t9TerminalReceiptDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-t9-terminal/1",
  qualificationExecutionId,
  t9LaunchReceiptDigest,
  t9TerminalStatus,
  t9EvidencePrefixDigest,
  orderedControllingBlockReceiptDigests,
  t9ReplacementReceiptDigestOrEmptyString,
  statisticsArtifactDigestOrEmptyString,
  t9FailureReceiptDigestOrEmptyString,
  numericEncodingSchemaDigest
]))))
```

`t9TerminalStatus` is exactly `T9_VALID` or `T9_INVALID`.
`t9ReplacementReceiptDigestOrEmptyString` is nonempty if and only if both the
failed primary and its one replacement block have complete receipts; an open
replacement is instead bound by the WAL and partial-block failure receipt.
`statisticsArtifactDigestOrEmptyString` is nonempty for
`T9_VALID`; `t9FailureReceiptDigestOrEmptyString` is nonempty for
`T9_INVALID`. The opposite-status fields are empty strings, so the envelope
has one canonical shape.

Append the exclusive `T9_TERMINAL` event, then define without a digest cycle:

```text
t9WalDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-t9-wal/1",
  qualificationExecutionId,
  finalEventCount,
  orderedFinalT9WalEventDigests
]))))

t9ExecutionReceiptDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-t9-execution-receipt/1",
  qualificationExecutionId,
  t9TerminalReceiptDigest,
  t9TerminalStatus,
  t9WalDigest,
  numericEncodingSchemaDigest
]))))
```

The terminal transition is atomic and exclusive. `T9_INVALID` maps every cell
in the batch/environment to existing `INVALID`, hence `WAVE_BLOCKED` or final
evidence-BLOCKED. It never creates a second `qualificationExecutionId` or a
new T8/T9 attempt.

Bootstrap seed version 3 supersedes §9's version 2. It remains independent of
observed sample values and adds the pre-observation T9 launch plus numeric
schema:

```text
bootstrapSeed = SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-bootstrap-seed/3",
  qualificationExecutionId,
  t9LaunchReceiptDigest,
  pilotTerminalReceiptDigest,
  numericEncodingSchemaDigest,
  statisticsSchemaDigest,
  buildSchemaDigest,
  runtimeSchemaDigest,
  runnerSchemaDigest,
  batchPlanDigest,
  pairwiseDenominatorDigest,
  candidateBundleDigest,
  baselineBundleDigest,
  "bootstrap-v3"
])))
```

The bootstrap/statistics artifact binds `t9WalPrefixDigest` for the complete
sample prefix and every binary64 scalar; the T9 terminal binds that artifact.
Every raw sample, block/replacement, bootstrap, aggregate, wave/final cell,
history, supersession, and close receipt binds
`numericEncodingSchemaDigest`, the canonical grouping/class/group identities,
pilot terminal, `t9LaunchReceiptDigest`, applicable T9 event/prefix digests,
`t9TerminalReceiptDigest`, `t9WalDigest`, and `t9ExecutionReceiptDigest`.
Earlier-stage receipts bind every predecessor identity available at their
stage; no later digest backfeeds or creates an identity cycle.

### 10.5 Exact fifth-repair instructions to the ADDENDA-06 writer

The writer must change only `ADDENDA-06.md`, retain **PROPOSED · NOT RATIFIED**
and every non-conflicting §§6–9/BR-1–BR-10 safeguard, and authorize no harness
or measurement. Transpose §§10.1–10.4 normatively:

1. replace the global numeric ambiguity with safe nonnegative JSON integers
   only for schema/control integers, exact `u64be:` strings for observed
   unsigned counters, and exact `f64be:` IEEE-754 binary64 strings for all
   continuous/derived measurement scalars; add the numeric schema digest and
   phase-specific nonfinite invalidity mapping everywhere;
2. replace discretionary pilot recovery with the single hash-chained WAL,

   version-2 terminal receipts, final WAL, and pilot terminal digest;
3. form every complete equal-work class, sort peer-cell identities totally,
   reject duplicate baseline IDs, partition only into consecutive maximal

   full class/grouping/group identities into T0 and batch-plan version 2;
4. add atomically launched, one-shot T9 state and WAL keyed by
   `qualificationExecutionId`, with exact scheduled event indices/prefixes,
   total failure decision/locus/receipt serialization, one live-detected
   whole-block replacement total, exclusive VALID/INVALID terminal, and
   crash-finalization law; and
5. transpose bootstrap seed version 3 and bind numeric, grouping, pilot, and
   T9 launch/event/prefix/terminal/execution identities to every evidence and

Retain without weakening: canonical source/genesis/hypothesis bytes; reviewed
causal supersession; complete independent pairwise denominators and peer-cell
receipts; qualification-kind/environment separation; T0→T9 forward topology;
one-shot T8; complete Williams cycles; paired log-median aggregate; unbiased
10,000-resample bootstrap and type-1 bounds; distinct wave/final states;
semantic-first correctness; fixed/immediate anchors; finite LIVE/deposed/C14
registry; strict RED/BLOCKED non-win law; OC-1A/B; Phase-A/PB0/PB13G
boundaries; modular/readable grammar; no benchmark-only fast path; and separate
E-3 parse-that-pin authority. Repaired bytes require two fresh independent
challenges, root gestalt, and explicit owner decisions. A5 and B5 grant no
acceptance credit.

Fifth-amendment receipt: current A5 and B5 were read in full together with the
governing harden chain. No harness, smoke, pilot, benchmark, measurement, code,
production, dependency, BBNF, parse-that, PB0, PB13P, coordination, or inbox
change was performed or authorized.

## 11. Sixth controlling amendment — total crash loci, acyclic finalization authority, exact peer rows, single block publication, replacement cuts, and unbounded RNG rejection

This section is the controlling repair for the six defects confirmed by the
current A6 and B6 challenges. It supersedes only conflicting pilot/T9 recovery,
peer-row, block-publication, replacement-order, bootstrap-RNG, and terminal
binding language in §§6–10. Every non-conflicting requirement of §§1–10 remains
normative. This amendment authorizes specification hardening only: no harness,
pilot, smoke, benchmark, measurement, dependency, parser, production, PB0,
PB13P, BBNF, coordination, or inbox action.

### 11.1 One acyclic finalization-authority protocol for T8 and T9

Timeout, missing output, partial output, process inspection, result inspection,
or the mere existence of a restart process never transfers finalization
authority. A live owner retains sole authority over its attempt. A recovery
actor may acquire authority only from one of these exact evidence cases:

| `authorityKind` | `recoveryBasis` | required evidence | fields that must be the empty string |
|---|---|---|---|
| `LIVE_OWNER` | `LIVE_OWNER` | the launch-bound owner token, current live-owner proof, and an atomic compare-and-append that still observes that owner | durable-termination, proof-restart, and no-live-owner evidence |
| `RECOVERY` | `DURABLE_TERMINATION` | a durable termination/signal receipt for the launched owner and an atomic compare-and-append that observes no later live owner | proof-restart and no-live-owner evidence |
| `RECOVERY` | `PROOF_RESTART_NO_LIVE_OWNER` | a proof-restart receipt plus independently recorded no-live-owner evidence and an atomic compare-and-append that observes no live owner | durable-termination evidence |

The named proofs are content-addressed receipts, not booleans, timestamps, or
free prose. The launch receipt binds the owner token. The current WAL prefix is
the prefix immediately before the authority event and is immutable once the
atomic append succeeds. Define, separately for the pilot and T9:

```text
finalizationAuthorityEvidenceDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-finalization-authority-evidence/1",
  domain,                         // exactly "T8_PILOT" or "T9_CONTROL"
  attemptIdentity,
  launchReceiptDigest,
  authorityKind,
  recoveryBasis,
  ownerTokenDigestOrEmptyString,
  liveOwnerProofDigestOrEmptyString,
  durableTerminationReceiptDigestOrEmptyString,
  proofRestartReceiptDigestOrEmptyString,
  noLiveOwnerEvidenceDigestOrEmptyString,
  authorityPreEventWalPrefixDigest
])))

pilotFinalizationAuthorityReceiptDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-pilot-finalization-authority-receipt/1",
  pilotAttemptId,
  finalizationAuthorityEvidenceDigest,
  authorityPreEventWalPrefixDigest
])))

t9FinalizationAuthorityReceiptDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-t9-finalization-authority-receipt/1",
  qualificationExecutionId,
  finalizationAuthorityEvidenceDigest,
  authorityPreEventWalPrefixDigest
])))
```

`attemptIdentity` is `pilotAttemptId` for T8 and `qualificationExecutionId`
for T9. `domain` and the corresponding receipt formula must agree. The actor
atomically compare-and-appends exactly one
`FINALIZATION_AUTHORITY [finalizationAuthorityReceiptDigest]` event. That event
must precede any `FAILURE_DECISION`, synthesized invalid completion, valid
panel/statistics finalization, or terminal event performed by that actor. A
failed comparison grants no authority and permits no append. The exact same
authority receipt remains controlling through the resulting terminal suffix;
there is no second acquisition inside that suffix.

The authority evidence and receipt depend only on launch-bound evidence and
the prefix before `FINALIZATION_AUTHORITY`. Failure, panel, statistics,
terminal, final-WAL, and execution receipts are strictly later descendants.
They may bind the authority receipt; the authority receipt must never bind any
of them. This is the mandatory acyclic order:

```text
launch/evidence prefix
  -> finalizationAuthorityEvidenceDigest
  -> FINALIZATION_AUTHORITY
  -> committed-detection completion, failure decision, or valid-finalization work
  -> terminal
  -> final WAL
  -> execution receipt
```

Pilot failure, pilot panel, pilot terminal, T9 failure, T9 terminal, and T9
execution receipts must each bind their applicable finalization-authority
receipt as an explicit field. The exact versioned pilot failure/terminal
formulas in §11.2 and T9 failure/terminal/execution formulas in §11.4 are
controlling; they replace the earlier formulas and transitively bind prior
evidence through their named WAL/evidence prefixes. They do not inherit a
blanket requirement to retain every earlier field or field order. Only the
pilot panel and qualification-execution formulas explicitly described in
§11.2 use retain-and-insert construction. A successful live-owner terminal
therefore has a `LIVE_OWNER` authority receipt; a recovered or failure
terminal has whichever exact row above authorized it. No terminal receipt is
valid with an empty authority field.

### 11.2 Exhaustive T8 crash phase, locus, synthesis, and authority table

Pilot recovery derives exactly one row from the longest valid WAL prefix. The
reported locus is always `[sOrNull,kOrNull,oOrNull]`. `subjectIdOrEmptyString`
is nonempty if and only if `sOrNull` is non-null, and then is exactly the
subject identity scheduled at zero-based index `s`. `k` and `o` are likewise
zero-based scheduled indices. A completed subordinate index remains the locus
through its immediately following between-items phase, but only an unmatched
intent is synthesized.

| exact crash phase from the longest valid prefix | locus | open intent(s) eligible for deterministic invalid synthesis | subject ID |
|---|---|---|---|
| after `PILOT_LAUNCHED`, before first `SUBJECT_INTENT` | `[null,null,null]` | none | empty |
| after `SUBJECT_INTENT(s)`, before first `ROUND_INTENT` | `[s,null,null]` | subject | scheduled subject `s` |
| after `ROUND_INTENT(s,k)`, before first `OBS_INTENT` | `[s,k,null]` | round, then subject | scheduled subject `s` |
| after `OBS_INTENT(s,k,o)`, before its terminal observation event | `[s,k,o]` | observation, then round, then subject | scheduled subject `s` |
| after a terminal observation `o`, before `OBS_INTENT(o+1)` or `ROUND_COMPLETE` | `[s,k,o]` | round, then subject; never re-synthesize observation `o` | scheduled subject `s` |
| after a `ROUND_COMPLETE` whose status requires another round, before `ROUND_INTENT(k+1)` | `[s,k,null]` | subject only; never re-synthesize round `k` | scheduled subject `s` |
| after the subject's final `ROUND_COMPLETE`, before `SUBJECT_COMPLETE` | `[s,k,null]` | subject only; never re-synthesize round `k` | scheduled subject `s` |
| after `SUBJECT_COMPLETE(s)`, before `SUBJECT_INTENT(s+1)` | `[null,null,null]` | none | empty |
| after the last `SUBJECT_COMPLETE`, before `PILOT_DERIVATION_INTENT` | `[null,null,null]` | none | empty |
| after `PILOT_DERIVATION_INTENT`, before `PILOT_PANEL_COMPLETE` | `[null,null,null]` | the derivation-invalid completion only; no subject/round/observation synthesis | empty |
| after a complete panel, before the exclusive pilot terminal | `[null,null,null]` | none | empty |

The between-subject row includes every completed subject except the final one;
the post-last-subject row names the final one explicitly so neither can inherit
a stale subject locus. A derivation failure uses existing
`PILOT_DERIVATION_INVALID` precedence and the all-null locus. A crash without
an unmatched intent never fabricates a subject, round, observation, or sample.

For every crash row, the controlling actor first acquires and appends the
§11.1 pilot authority receipt. It then appends one committed
`FAILURE_DECISION` containing the exact phase, locus, subject ID rule, failure
code, simultaneous-invalidity precedence winner, authority receipt, and the
pre-decision prefix. It synthesizes only the unmatched intents named in the
table, in child-to-parent order, and emits the one invalid panel/terminal
suffix. A live owner follows the same serialization. Re-entry observes the
existing authority/failure decision and may append only its missing suffix;
it may not make a second decision or acquire a different authority.

Pilot receipt formulas are versioned and bound without cycles as follows:

```text
pilotFailureReceiptDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-pilot-failure-receipt/3",
  pilotAttemptId,
  pilotFinalizationAuthorityReceiptDigest,
  pilotFailureCode,
  pilotFailurePhase,
  [sOrNull, kOrNull, oOrNull],
  subjectIdOrEmptyString,
  failureDecisionEventDigest,
  pilotFailureEvidencePrefixDigest,
  orderedSynthesizedInvalidEventDigests
])))

pilotTerminalReceiptDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-pilot-terminal-receipt/3",
  pilotAttemptId,
  pilotFinalizationAuthorityReceiptDigest,
  pilotTerminalStatus,
  pilotPanelReceiptDigest,
  pilotFailureReceiptDigestOrEmptyString,
  pilotEvidencePrefixDigest,
  numericEncodingSchemaDigest
])))
```

The version-3 pilot panel receipt retains every version-2 panel field in order
and inserts `pilotFinalizationAuthorityReceiptDigest` immediately after
`pilotAttemptId`. The version-3 qualification execution identity retains every
version-2 field in order, replaces the terminal with the version-3
`pilotTerminalReceiptDigest`, inserts that terminal's authority receipt
immediately after `pilotAttemptId`, and uses tag
`v-pi-qualification-execution-id/3`. Thus the authority is explicit and also
transitively bound, while no later receipt feeds the authority digest.

### 11.3 Exact canonical peer-cell row and grouping bytes

There is one allowed peer-cell row shape:

```text
canonicalPeerCellRow = [
  baselineSubjectId,
  baselineBundleDigest,
  pairwiseDenominatorDigest,
  peerCellManifestDigest
]

canonicalPeerCellRowBytes = UTF8(JSON.stringify(canonicalPeerCellRow))

canonicalPeerCellRowSchemaDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-canonical-peer-cell-row-schema/1",
  [
    "baselineSubjectId",
    "baselineBundleDigest",
    "pairwiseDenominatorDigest",
    "peerCellManifestDigest"
  ]
])))
```

All four entries are required strings in that order. No object form, key,
label, candidate identity, qualification kind, environment identity, index,
annotation, or other fifth field is permitted. Omitting or duplicating a field,
using a non-string field, or attaching metadata is structural RED.

`orderedAllPeerCells` contains only these exact four-element arrays and is
sorted by unsigned lexicographic comparison of
`canonicalPeerCellRowBytes`. The sort-key bytes are therefore the row bytes;
there is no distinct projection. Every complete equal-work-class slice and
every maximal 15-baseline group contains only consecutive rows copied byte for
byte from `orderedAllPeerCells`. Grouping/class/plan receipts hash these exact
arrays, `canonicalPeerCellRowSchemaDigest`, and the existing class/group
boundary identities. Duplicate `baselineSubjectId` remains structural RED even
if another field differs.

### 11.4 T9 single block publisher, exact replacement state machine, and every crash cut

`BLOCK_COMPLETE` is the sole publisher of `t9BlockReceiptDigest`. Supersede the
conflicting event payloads with these exact shapes:

```text
BLOCK_INVALID_DETECTED [
  b, attemptOrdinal, pOrNull, jOrNull,
  t9FailureCode,
  preDetectionEvidencePrefixDigest
]

BLOCK_COMPLETE [
  b, attemptOrdinal,
  t9BlockReceiptDigest,
  blockStatus
]
```

`attemptOrdinal` is `0` for the primary and `1` for the one replacement.
`blockStatus` is exactly `VALID` or `INVALID`. A detection event contains no
block receipt, receipt placeholder, completion prefix, or terminal digest. For
an invalid block, append `BLOCK_INVALID_DETECTED`, set
`invalidBlockEvidenceAnchorDigest` to that committed detection event's digest,
compute the block receipt from that fixed anchor, then append
`BLOCK_COMPLETE ... INVALID`. The live uninterrupted path performs detection
then completion with no intervening event. A valid block has no detection
event and is published once by `BLOCK_COMPLETE ... VALID`.

The version-2 block receipt is exact:

```text
t9BlockReceiptDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-t9-block/2",
  qualificationExecutionId,
  b, attemptOrdinal,
  scheduledSubjectIds,
  orderedSubjectReceiptDigests,
  orderedRawSampleEventDigests,
  blockStatus,
  t9InvalidityCodeOrEmptyString,
  validBlockEvidencePrefixDigestOrEmptyString,
  invalidBlockEvidenceAnchorDigestOrEmptyString,
  numericEncodingSchemaDigest
])))
```

For `VALID`, `validBlockEvidencePrefixDigestOrEmptyString` is the WAL prefix
immediately before `BLOCK_COMPLETE` and
`invalidBlockEvidenceAnchorDigestOrEmptyString` is empty. For `INVALID`, the
valid-prefix field is empty and `invalidBlockEvidenceAnchorDigestOrEmptyString`
is exactly the unique committed `BLOCK_INVALID_DETECTED` event digest. It is
not the prefix before completion. Any other empty/nonempty combination is
structural RED.

If a crash occurs after `BLOCK_INVALID_DETECTED` but before `BLOCK_COMPLETE`,
the durable detection payload is the committed invalid decision. Recovery
must first acquire and append §11.1 finalization authority, then append the one
missing `BLOCK_COMPLETE ... INVALID` using the same committed detection-event
anchor, and then terminalize according to the state table below. The authority
event changes the WAL prefix but cannot change or recompute the block receipt,
because the receipt binds the fixed detection anchor rather than the
pre-completion prefix. Recovery must not append a second detection or change
its code/locus. If the completion already exists, recovery does not append
another. This gives the receipt exactly one publisher and one invalid anchor.

The sole allowed first-invalid replacement order is:

```text
primary BLOCK_INVALID_DETECTED
-> primary BLOCK_COMPLETE INVALID
-> BLOCK_REPLACEMENT_INTENT
-> replacement BLOCK_INTENT
-> replacement work
-> replacement BLOCK_COMPLETE VALID|INVALID
```

Appending `BLOCK_REPLACEMENT_INTENT` atomically consumes the attempt-global
replacement budget. No earlier event consumes it; no later crash refunds it.
The following cases are exhaustive:

| durable state/cut | required next state and terminal consequence |
|---|---|
| primary `BLOCK_COMPLETE VALID` | primary receipt controls the block; proceed once |
| primary `BLOCK_INVALID_DETECTED` without completion | acquire and append authority, append only its unique primary `BLOCK_COMPLETE INVALID` from the same detection anchor, then terminalize; do not start a replacement |
| primary `BLOCK_COMPLETE INVALID`, before replacement intent | a live uninterrupted owner may append the one replacement intent; a crash at this cut acquires authority and terminalizes with no replacement |
| replacement intent, before replacement block intent | budget is consumed; locus is exactly `[b,1,null,null]`; partial-block digest is empty; replacement-block receipt is empty; append no block/detection/completion synthesis; use authority and append the deterministic failure suffix |
| replacement `BLOCK_INTENT`, before replacement detection/completion | locus is `[b,1,pOrNull,jOrNull]` from the longest valid prefix; partial-block evidence is nonempty exactly when durable replacement work exists; use authority, then append the one deterministic replacement invalid detection/completion and failure suffix |
| replacement `BLOCK_COMPLETE VALID` | replacement receipt controls the block; replacement receipt binds both complete block receipts; proceed once |
| replacement `BLOCK_INVALID_DETECTED` without completion | acquire and append authority, append only its unique replacement `BLOCK_COMPLETE INVALID` from the same detection anchor, then terminalize |
| replacement `BLOCK_COMPLETE INVALID` | use authority and terminalize; never replace again |
| later primary invalid after the global budget was consumed by another block | publish that primary invalid detection/completion, then use authority and terminalize; never append another replacement intent |

A crash before any invalid detection is not permitted to infer a live-detected
replacement opportunity. After authority acquisition and one failure decision,
recovery may synthesize the unique invalid detection/completion for an open
`BLOCK_INTENT`, then terminalizes; it does not append
`BLOCK_REPLACEMENT_INTENT`. A crash after a valid completion proceeds only from
that completion and never replays work. Every event append is compare-and-
append/idempotent against the exact expected predecessor.

`t9ReplacementReceiptDigestOrEmptyString` is nonempty if and only if a
replacement `BLOCK_COMPLETE` exists and both the primary and replacement block
receipts are complete. The replacement-intent-only cut therefore has an empty
replacement receipt even though the budget is consumed. The terminal receipt
binds the replacement-intent event directly through the evidence prefix.

T9 failure and terminal bindings advance versions without a backward edge:

```text
t9FailureReceiptDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-t9-failure-receipt/2",
  qualificationExecutionId,
  t9FinalizationAuthorityReceiptDigest,
  t9FailureCode,
  t9FailurePhase,
  [bOrNull, attemptOrdinalOrNull, pOrNull, jOrNull],
  failureDecisionEventDigest,
  t9FailureEvidencePrefixDigest,
  partialBlockReceiptDigestOrEmptyString,
  orderedSynthesizedInvalidEventDigests
])))

t9TerminalReceiptDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-t9-terminal-receipt/2",
  qualificationExecutionId,
  t9FinalizationAuthorityReceiptDigest,
  t9TerminalStatus,
  t9EvidencePrefixDigest,
  orderedControllingBlockReceiptDigests,
  t9ReplacementReceiptDigestOrEmptyString,
  statisticsArtifactDigestOrEmptyString,
  t9FailureReceiptDigestOrEmptyString,
  numericEncodingSchemaDigest
])))

t9ExecutionReceiptDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-t9-execution-receipt/2",
  qualificationExecutionId,
  t9FinalizationAuthorityReceiptDigest,
  t9TerminalReceiptDigest,
  t9TerminalStatus,
  t9WalDigest,
  numericEncodingSchemaDigest
])))
```

The final T9 WAL is computed only after the exclusive terminal append, so it
remains a descendant of the authority and terminal receipts. A valid T9 owner
acquires `LIVE_OWNER` authority after all controlling block/statistics evidence
and before `T9_TERMINAL`; every invalid recovery path acquires authority before
its failure decision and before any missing `BLOCK_COMPLETE`. The fixed
detection anchor prevents that authority event from changing the invalid block
receipt.

### 11.5 Raw `u32be`, unbounded rejection index, bootstrap seed v4, and RNG v2

For bootstrap selection only:

```text
u32be(x) = the four raw octets
  [(x >> 24) & 255, (x >> 16) & 255, (x >> 8) & 255, x & 255]
```

`x` must be an exact unsigned integer with `0 <= x <= 2^32 - 1`; the result is
exactly four bytes in most-significant-byte-first order. It is not hex, base64,
JSON, UTF-8 decimal, or a `u64be:` string. Bootstrap resample index `r` and draw
index `d` use these raw bytes and are structurally RED if out of range.

The rejection index `j` is an arbitrary-precision nonnegative integer. Its
canonical representation is ASCII base-10 with no sign and no leading zero,
except that zero is exactly the one byte string `"0"`. `j` begins at zero and
increments mathematically without a machine-word bound until a draw is
accepted. Its canonical ASCII bytes are the final field of the RNG preimage,
so no following delimiter is needed and no retry seed exists.

Define and bind the exact RNG schema:

```text
bootstrapRngSchemaDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-bootstrap-rng-schema/2",
  "sha256-rejection-sampling",
  "u32be-r-raw-four-octets",
  "u32be-d-raw-four-octets",
  "j-final-canonical-ascii-decimal-unbounded"
])))

candidateBytes = SHA256(
  UTF8("v-pi-bootstrap-rng/2\u0000")
  || bootstrapSeed
  || u32be(r)
  || u32be(d)
  || ASCII(canonicalDecimal(j))
)
```

The existing unbiased rejection threshold and candidate-to-index mapping apply
to `candidateBytes` unchanged. A rejected candidate increments only `j`; it
does not change the seed, `r`, `d`, or any receipt identity. Consequently the
algorithm remains defined past `2^32 - 1` rejections and cannot wrap.

Bootstrap seed version 4 supersedes version 3:

```text
bootstrapSeed = SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-bootstrap-seed/4",
  qualificationExecutionId,
  t9LaunchReceiptDigest,
  pilotTerminalReceiptDigest,
  numericEncodingSchemaDigest,
  statisticsSchemaDigest,
  bootstrapRngSchemaDigest,
  buildSchemaDigest,
  runtimeSchemaDigest,
  runnerSchemaDigest,
  batchPlanDigest,
  pairwiseDenominatorDigest,
  candidateBundleDigest,
  baselineBundleDigest,
  "bootstrap-v4"
])))
```

Every bootstrap draw trace, aggregate/statistics artifact, T9 terminal, wave,
final, history, supersession, and close receipt binds
`bootstrapRngSchemaDigest` and the version-4 seed identity available at its
stage. The fixed 10,000 resamples and all existing statistical laws remain
unchanged.

### 11.6 Exact sixth-repair instructions to the ADDENDA-06 writer

The writer must change only `ADDENDA-06.md`, retain **PROPOSED · NOT RATIFIED**,
retain §10 except where this amendment explicitly supersedes it, and authorize
no harness, pilot, smoke, benchmark, measurement, dependency, parser,
production, PB0, PB13P, BBNF, coordination, or inbox action. Transpose
§§11.1–11.5 normatively and exactly:

1. add the content-addressed, compare-and-append finalization-authority
   protocol for live owners and the two allowed recovery bases; make timeout
   and result inspection explicitly non-authoritative; bind the authority

2. replace pilot crash recovery with the exhaustive phase/locus/synthesis table,

3. define `canonicalPeerCellRow` as exactly the four-string array and require

4. make `BLOCK_COMPLETE` the sole block-receipt publisher; require detection,

5. define `u32be` as four raw unsigned big-endian bytes, make rejection `j` an
   unbounded canonical ASCII decimal final preimage field, and use bootstrap

6. preserve the acyclic dependency direction: authority receipts bind only

   as later descendants.

Retain without weakening: canonical source/genesis/hypothesis bytes; reviewed
causal supersession; complete independent pairwise denominators and peer-cell
receipts; qualification-kind/environment separation; T0→T9 forward topology;

aggregate; unbiased 10,000-resample bootstrap and type-1 bounds; distinct

LIVE/deposed/C14 registry; strict RED/BLOCKED non-win law; OC-1A/B;
Phase-A/PB0/PB13G boundaries; modular/readable grammar; no benchmark-only fast
path; and separate E-3 parse-that-pin authority. Repaired bytes require two
fresh independent challenges, root gestalt, and explicit owner decisions. A6
and B6 grant no acceptance credit.

Sixth-amendment receipt: current A6 and B6 were read in full together with the
