## 12. Seventh controlling amendment — closed authority union, authority-first derived evidence, total re-entry and phases, and finite unbiased RNG

This section is the narrow controlling repair for A7-01 through A7-04 and
B7-01 through B7-03. It supersedes only conflicting authority-evidence,
authority/derived-artifact order, post-authority continuation, failure-phase,
T9 crash-cut, and bootstrap rejection language in §§6–11. Every other sound
requirement remains normative. This amendment authorizes formation hardening
only: no harness, pilot, smoke, benchmark, measurement, parser, dependency,
production, PB0, PB13P, BBNF, coordination, or inbox action.

### 12.1 Closed authority evidence and one acyclic raw/derived order

The five `OrEmptyString` proof fields in the §11.1 authority evidence envelope
form this exact closed tagged union. “Nonempty” means exactly one lowercase
64-hex digest of the named content-addressed receipt; “empty” means exactly the
zero-length JSON string. No field may contain a boolean, timestamp, prose,
placeholder, another branch's proof, or a repeated proof.

| `authorityKind` | `recoveryBasis` | owner token | live-owner proof | durable-termination receipt | proof-restart receipt | no-live-owner evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `LIVE_OWNER` | `LIVE_OWNER` | nonempty | nonempty | empty | empty | empty |
| `RECOVERY` | `DURABLE_TERMINATION` | empty | empty | nonempty | empty | empty |
| `RECOVERY` | `PROOF_RESTART_NO_LIVE_OWNER` | empty | empty | empty | nonempty | nonempty |

Every other tag pair or empty/nonempty combination is structural RED. The
atomic comparison named by the selected row remains mandatory but is an
operation over those exact bytes, not a sixth optional proof field. In
particular, stale live-owner bytes can never coexist with either recovery
branch.

Before authority, the domain's total longest-prefix function computes one
canonical `finalizationPlanDigest` solely from the authority-pre-event prefix:

```text
finalizationPlanDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-finalization-plan/1",
  domain,
  attemptIdentity,
  authorityPreEventWalPrefixDigest,
  finalizationPlanClass,
  failurePhaseOrEmptyString,
  locusOrAllNull,
  orderedPreDecisionClosureDescriptors,
  derivedDispositionFunctionId
]))))
```

`finalizationPlanClass` is exactly one of
`PILOT_DERIVE_PANEL`, `PILOT_FAIL_FROM_PREFIX`, `T9_DERIVE_STATISTICS`,
`T9_FAIL_FROM_PREFIX`, or `T9_COMPLETE_COMMITTED_INVALID_THEN_FAIL`.
`orderedPreDecisionClosureDescriptors` is empty except for the last class,
where it is exactly one array
`["BLOCK_COMPLETE",b,attemptOrdinal,invalidDetectionEventDigest]`.
`derivedDispositionFunctionId` is exactly
`v-pi-pilot-finalization-function/1` for T8 or
`v-pi-t9-finalization-function/1` for T9. The phase/locus fields use §12.2 and
§11.2/§12.3; derive plans use an empty phase and all-null locus. No actor,
recovery proof, result, panel, statistic, receipt computed after authority,
timestamp, or implementation choice enters this plan.

Authority evidence and receipts advance to version 2. Their version-2 formulas
retain every version-1 field in order and insert `finalizationPlanDigest`
immediately after `authorityPreEventWalPrefixDigest`; their tags become
`v-pi-finalization-authority-evidence/2`,
`v-pi-pilot-finalization-authority-receipt/2`, and
`v-pi-t9-finalization-authority-receipt/2`. The authority event therefore
commits not merely a prefix but the one closure function that prefix admits.
The plan is derived before authority; its statistics/panel result is not.

For both T8 and T9, distinguish **raw controlling evidence** from **derived
finalization evidence**:

- T8 raw evidence is the immutable longest valid pilot WAL through every
  action-bearing observation/terminal observation and any already durable
  round/subject completion. A pilot panel, selected common `N`, flags, failure
  envelope, and terminal are derived evidence.
- T9 raw evidence is the immutable longest valid T9 WAL through every executed
  semantic precheck, warmup/process receipt, sample terminal, semantic
  postcheck, subject receipt, and already published block receipt. A missing
  completion for an already committed invalid-detection event is mechanical
  derived closure. Bootstrap samples, aggregate/statistics artifacts, failure
  envelopes, and terminal receipts are derived evidence.

No process launch, warmup, clock read, sample, semantic observation, replacement
intent, replacement work, or other action-bearing event may occur after
`FINALIZATION_AUTHORITY`. Pure deterministic derivation and idempotent receipt
publication are the only permitted descendants. The one normative success
orders are:

```text
T8 raw observation WAL
  -> FINALIZATION_AUTHORITY
  -> deterministic pilot panel derivation
  -> PILOT_FINALIZATION_DECISION VALID
  -> PILOT_PANEL_COMPLETE
  -> pilot terminal

T9 complete raw block/sample WAL
  -> FINALIZATION_AUTHORITY
  -> deterministic bootstrap/statistics derivation
  -> T9_FINALIZATION_DECISION VALID
  -> STATISTICS_COMPLETE
  -> T9 terminal
```

The panel and statistics artifact formulas are advanced one version. Each
retains its immediately prior exact fields in order and inserts its applicable
`pilotFinalizationAuthorityReceiptDigest` or
`t9FinalizationAuthorityReceiptDigest` immediately after its attempt identity.
The statistics artifact also binds `bootstrapRngSchemaDigest` and the exact
bootstrap seed version in §12.4. Therefore raw evidence precedes authority;
the authority binds no panel/statistics digest; and every derived
panel/statistics artifact binds the already committed authority. The
`PILOT_PANEL_COMPLETE`/`STATISTICS_COMPLETE` event publishes that derived
artifact only after the corresponding finalization decision. The opposite
order, including the §11.4 sentence that placed T9 authority after statistics,
is retracted.

Failure uses the same forward direction:

```text
raw or longest-valid evidence prefix
  -> FINALIZATION_AUTHORITY
  -> any mechanically required completion whose bytes were fixed by that prefix
  -> deterministic derivation or invalidity classification
  -> domain FINALIZATION_DECISION INVALID
  -> remaining uniquely synthesized failure suffix
  -> terminal
```

The pre-decision completion allowance is narrow: it is only a missing
`BLOCK_COMPLETE` whose valid subject/sample prefix or committed invalid-
detection anchor already fixes its unique bytes. It can execute no work and
cannot create a replacement opportunity. Every other synthesized invalid
observation/subject/block or failure event follows the INVALID decision.

The two domain decision events are exact:

```text
PILOT_FINALIZATION_DECISION [pilotFinalizationDecisionReceiptDigest]
T9_FINALIZATION_DECISION    [t9FinalizationDecisionReceiptDigest]

pilotFinalizationDecisionReceiptDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-pilot-finalization-decision/1",
  pilotAttemptId,
  pilotFinalizationAuthorityReceiptDigest,
  finalizationPlanDigest,
  authorityPreEventWalPrefixDigest,
  pilotFinalizationDisposition,
  pilotPanelReceiptDigestOrEmptyString,
  pilotFailureCodeOrEmptyString,
  pilotFailurePhaseOrEmptyString,
  [sOrNull,kOrNull,oOrNull]
]))))

t9FinalizationDecisionReceiptDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-t9-finalization-decision/1",
  qualificationExecutionId,
  t9FinalizationAuthorityReceiptDigest,
  finalizationPlanDigest,
  authorityPreEventWalPrefixDigest,
  t9FinalizationDisposition,
  statisticsArtifactDigestOrEmptyString,
  t9FailureCodeOrEmptyString,
  t9FailurePhaseOrEmptyString,
  [bOrNull,attemptOrdinalOrNull,pOrNull,jOrNull]
]))))
```

Each disposition is exactly `VALID` or `INVALID`. `VALID` requires the derived
artifact digest nonempty, every failure field empty, and an all-null locus.
`INVALID` requires the derived artifact field empty, one closed failure code,
one phase token from §12.2, and that phase's exact locus from §11.2 or §12.3.
Any other combination is structural RED. These events replace the older
failure-only decision publisher; every earlier
`failureDecisionEventDigest` field now contains the applicable domain
`FINALIZATION_DECISION` event digest. The panel/statistics artifact is computed
before the decision but published after it, so the decision may bind the
artifact without a digest cycle.

An authority append irrevocably enters **deterministic closure**. If the actor
crashes after authority but before a decision, re-entry validates the existing
authority receipt, reads its bound `finalizationPlanDigest` and
`authorityPreEventWalPrefixDigest`, executes only that plan's pure derivation,
and compare-and-appends the one byte-identical decision. It neither reacquires
nor reclassifies authority, adds continuation proof fields, changes authority
kind/basis, or inspects a new result. Concurrent closers can propose only those
same decision bytes; one compare-and-append wins and the others follow it. If a
decision already exists, any re-entering actor appends only the next
byte-identical missing derived suffix event. A crash at any later derived
suffix cut repeats this rule. Thus neither T8 nor T9 has a stuck post-authority
prefix, and no recovery evidence or actor identity can alter a decided
terminal.

### 12.2 Closed failure-phase token vocabularies

`pilotFailurePhase` is exactly one of these eleven ASCII tokens, mapped in
order to the eleven §11.2 crash-table rows:

```text
PILOT_POST_LAUNCH
PILOT_SUBJECT_OPEN_PRE_ROUND
PILOT_ROUND_OPEN_PRE_OBSERVATION
PILOT_OBSERVATION_OPEN
PILOT_BETWEEN_OBSERVATIONS
PILOT_BETWEEN_ROUNDS
PILOT_POST_FINAL_ROUND
PILOT_BETWEEN_SUBJECTS
PILOT_POST_LAST_SUBJECT
PILOT_DERIVATION_OPEN
PILOT_POST_PANEL
```

For `PILOT_BETWEEN_OBSERVATIONS`, the row includes both the cut before the next
observation and the cut before `ROUND_COMPLETE`; for `PILOT_BETWEEN_ROUNDS`,
the completed round requires another round. The exact locus and synthesis are
those in the corresponding §11.2 row. If authority is already present but no
decision exists, the token is still derived from the authority's pre-event
prefix; `FINALIZATION_AUTHORITY` does not create another failure phase.

`t9FailurePhase` is exactly one of these twenty-one ASCII tokens:

```text
T9_POST_LAUNCH
T9_BLOCK_OPEN_PRE_SUBJECT
T9_SUBJECT_OPEN_PRE_PRECHECK
T9_SUBJECT_READY_PRE_SAMPLE
T9_SAMPLE_OPEN
T9_BETWEEN_SAMPLES
T9_POST_FINAL_SAMPLE
T9_SAMPLE_INVALID_RECORDED
T9_BETWEEN_SUBJECTS
T9_SUBJECT_INVALID_RECORDED
T9_POST_FINAL_SUBJECT
T9_INVALID_DETECTED_PRE_COMPLETE
T9_BETWEEN_VALID_BLOCKS
T9_PRIMARY_INVALID_PRE_REPLACEMENT
T9_REPLACEMENT_INTENT_OPEN
T9_POST_REPLACEMENT_VALID
T9_POST_REPLACEMENT_INVALID
T9_POST_FINAL_BLOCK
T9_STATISTICS_DERIVATION
T9_POST_DECISION
T9_TERMINAL_SUFFIX
```

No case-folding, prose label, abbreviation, alternate separator, or unknown
token is permitted. `T9_POST_DECISION` and `T9_TERMINAL_SUFFIX` never replace
the origin token inside an already committed invalid decision: they classify
only the current re-entry cut, while the failure receipt retains the decision's
original token and locus. A valid decision/terminal has no
`t9FailurePhase`; its corresponding `OrEmptyString` field is exactly empty.

### 12.3 Total T9 longest-prefix, locus, decision, and synthesis function

The following table is exhaustive over every hash-correct T9 WAL prefix. A
prefix first matches the most specific row whose left column describes its
last event and unmatched intent. `lastP` and `lastJ` are the final zero-based
subject/sample positions in the frozen row; completed subordinate indices are
retained through their immediately following between-items cut. “Close block
invalid” means: append no action-bearing work, append a sample invalid event
only for an unmatched sample intent, synthesize an open subject receipt if and
only if its subject intent is unmatched, append one
`BLOCK_INVALID_DETECTED` if none exists, and publish the sole invalid
`BLOCK_COMPLETE` from its fixed detection anchor. Child closure precedes
parent closure. No row starts or resumes a replacement.

There is one pre-authority normalization. If an open block has every scheduled
`SUBJECT_COMPLETE` receipt, all are `VALID`, every required raw sample and
semantic postcheck is durable, and no invalid-detection event exists, any actor
must compare-and-append its sole `BLOCK_COMPLETE ... VALID` before attempting
authority. The valid block receipt binds the prefix immediately before that
completion exactly as §11.4 requires. A compare failure follows the already
committed identical completion. This action-free normalization is not
finalization authority, cannot start work or replacement, and ensures a fully
evidenced open valid block has one identity rather than a choice between VALID
and crash-invalid. No other open block may be normalized valid.

| longest valid WAL prefix / exact cut | phase token | exact locus | deterministic authority/decision/synthesis consequence |
|---|---|---|---|
| `T9_LAUNCH`, before first primary `BLOCK_INTENT` | `T9_POST_LAUNCH` | `[null,null,null,null]` | acquire authority; decide INVALID `CONTROLLER_CRASH_OR_INCOMPLETE`; synthesize no block event |
| primary or replacement `BLOCK_INTENT(b,a)`, before `SUBJECT_INTENT` | `T9_BLOCK_OPEN_PRE_SUBJECT` | `[b,a,null,null]` | acquire authority; decide INVALID; close block invalid only |
| `SUBJECT_INTENT(b,a,p)`, before `SUBJECT_PRECHECK_COMPLETE` | `T9_SUBJECT_OPEN_PRE_PRECHECK` | `[b,a,p,null]` | acquire authority; decide INVALID; synthesize subject then close block invalid |
| `SUBJECT_PRECHECK_COMPLETE(b,a,p)`, before first `SAMPLE_INTENT` | `T9_SUBJECT_READY_PRE_SAMPLE` | `[b,a,p,null]` | acquire authority; decide INVALID; synthesize subject then close block invalid |
| unmatched `SAMPLE_INTENT(b,a,p,j)` | `T9_SAMPLE_OPEN` | `[b,a,p,j]` | acquire authority; decide INVALID; append its one `SAMPLE_INVALID`, synthesize subject, then close block invalid |
| terminal sample `j<lastJ`, before `SAMPLE_INTENT(j+1)` | `T9_BETWEEN_SAMPLES` | `[b,a,p,j]` | acquire authority; decide INVALID; do not synthesize sample `j`; synthesize subject then close block invalid |
| terminal sample `lastJ`, before `SUBJECT_COMPLETE` | `T9_POST_FINAL_SAMPLE` | `[b,a,p,lastJ]` | acquire authority; decide INVALID; synthesize subject then close block invalid |
| terminal `SAMPLE_INVALID(b,a,p,j)`, before invalid subject/block closure | `T9_SAMPLE_INVALID_RECORDED` | `[b,a,p,j]` | retain its precedence code; acquire authority; decide INVALID; synthesize subject then close block invalid; never append another sample event |
| `SUBJECT_COMPLETE VALID(b,a,p)` with `p<lastP`, before next `SUBJECT_INTENT` | `T9_BETWEEN_SUBJECTS` | `[b,a,p,null]` | acquire authority; decide INVALID; never resynthesize subject `p`; close block invalid |
| `SUBJECT_COMPLETE INVALID(b,a,p)`, before block detection/completion | `T9_SUBJECT_INVALID_RECORDED` | `[b,a,p,null]` | retain its precedence code; acquire authority; decide INVALID; append only block detection/completion and failure suffix |
| final `SUBJECT_COMPLETE VALID(b,a,lastP)`, before valid block publication | `T9_POST_FINAL_SUBJECT` | `[b,a,lastP,null]` | first apply the unique pre-authority valid-block normalization; then follow the resulting between-block or post-final-block row |
| `BLOCK_INVALID_DETECTED(b,a,p?,j?)`, before its `BLOCK_COMPLETE` | `T9_INVALID_DETECTED_PRE_COMPLETE` | `[b,a,pOrNull,jOrNull]` from detection | acquire authority with `T9_COMPLETE_COMMITTED_INVALID_THEN_FAIL`; preserve code/locus and detection anchor; append only the planned missing invalid completion, then decide/finish INVALID |
| primary `BLOCK_COMPLETE VALID`, or replacement `BLOCK_COMPLETE VALID`, before a later primary block intent | `T9_BETWEEN_VALID_BLOCKS` | `[null,null,null,null]` | acquire authority on crash; decide INVALID; synthesize no completed block or replacement |
| primary `BLOCK_COMPLETE INVALID`, before `BLOCK_REPLACEMENT_INTENT` | `T9_PRIMARY_INVALID_PRE_REPLACEMENT` | `[b,0,null,null]` | a still-live executor may append the one replacement intent before authority; after crash/authority decide INVALID and never replace |
| `BLOCK_REPLACEMENT_INTENT(b)`, before replacement `BLOCK_INTENT` | `T9_REPLACEMENT_INTENT_OPEN` | `[b,1,null,null]` | budget remains consumed; acquire authority; decide INVALID; partial and replacement receipt fields empty; synthesize no block event |
| replacement `BLOCK_COMPLETE VALID`, before a later primary block intent | `T9_POST_REPLACEMENT_VALID` | `[null,null,null,null]` | replacement receipt is complete; acquire authority on crash; decide INVALID; synthesize nothing already complete |
| replacement `BLOCK_COMPLETE INVALID`, or any later primary invalid completion after budget consumption | `T9_POST_REPLACEMENT_INVALID` | `[b,a,null,null]` | acquire authority; decide INVALID; never replace; bind all complete failed receipts |
| final controlling valid `BLOCK_COMPLETE`, with the complete schedule durable and before authority | `T9_POST_FINAL_BLOCK` | `[null,null,null,null]` | acquire authority; derive bootstrap/statistics; decide VALID and publish statistics, or decide INVALID under the statistics row below |
| authority exists and no domain decision exists | phase from authority-bound pre-event row, or `T9_STATISTICS_DERIVATION` while deriving a complete raw execution | locus from that row, or all null for derivation | never reacquire authority; deterministically recompute and compare-and-append the one decision; execute no timed work |
| statistics derivation yields a nonfinite scalar, schema mismatch, bounded rejection exhaustion, or controller resource exhaustion | `T9_STATISTICS_DERIVATION` | `[null,null,null,null]` | use existing authority; decide INVALID with the exact closed code; publish no `STATISTICS_COMPLETE`; append failure/terminal suffix |
| valid decision exists, before `STATISTICS_COMPLETE` | `T9_POST_DECISION` | `[null,null,null,null]` | append only the already decided authority-bound statistics artifact and its completion event |
| invalid decision exists before any required invalid block/failure event | `T9_POST_DECISION` as a re-entry cut | locus and origin phase from decision | append only the decision's child-to-parent synthesized closure and failure suffix; do not re-decide |
| `STATISTICS_COMPLETE`, before valid `T9_TERMINAL` | `T9_TERMINAL_SUFFIX` | `[null,null,null,null]` | append only the deterministic valid terminal |
| `T9_FAILURE`, before invalid `T9_TERMINAL`, or any missing event in a decided terminal suffix | `T9_TERMINAL_SUFFIX` as a re-entry cut | locus and origin phase from decision | append only the next missing deterministic invalid suffix event |
| exclusive `T9_TERMINAL` exists | no failure phase | status-bound locus absent | append nothing; compute/follow the final WAL and execution receipt only |
| malformed/gapped suffix after a longest hash-correct prefix | phase/locus of that longest-prefix row | that row's locus | bind the exact corrupt suffix digest, choose `HARNESS_SCHEMA_DIGEST_MISMATCH` by precedence, and follow that row; corrupt bytes never advance the prefix |

Rows describing a primary/replacement open block apply with `a=0`/`a=1`
respectively. A live predeclared subject invalidity already represented by
`SAMPLE_INVALID` or `BLOCK_INVALID_DETECTED` retains its earlier precedence
code instead of `CONTROLLER_CRASH_OR_INCOMPLETE`; otherwise incomplete action
evidence uses that controller code. A prefix with the complete raw schedule is
the only prefix eligible for deterministic valid statistics derivation. A cut
after any earlier valid block but before the next block intent is incomplete,
not an implicit completed schedule, unless that block is mechanically the
frozen final controlling block.

This table also controls post-authority re-entry: the authority's bound
pre-event prefix selects the row once; later authority/decision/suffix events
cannot move the origin phase or locus. The §11.4 replacement state machine,
single `BLOCK_COMPLETE` publisher, fixed invalid-detection anchor, and global
one-replacement budget remain unchanged.

### 12.4 Proof-total finite unbiased bootstrap rejection

RNG schema version 3 supersedes §11.5's unbounded version 2. `r` and `d` retain
their exact four-raw-octet `u32be` encoding. `j` retains canonical unsigned
ASCII decimal encoding but is now confined to the closed integer interval
`0 <= j <= 65535`; exactly 65,536 candidate positions exist for each
`[r,d]`. Define:

```text
bootstrapRngSchemaDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-bootstrap-rng-schema/3",
  "sha256-bounded-rejection-sampling",
  "u32be-r-raw-four-octets",
  "u32be-d-raw-four-octets",
  "j-final-canonical-ascii-decimal-0-through-65535",
  65536,
  "first-accepted-or-terminal-invalid-no-redraw"
]))))

candidateBytes = SHA256(
  UTF8("v-pi-bootstrap-rng/3\u0000")
  || bootstrapSeed
  || u32be(r)
  || u32be(d)
  || ASCII(canonicalDecimal(j))
)
```

For each cell, `n` is exactly its frozen complete-Williams `blockCount`:
`L*ceil(5/L)` for wave or `L*ceil(10/L)` for final, with `L=m` for even `m`
and `L=2m` for odd `m`; no runner-selected `n` exists. Let
`limit=floor(2^64/n)*n`; read `word` as the unsigned big-endian integer in the
first eight candidate bytes. Inspect `j=0,1,...,65535` in that order and return
`word mod n` for the first `word < limit`. At every accepted position, each
index `0..n-1` has exactly `limit/n` preimage words, so selection has no modulo
bias. The first-accept rule treats output indices symmetrically. The finite
loop is total for all candidate-byte sequences: if no word is accepted, append
no draw and classify exactly
`BOOTSTRAP_RNG_REJECTION_EXHAUSTED` at
`T9_STATISTICS_DERIVATION` with all-null locus. If the frozen runtime cannot
compute or retain a required candidate before reaching the bound, classify
exactly `BOOTSTRAP_RNG_RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED` at the same phase/locus. Both codes
are appended after the existing T9 authority as one INVALID decision, publish
no statistics completion, and map the one-shot execution to `T9_INVALID`,
cell `INVALID`, wave `WAVE_BLOCKED`, and final evidence-BLOCKED.

For decision and receipt fields, `t9FailureCode` and `t9InvalidityCode` name
the same token from one ordered closed enum; they are not independently
extensible vocabularies. `BOOTSTRAP_RNG_REJECTION_EXHAUSTED` and
`BOOTSTRAP_RNG_RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED` are inserted in that enum after
`MEASUREMENT_NONFINITE` and before `CONTROLLER_CRASH_OR_INCOMPLETE`;
simultaneous predicates use that exact order. Neither disposition permits
another `j`, bootstrap seed,
qualification execution, matched block, pilot, draw, resample, environment,
or result-conditioned retry. The partial bootstrap trace and exact failing
`[r,d]` are retained in the failure evidence, but they never become a partial
confidence interval.

Bootstrap seed version 5 supersedes version 4:

```text
bootstrapSeed = SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-bootstrap-seed/5",
  qualificationExecutionId,
  t9LaunchReceiptDigest,
  pilotTerminalReceiptDigest,
  numericEncodingSchemaDigest,
  statisticsSchemaDigest,
  bootstrapRngSchemaDigest,
  buildSchemaDigest,
  runtimeSchemaDigest,
  runnerSchemaDigest,
  batchPlanDigest,
  pairwiseDenominatorDigest,
  candidateBundleDigest,
  baselineBundleDigest,
  "bootstrap-v5"
])))
```

Every bootstrap trace, statistics artifact, T9 decision/terminal/execution,
wave/final, history, supersession, and close receipt binds RNG schema version 3
and seed version 5. The 10,000-resample count, paired log-ratio population,
median, type-1 bounds, no outlier deletion, and no valid-batch retry laws remain
unchanged.

## 13. Eighth controlling amendment — total T8 plan selection, corrupt-store binding, and deterministic finite-bootstrap failure

**Status:** controlling formation repair for fresh challenge B8-01 through
B8-03. This section supersedes only conflicting T8 plan-selection,
corrupt-suffix, finalization-receipt, statistics-resource, and bounded-RNG
failure language in BR-5/BR-6 and §§6–12. Every other accepted safeguard and
strict peer-beating rule remains normative. It grants no harness, pilot,
measurement, optimization, grammar, dependency, production, PB0, PB13P,
BBNF, coordination, inbox, or script authority.

Fresh challenge A8's `ACCEPT` applies only to ADDENDA-06 SHA-256
`a4fe985e73e520fb1db717b4408567ec4d124a8e3b4caf51f65e4dfa5f259a14`
under harden SHA-256
`04a1725b11cb1ef630031458ed1261ecb0de9a47080b376d7165357ba33dd82b`.
B8's `REJECT` proves those bytes non-canonical. Any transposition of this
section invalidates both A8 and B8; they grant zero acceptance credit to the

### 13.1 One frozen raw-store view and exact corrupt-suffix binding

For T8 and T9, finalization begins from one atomically read raw-store snapshot.
A durable action store is an ordered sequence of atomically written raw record
blobs; record framing is part of each blob, and an otherwise unframed trailing
byte sequence is retained as the final blob. This gives even malformed bytes
one unambiguous durable boundary without attempting to parse them first.
A **valid WAL prefix** is the longest ordered prefix beginning at its exact
genesis whose stored records have canonical framing, consecutive indices,
correct hash links, exact event/payload schema, and a permitted state-machine
transition. The **corrupt suffix** is every remaining stored raw record,
starting with the first record that fails any one of those requirements. A
skipped index followed by a later record starts the suffix at that later raw
record. Absence after an incomplete valid prefix is not a corrupt suffix; it
is the applicable crash/incomplete row in §§13.2 or 12.3.

The durable store exposes each remaining raw record including its framing as
canonical base64. Let `orderedCorruptRawRecordBytesBase64` be that ordered
array. Define exactly:

```text
corruptSuffixDigestOrEmptyString =
  ""                                      when the array is empty
  hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
    "v-pi-corrupt-wal-suffix/1",
    domain,
    attemptIdentity,
    authorityPreEventWalPrefixDigest,
    orderedCorruptRawRecordBytesBase64
  ]))))                                   otherwise

authorityPreEventRawStoreViewDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-authority-raw-store-view/1",
  domain,
  attemptIdentity,
  authorityPreEventWalPrefixDigest,
  corruptSuffixDigestOrEmptyString
]))))
```

The empty string is permitted **if and only if** the corrupt-record array is
empty. A nonempty value is exactly one lowercase 64-hex digest of the
non-empty array above. The raw array is retained in the owner-selected durable
artifact store; a digest without its retained content is evidence-BLOCKED.
No event inside the corrupt suffix advances the valid prefix, supplies an
observation, closes an intent, consumes or restores replacement budget, or
provides a result.

Before authority, the compare-and-append observes the exact
`authorityPreEventRawStoreViewDigest`, not merely the valid-prefix digest. It
atomically seals that action store and create-if-absent opens one separate
**finalization closure WAL** keyed by the raw-store view. Authority is the
closure WAL's genesis event; no finalization event is appended physically
after corrupt action-store bytes. Therefore a no-suffix snapshot and a
corrupt-suffix snapshot cannot acquire the same authority, and corrupt bytes
cannot make a valid authority event part of their own corrupt tail.

For both domains define:

```text
finalizationClosureWalEventDigest[i] = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-finalization-closure-wal-event/1",
  domain,
  attemptIdentity,
  i,
  i == 0 ? authorityPreEventRawStoreViewDigest
         : finalizationClosureWalEventDigest[i-1],
  finalizationPlanDigest,
  numericEncodingSchemaDigest,
  eventType,
  eventPayload
]))))
```

Event zero is exactly `FINALIZATION_AUTHORITY`; later closure records are the
domain decision, permitted fixed block completion, derived publication,
failure, panel/statistics completion, and terminal events in their controlling
order. The action WAL and closure WAL are immutable ordered segments of one
evidence object, not alternative histories. Any earlier formula that placed a
post-authority event back into the action WAL is superseded. Conforming closure
appends are canonical compare-and-appends from the bound plan/decision. Later
physical mutation of the sealed action store, or a malformed attempted closure
append, is **structural proof RED**; it cannot time-travel into the plan, create
a second decision, or revise an existing terminal.

The finalization plan advances to this exact version 2:

```text
finalizationPlanDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-finalization-plan/2",
  domain,
  attemptIdentity,
  authorityPreEventWalPrefixDigest,
  corruptSuffixDigestOrEmptyString,
  authorityPreEventRawStoreViewDigest,
  finalizationPlanClass,
  failurePhaseOrEmptyString,
  locusOrAllNull,
  orderedPreDecisionClosureDescriptors,
  derivedDispositionFunctionId
]))))
```

`derivedDispositionFunctionId` advances to
`v-pi-pilot-finalization-function/2` for T8 and
`v-pi-t9-finalization-function/2` for T9. Every other plan-class,
closure-descriptor, phase, and locus rule remains as narrowed below. A
nonempty corrupt suffix forces `HARNESS_SCHEMA_DIGEST_MISMATCH` by the frozen
precedence. For T8 its plan is `PILOT_FAIL_FROM_PREFIX`. For T9 its plan is
`T9_FAIL_FROM_PREFIX`, except a valid prefix ending in a committed
`BLOCK_INVALID_DETECTED` still uses
`T9_COMPLETE_COMMITTED_INVALID_THEN_FAIL` so the already fixed sole block
completion is published; in either case corruption prevents a VALID derived
result. The phase/locus is that of the longest valid prefix's table row.

The finalization-authority envelopes advance to these exact version-3 arrays;
the §12.1 closed five-field authority union remains unchanged:

```text
finalizationAuthorityEvidenceDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-finalization-authority-evidence/3",
  domain,
  attemptIdentity,
  launchReceiptDigest,
  authorityKind,
  recoveryBasis,
  ownerTokenDigestOrEmptyString,
  liveOwnerProofDigestOrEmptyString,
  durableTerminationReceiptDigestOrEmptyString,
  proofRestartReceiptDigestOrEmptyString,
  noLiveOwnerEvidenceDigestOrEmptyString,
  authorityPreEventWalPrefixDigest,
  corruptSuffixDigestOrEmptyString,
  authorityPreEventRawStoreViewDigest,
  finalizationPlanDigest
]))))

pilotFinalizationAuthorityReceiptDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-pilot-finalization-authority-receipt/3",
  pilotAttemptId,
  finalizationAuthorityEvidenceDigest,
  authorityPreEventWalPrefixDigest,
  corruptSuffixDigestOrEmptyString,
  authorityPreEventRawStoreViewDigest,
  finalizationPlanDigest
]))))

t9FinalizationAuthorityReceiptDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-t9-finalization-authority-receipt/3",
  qualificationExecutionId,
  finalizationAuthorityEvidenceDigest,
  authorityPreEventWalPrefixDigest,
  corruptSuffixDigestOrEmptyString,
  authorityPreEventRawStoreViewDigest,
  finalizationPlanDigest
]))))
```

Every domain decision carries the same value explicitly. Pilot decision
version 2 is the version-1 array with
`corruptSuffixDigestOrEmptyString` inserted immediately after
`authorityPreEventWalPrefixDigest`. T9 decision version 2 inserts, in this
order after that prefix field,
`corruptSuffixDigestOrEmptyString` and
`derivedFailureEvidenceReceiptDigestOrEmptyString`. Their tags become
`v-pi-pilot-finalization-decision/2` and
`v-pi-t9-finalization-decision/2`. The T9 derived-failure field is governed
only by §13.4; every other decision uses the empty string.

The following exact evidence versions carry the corrupt-suffix value directly
as well as transitively through authority and decision:

- pilot failure receipt version 4 inserts it after
  `pilotFailureEvidencePrefixDigest`;
- pilot panel version 4 inserts it after
  `pilotFinalizationAuthorityReceiptDigest`;
- pilot terminal version 4 inserts it after
  `pilotFinalizationAuthorityReceiptDigest`;
- T9 failure receipt version 3 inserts it after
  `t9FailureEvidencePrefixDigest`, followed by
  `derivedFailureEvidenceReceiptDigestOrEmptyString`;
- T9 evidence-prefix version 2 inserts it after
  `qualificationExecutionId`;
- T9 terminal version 3 inserts it after
  `t9FinalizationAuthorityReceiptDigest`, followed by
  `derivedFailureEvidenceReceiptDigestOrEmptyString`;
- pilot WAL version 2 and T9 WAL version 2 use the exact segmented formulas
  below; and
- T9 execution receipt version 3 inserts it after
  `t9FinalizationAuthorityReceiptDigest`, followed by
  `derivedFailureEvidenceReceiptDigestOrEmptyString`.

The final evidence formulas are exactly:

```text
pilotWalDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-pilot-wal/2",
  pilotAttemptId,
  finalActionEventCount,
  orderedFinalPilotActionWalEventDigests,
  corruptSuffixDigestOrEmptyString,
  finalClosureEventCount,
  orderedFinalPilotClosureWalEventDigests
]))))

t9WalDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-t9-wal/2",
  qualificationExecutionId,
  finalActionEventCount,
  orderedFinalT9ActionWalEventDigests,

```

Both counts equal the corresponding array lengths and are safe non-negative
JSON integers. Closure event zero must reproduce the authority receipt bound
to the same raw-store view and plan. An event appearing in both arrays, a gap,
or any other segmentation is structural RED.

The other named version changes are retain-and-insert rules only for the
named immediately prior exact
versions; no older displaced field reappears. A VALID pilot or T9 terminal
requires the corrupt-suffix value empty. A nonempty value requires INVALID,
`HARNESS_SCHEMA_DIGEST_MISMATCH`, and the matching failure chain. For an
invalidity unrelated to corrupt raw records the value remains empty. Thus
absence, presence, authority, decision, failure, terminal, final WAL, and T9
execution all agree without a reverse digest edge.

### 13.2 Total mutually exclusive T8 prefix-to-plan selector

The selector below replaces the prose application of the old eleven-row pilot
crash table. It is exhaustive over every pilot raw-store snapshot and is
evaluated in this strict order:

1. if a valid `FINALIZATION_AUTHORITY` exists, never select another plan;
   follow its bound version-2 plan and §12.1 deterministic suffix;
2. otherwise, if `corruptSuffixDigestOrEmptyString` is nonempty, select the
   corruption override in §13.1; and
3. otherwise select the one matching hash-correct row below.

Rows are mutually exclusive. `lastS`, `lastK`, and `lastO` are frozen final
indices for the applicable subject/round/observation. “Fail” means plan class
`PILOT_FAIL_FROM_PREFIX`; “derive” means plan class
`PILOT_DERIVE_PANEL`. A fail row binds its displayed phase and locus. A derive
row binds empty plan phase and `[null,null,null]`; its pure outcome rule below
alone may put `PILOT_DERIVATION_OPEN` into an INVALID decision.

| longest valid pilot WAL prefix / exact cut | sole plan class | plan phase and locus | sole consequence |
|---|---|---|---|
| `PILOT_LAUNCH`, before first `SUBJECT_INTENT` | fail | `PILOT_POST_LAUNCH`, `[null,null,null]` | INVALID `CRASH_OR_INCOMPLETE`; synthesize no child |
| `SUBJECT_INTENT(s)`, before `ROUND_INTENT` | fail | `PILOT_SUBJECT_OPEN_PRE_ROUND`, `[s,null,null]` | INVALID; synthesize only the open subject after decision |
| `ROUND_INTENT(s,k)`, including durable `SUBJECT_READY`, before first `OBSERVATION_INTENT` | fail | `PILOT_ROUND_OPEN_PRE_OBSERVATION`, `[s,k,null]` | INVALID; synthesize round then subject after decision |
| unmatched `OBSERVATION_INTENT(s,k,o)` | fail | `PILOT_OBSERVATION_OPEN`, `[s,k,o]` | INVALID; synthesize observation, round, subject after decision |
| terminal observation `o<lastO`, before the next observation intent, or terminal `lastO` before `ROUND_COMPLETE` | fail | `PILOT_BETWEEN_OBSERVATIONS`, `[s,k,o]` | INVALID; never resynthesize observation `o`; synthesize round then subject |
| `ROUND_COMPLETE` requiring another round, before its next `ROUND_INTENT` | fail | `PILOT_BETWEEN_ROUNDS`, `[s,k,null]` | INVALID; never resynthesize completed round; synthesize subject |
| final `ROUND_COMPLETE`, before `SUBJECT_COMPLETE` | fail | `PILOT_POST_FINAL_ROUND`, `[s,k,null]` | INVALID; synthesize subject only |
| selected `SUBJECT_COMPLETE(s)` with `s<lastS`, before next subject intent | fail | `PILOT_BETWEEN_SUBJECTS`, `[null,null,null]` | INVALID; synthesize no completed subject |
| selected final `SUBJECT_COMPLETE(lastS)`, before `PILOT_DERIVATION_INTENT` | fail | `PILOT_POST_LAST_SUBJECT`, `[null,null,null]` | INVALID `CRASH_OR_INCOMPLETE`; no derivation is inferred |
| a durable invalid observation, round, or subject terminal, before authority | fail | phase/locus of the first durable invalid origin above | INVALID with the earliest durable precedence code; synthesize only its still-open ancestors |
| `PILOT_DERIVATION_INTENT` after exactly every scheduled subject has one ordered `SUBJECT_SELECTED` receipt and no invalid predicate | derive | empty, `[null,null,null]` | run the pure derivation function below; no crash plan competes |
| a hash-correct but state-illegal event, including early/duplicate derivation intent, decision, panel, or terminal before authority | corruption override | phase/locus of the preceding valid row | the illegal record begins the corrupt suffix; INVALID schema mismatch |
| authority exists, no decision | bound plan | phase/locus already bound by that plan | recompute the one pure decision; never reacquire or resume work |
| VALID decision, before `PILOT_PANEL_COMPLETE` | bound derive plan | empty, all-null | publish only the decision-bound valid panel |
| INVALID decision, before required failure/panel suffix | bound plan | phase/locus inside decision | append only its child-to-parent synthesized closure, failure, and invalid panel |
| `PILOT_PANEL_COMPLETE`, before `PILOT_TERMINAL` | bound plan | valid empty/all-null or invalid decision origin | append only the deterministic terminal |
| exclusive `PILOT_TERMINAL` | bound plan | status-bound | append nothing; compute/follow final WAL evidence |

The live path may append `PILOT_DERIVATION_INTENT` after the final selected
subject and before authority. Once that intent is durable over the complete
selected-subject prefix, live owner and recovery actor see the identical
derive row. No actor kind, crash timing, or availability observation can turn
it into `PILOT_FAIL_FROM_PREFIX`.

The pure version-2 pilot disposition function reads only the complete selected
subject receipts, exact `f64be` values, frozen threshold constants, and
canonical common-`N` algorithm. It has exactly two results:

- if every decoded/derived value is finite, positive, in range, and the exact
  common-`N`/flag rule succeeds, produce the authority-bound panel artifact
  and decision `VALID`, with empty failure phase and all-null locus; or
- if any deterministic derivation predicate fails, produce no panel artifact
  digest in the decision and decide `INVALID` with code
  `PILOT_DERIVATION_INVALID`, phase `PILOT_DERIVATION_OPEN`, all-null locus,
  then publish the unique failure/invalid-panel suffix.

This invalid result is an outcome of the one derive plan, not a second fail
plan. Missing observations/subjects or an absent derivation intent never enter
that function. A nonempty corrupt suffix is selected before the table and
therefore cannot be laundered into derivation.

### 13.3 Pre-activation statistics-resource admission; no runtime-exhaustion verdict

`BOOTSTRAP_RNG_RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED` is **RETRACTED** from every failure-code
enum, precedence list, T9 table row, decision, failure, terminal, execution,
wave/final mapping, and owner-choice sentence. Host memory pressure, scheduler
failure, process death, or an implementation's inability to allocate after
authority is not a deterministic function of authority-bound bytes and may
not select an INVALID result.

Instead, the reviewed T0/T1 freeze must contain a streaming implementation
proof and exact `statisticsResourcePlanDigest`. For each batch it binds:

```text
bootstrapResampleCount = 10000
bootstrapDrawsPerResample = blockCount
bootstrapPeerCellCount = orderedPeerCellsInGroup.length
bootstrapMaximumCompletedDrawCount =
  bootstrapPeerCellCount * 10000 * blockCount
bootstrapMaximumCandidateCount =
  bootstrapPeerCellCount * 10000 * blockCount * 65536
bootstrapMaximumSingleDrawTraceCount = 65536

statisticsResourcePlanDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-statistics-resource-plan/1",
  statisticsImplementationSourceDigest,
  statisticsSchemaDigest,
  bootstrapRngSchemaDigest,
  blockCount,
  bootstrapPeerCellCount,
  bootstrapResampleCount,
  bootstrapMaximumCompletedDrawCount,
  bootstrapMaximumCandidateCount,
  bootstrapMaximumSingleDrawTraceCount,
  maximumScratchBytes,
  maximumRetainedTraceBytes,
  streamingBoundProofDigest
]))))
```

Every count and byte bound is a safe non-negative JSON control integer. The
review proves that the implementation stores at most 10,000 bootstrap median
scalars, the completed-draw receipt prefix, and one current draw's at-most
65,536-candidate rejection trace; it never accumulates all candidate bytes in
memory. The exact raw trace artifacts may stream to the selected durable
content-addressed store.

Before T7 activation—and therefore before any T8 attempt, T9 launch, sample,
or inspected timing result—the environment must reserve and touch the fixed
scratch/storage buffers and verify the content-addressed sink against those
bounds. Define:

```text
statisticsResourceAdmissionReceiptDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-statistics-resource-admission/1",
  qualificationRunId,
  environmentClassDigest,
  batchPlanDigest,
  statisticsResourcePlanDigest,
  maximumScratchBytes,
  maximumRetainedTraceBytes,
  durableArtifactStoreDigest,
  "RESERVED_TOUCHED_AND_VERIFIED"
]))))
```

`durableArtifactStoreDigest` is the exact store identity frozen under the
owner's §4/ADDENDA-06 §13 retention choice; a path string or newly selected
store is not a substitute.

Batch activation advances to this exact version 2:

```text
batchActivationReceiptDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-batch-activation/2",
  candidateCausalReceiptDigest,
  qualificationId,
  qualificationRunId,
  runnerSchemaDigest,
  numericEncodingSchemaDigest,
  canonicalGroupPartitionDigest,
  canonicalGroupDigestForGroupIndex,
  batchScheduleDigest,
  statisticsResourcePlanDigest,
  statisticsResourceAdmissionReceiptDigest,
  mechanicalScheduleVerificationDigest
]))))
```

Failure to produce the resource receipt leaves T7 BLOCKED and
creates no T8 attempt. A resource-plan or implementation-byte change requires
the ordinary reviewed causal/schema supersession and cannot be made after
observations.

After authority, the statistics function is the frozen finite streaming
function. If its host process dies, the WAL remains at the same authority-
bound prefix and a conforming recovery actor recomputes the same pure result;
no resource event, failure code, redraw, new execution, or result-conditioned
environment is appended. `CONTROLLER_CRASH_OR_INCOMPLETE` remains available
only for missing action-bearing **pre-authority raw execution** under the
existing T9 table. It cannot classify a crash inside post-authority pure
derivation. An executor that cannot satisfy the frozen resource admission may
not activate or acquire T9 finalization authority.

### 13.4 Exact deterministic bounded-rejection exhaustion evidence

RNG schema version 3, bootstrap seed version 5, the `j=0..65535` first-accept
law, 10,000 resamples, and no redraw remain unchanged. Statistics visits peer
cells in exact `orderedPeerCellsInGroup` order. Within each cell iteration is
exactly lexicographic: `r=0..9999` outermost; for each `r`, `d=0..n-1`; for
each draw, `j=0..65535`. Therefore the first exhausted cell and its first
failing `[r,d]` are unique.

For every draw define a rejected-candidate chain. Its genesis and step are:

```text
rejectedCandidateChainDigest[-1] = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-bootstrap-rejected-candidate-chain-genesis/1",
  qualificationExecutionId,
  peerCellIndex,
  peerCellManifestDigest,
  bootstrapSeedHex,
  n,
  r,
  d
]))))

rejectedCandidateChainDigest[j] = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-bootstrap-rejected-candidate-chain-step/1",
  rejectedCandidateChainDigest[j-1],
  r,
  d,
  j,
  hex(candidateBytes),
  "u64be:" + wordAsSixteenLowercaseHexDigits,
  "REJECTED"
]))))
```

The step exists only when `word >= limit`. For an accepted draw, retain its
exact `j`, candidate digest/word, selected index, and preceding rejected-chain
digest in:

```text
bootstrapAcceptedDrawReceiptDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-bootstrap-accepted-draw/1",
  qualificationExecutionId,
  peerCellIndex,
  peerCellManifestDigest,
  bootstrapSeedHex,
  n,
  r,
  d,
  acceptedJ,
  acceptedCandidateDigestHex,
  acceptedWordU64be,
  selectedBlockIndex,
  rejectedCandidateChainDigest[acceptedJ-1]
]))))
```

`bootstrapSeedHex` is lowercase hex of the exact 32 seed bytes. The candidate
digest is lowercase hex of the exact 32 `candidateBytes`; `acceptedWordU64be`
uses the existing exact observed-counter encoding. For `acceptedJ=0`, the
preceding chain is the genesis digest. These receipts are appended to one
ordered derived trace in `(r,d)` order.

If all 65,536 candidates for the current draw reject, define:

```text
bootstrapPartialTraceDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-bootstrap-partial-trace/1",
  qualificationExecutionId,
  peerCellIndex,
  peerCellManifestDigest,
  bootstrapSeedHex,
  n,
  r,
  d,
  orderedAcceptedDrawReceiptDigestsBeforeFailure,
  orderedCompletedResampleMedianF64beScalars,
  orderedCompletedPeerStatisticsArtifactDigestsBeforeFailure
]))))

bootstrapRejectionExhaustionReceiptDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-bootstrap-rejection-exhaustion/1",
  qualificationExecutionId,
  t9FinalizationAuthorityReceiptDigest,
  finalizationPlanDigest,
  corruptSuffixDigestOrEmptyString,
  peerCellIndex,
  peerCellManifestDigest,
  pairwiseDenominatorDigest,
  bootstrapRngSchemaDigest,
  bootstrapSeedHex,
  n,
  r,
  d,
  65536,
  rejectedCandidateChainDigest[65535],
  bootstrapPartialTraceDigest,
  "BOOTSTRAP_RNG_REJECTION_EXHAUSTED"
]))))
```

`peerCellIndex` is the zero-based position in `orderedPeerCellsInGroup` and
must name the row whose manifest digest is present. The completed-peer array
has exactly `peerCellIndex` entries and contains each prior cell's valid
statistics artifact in that same canonical order. Within the failing cell,
the ordered accepted-draw array has exactly `r*n+d` entries. The completed-
resample scalar array has exactly `r` entries and is derived from the first
`r*n` draw receipts. Across the batch, exactly
`peerCellIndex*10000*n + r*n+d` accepted draw receipts precede exhaustion.
Both arrays and the 65,536 exact rejected-candidate raw trace records are
retained; the digests alone are not a retention substitute. Any count, order,
candidate, chain, scalar, seed, cell, or failing-locus mismatch is structural
RED.

The exhaustion receipt is deterministic derived evidence computed after the
already bound T9 authority. The T9 version-2 decision uses disposition
`INVALID`, empty statistics-artifact field, code
`BOOTSTRAP_RNG_REJECTION_EXHAUSTED`, phase
`T9_STATISTICS_DERIVATION`, all-null locus, and places this exact receipt in
`derivedFailureEvidenceReceiptDigestOrEmptyString`. After that decision,
append exactly:

```text
BOOTSTRAP_REJECTION_EXHAUSTED [bootstrapRejectionExhaustionReceiptDigest]
T9_FAILURE                    [t9FailureReceiptDigest]
T9_TERMINAL                   [t9TerminalReceiptDigest]
```

The closed T9 WAL schema gains only that derived exhaustion event. T9 failure
version 3, terminal version 3, WAL version 2, and execution version 3 bind the
same receipt directly or through the decision as required in §13.1. For every
other failure code, `derivedFailureEvidenceReceiptDigestOrEmptyString` is the
empty string; a nonempty value with another code, or an empty value with the
rejection-exhausted code, is structural RED.

A crash before the exhaustion decision leaves no alternate observation: on
re-entry the authority-bound seed, cell, `n`, and raw matched-block evidence
regenerate the identical accepted-draw prefix, rejected chain, first failing
`[r,d]`, receipt, and decision bytes. A crash after decision appends only the
missing event/failure/terminal suffix. No later `j`, draw, resample, seed,
pilot, matched block, environment, qualification execution, or partial
confidence interval exists. The deterministic exhaustion remains `T9_INVALID`,
cell `INVALID`, wave `WAVE_BLOCKED`, and final evidence-BLOCKED.

The complete-sample T9 table's statistics row now reads only: deterministic
nonfinite/schema derivation failure, or
`BOOTSTRAP_RNG_REJECTION_EXHAUSTED`, each under the existing authority. The
retracted resource token is not an alternate row. A corrupt suffix is selected
before statistics and therefore cannot coexist with an exhaustion receipt.

## 14. Ninth controlling amendment — disjoint T8 selection, segmented prefix algebra, and exact statistics artifacts

**Status:** controlling formation repair for current A9-01 and B9-01/B9-02.
This section supersedes only conflicting T8-selector predicates, post-authority
prefix fields, closure-event schemas, and statistics-artifact references in
BR-5/BR-6 and §§6–13. Every other strict semantic, peer, environment,
Williams-cycle, resource-admission, one-shot, owner, and BBNF boundary remains
normative. It grants no harness, pilot, smoke, benchmark, measurement,
optimization, parser, dependency, production, PB0, PB13P, BBNF, coordination,
inbox, or script authority.

Current A9 and B9 challenged ADDENDA-06 SHA-256
`5541413532fccaaf0e71d52f26da784e9a53d6dc282c588c34eedc0d9173a21f`
under harden SHA-256
`89ebd79e137dce68fdc036c61d2be54b0407862d44eeca342e92b70da31e8e28`.
Both are `REJECT`; any transposition of this section changes the governing
bytes, so A9/B9 grant zero acceptance credit to the next artifact.

### 14.1 Strict total T8 selector with durable-invalid precedence

The §13.2 selector is replaced by this strict, first-match law. Exactly one
case is selected; later cases are not inspected after a match:

1. if a valid closure-WAL `FINALIZATION_AUTHORITY` exists, follow only its
   bound plan and the longest valid closure prefix under §14.2; an authority,
   decision, panel, failure, or terminal is never an action-prefix selector;
2. otherwise, if `corruptSuffixDigestOrEmptyString` is nonempty, select the
   §13.1 corruption override and `HARNESS_SCHEMA_DIGEST_MISMATCH`;
3. otherwise, scan the hash-correct action prefix for durable invalid
   terminals. If one or more exist, select the winning invalid origin by the
   frozen failure-code precedence in BR-5, then by the lowest action-event
   index among origins with that code. Bind that origin's code, phase, and
   locus exactly as specified below; no later OK/completion row competes;
4. otherwise, if the last action event is the one complete
   `PILOT_DERIVATION_INTENT` following exactly one ordered
   `SUBJECT_COMPLETE ... SUBJECT_SELECTED` per scheduled subject, select
   `PILOT_DERIVE_PANEL`; and
5. otherwise select the one last-action-event row in the success/incomplete
   table below.

The durable-invalid origins in step 3 are exact:

| earliest winning durable invalid origin | plan phase and locus | preserved code/source |
|---|---|---|
| `OBSERVATION_INVALID(s,k,o,code)` | `PILOT_OBSERVATION_OPEN`, `[s,k,o]` | the exact `code` in that event |
| `ROUND_COMPLETE(s,k,...,PILOT_INVALID)` with no earlier winning invalid observation | `PILOT_BETWEEN_OBSERVATIONS`, `[s,k,lastO]` | exactly `PILOT_N_EXHAUSTED`, proven by seven `OBSERVATION_OK` events at `k=30` and the frozen below-25-ms rule |
| `SUBJECT_COMPLETE(s,...,PILOT_INVALID)` | the phase/locus and code of its required earlier invalid observation or round | the already selected earlier origin; the subject event never replaces it |

A hash-correct `ROUND_COMPLETE ... PILOT_INVALID` not justified by one of
those two preceding origins, or a hash-correct
`SUBJECT_COMPLETE ... PILOT_INVALID` without its required earlier invalid
round, is a state-illegal record. By §13.1 it begins the corrupt suffix and is
therefore selected by step 2, not by step 3. Synthesized closure events are not
in the action prefix and cannot become a new durable-invalid origin.

Every row below has the implicit predicate **no durable invalid origin exists**
and is disjoint by its exact last event and status:

| exact last hash-correct action event / cut | sole plan phase and locus | sole consequence |
|---|---|---|
| `PILOT_LAUNCH`, before first subject intent | `PILOT_POST_LAUNCH`, `[null,null,null]` | fail `CRASH_OR_INCOMPLETE`; no child synthesis |
| unmatched `SUBJECT_INTENT(s)` | `PILOT_SUBJECT_OPEN_PRE_ROUND`, `[s,null,null]` | fail; synthesize subject after decision |
| `ROUND_INTENT(s,k)` or its `SUBJECT_READY`, before first observation intent | `PILOT_ROUND_OPEN_PRE_OBSERVATION`, `[s,k,null]` | fail; synthesize round then subject |
| unmatched `OBSERVATION_INTENT(s,k,o)` | `PILOT_OBSERVATION_OPEN`, `[s,k,o]` | fail; synthesize observation, round, subject |
| `OBSERVATION_OK(s,k,o)` with `o<lastO`, before the next observation intent, or `o=lastO` before round completion | `PILOT_BETWEEN_OBSERVATIONS`, `[s,k,o]` | fail; never resynthesize observation; synthesize round then subject |
| `ROUND_COMPLETE(s,k,...,DOUBLE)`, before `ROUND_INTENT(s,k+1)` | `PILOT_BETWEEN_ROUNDS`, `[s,k,null]` | fail; never resynthesize round; synthesize subject |
| `ROUND_COMPLETE(s,k,...,SUBJECT_SELECTED)`, before subject completion | `PILOT_POST_FINAL_ROUND`, `[s,k,null]` | fail; synthesize subject only |
| `SUBJECT_COMPLETE(s,...,SUBJECT_SELECTED)` with `s<lastS`, before next subject intent | `PILOT_BETWEEN_SUBJECTS`, `[null,null,null]` | fail; synthesize nothing already complete |
| `SUBJECT_COMPLETE(lastS,...,SUBJECT_SELECTED)`, before derivation intent | `PILOT_POST_LAST_SUBJECT`, `[null,null,null]` | fail `CRASH_OR_INCOMPLETE`; derivation is not inferred |

No bare “terminal observation,” “round completion,” or “subject completion”
predicate remains. `OBSERVATION_OK`, `DOUBLE`, and `SUBJECT_SELECTED` are
required literally in the success/incomplete table; every durable invalid
terminal is selected first. The complete derivation-intent row retains the
§13.2 pure VALID/`PILOT_DERIVATION_INVALID` outcome and all-null derivation
locus. A hash-correct-but-state-illegal stored record cannot reach step 5:
valid-prefix construction stops before it, the record enters the corrupt
suffix, and step 2 controls.

### 14.2 Exact segmented WAL prefix algebra and closure cuts

For either domain let `orderedActionEventDigests` be exactly the complete
hash-correct action-prefix digest array through
`authorityPreEventWalPrefixDigest`, and let `actionEventCount` be its safe
non-negative JSON-integer length. Its final element and count must reproduce
the applicable version-1 action-prefix formula; any mismatch is structural
RED. After atomic action-store sealing define:

```text
sealedActionSegmentPrefixDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-sealed-action-segment-prefix/1",
  domain,
  attemptIdentity,
  actionEventCount,
  orderedActionEventDigests,
  authorityPreEventWalPrefixDigest,
  corruptSuffixDigestOrEmptyString,
  authorityPreEventRawStoreViewDigest
]))))

finalizationClosureWalPrefixDigest[c] = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-finalization-closure-wal-prefix/1",
  domain,
  attemptIdentity,
  authorityPreEventRawStoreViewDigest,
  finalizationPlanDigest,
  c,
  orderedClosureEventDigests[0..c-1]
]))))

segmentedEvidencePrefixDigest[c] = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-segmented-evidence-prefix/1",
  domain,
  attemptIdentity,
  sealedActionSegmentPrefixDigest,
  corruptSuffixDigestOrEmptyString,
  finalizationClosureWalPrefixDigest[c]
]))))
```

`c` is the safe non-negative count of closure events already committed.
For `c=0`, the closure array is exactly empty. For `c>0`, it has exactly `c`
consecutive event digests, event zero is `FINALIZATION_AUTHORITY`, and every
event reproduces the same domain, attempt, raw-store view, plan, and numeric
schema. No closure event, receipt published by it, terminal digest, final-WAL
digest, or execution receipt enters its own pre-append prefix.

The segmented-prefix schema identity is:

```text
segmentedEvidencePrefixSchemaDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-segmented-evidence-prefix-schema/1",
  "sealed-action-segment-prefix/1",
  "finalization-closure-wal-prefix/1",
  "combined-prefix-before-current-closure-append",
  "event-zero-authority",
  "no-current-event-or-descendant"
]))))
```

Closure events advance to version 2. Event `i` is computed only from prefix
`i`, and its payload's final field is exactly that prefix:

```text
finalizationClosureWalEventDigest[i] = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-finalization-closure-wal-event/2",
  domain,
  attemptIdentity,
  i,
  i == 0 ? authorityPreEventRawStoreViewDigest
         : finalizationClosureWalEventDigest[i-1],
  finalizationPlanDigest,
  numericEncodingSchemaDigest,
  segmentedEvidencePrefixSchemaDigest,
  eventType,
  [...eventSpecificPayloadFields, segmentedEvidencePrefixDigest[i]]
]))))
```

Thus authority event zero contains
`[finalizationAuthorityReceiptDigest,segmentedEvidencePrefixDigest[0]]`.
Every later event contains its own pre-append prefix, never the prefix after
itself. The closed post-authority payload schemas are exactly:

```text
PILOT_FINALIZATION_DECISION [pilotFinalizationDecisionReceiptDigest,prefix]
OBSERVATION_INVALID         [s,k,o,pilotInvalidityCode,prefix]       // synthesized only
ROUND_COMPLETE              [s,k,pilotRoundDigest,"PILOT_INVALID",prefix] // synthesized only
SUBJECT_COMPLETE            [s,pilotSubjectReceiptDigest,"PILOT_INVALID",prefix] // synthesized only
PILOT_FAILURE               [pilotFailureReceiptDigest,prefix]
PILOT_PANEL_COMPLETE        [pilotPanelStatus,pilotPanelReceiptDigest,prefix]
PILOT_TERMINAL              [pilotTerminalStatus,pilotTerminalReceiptDigest,prefix]

T9_FINALIZATION_DECISION    [t9FinalizationDecisionReceiptDigest,prefix]
SAMPLE_INVALID              [b,a,p,j,t9InvalidityCode,prefix]       // synthesized only
SUBJECT_COMPLETE            [b,a,p,t9SubjectReceiptDigest,"INVALID",prefix] // synthesized only
BLOCK_INVALID_DETECTED      [b,a,pOrNull,jOrNull,t9FailureCode,prefix]
BLOCK_COMPLETE              [b,a,t9BlockReceiptDigest,blockStatus,prefix]
BOOTSTRAP_REJECTION_EXHAUSTED [bootstrapRejectionExhaustionReceiptDigest,prefix]
T9_FAILURE                  [t9FailureReceiptDigest,prefix]
STATISTICS_COMPLETE         [batchStatisticsArtifactDigest,prefix]
T9_TERMINAL                 [t9TerminalStatus,t9TerminalReceiptDigest,prefix]
```

Here `prefix` is literally `segmentedEvidencePrefixDigest[i]` for the event's
index `i`; `a` is `attemptOrdinal`. A post-authority event type not in this
list or an extra/missing/reordered payload field is structural RED. An event
used only by the action path keeps its action-WAL schema and may not appear in
the closure WAL.

All former ambiguous post-authority prefix names are now exact aliases at
their event cuts:

- `preDetectionEvidencePrefixDigest` is the prefix before its
  `BLOCK_INVALID_DETECTED` append;
- a synthesized round/subject receipt's `*WalPrefixDigest` is the prefix
  before the corresponding `ROUND_COMPLETE`/`SUBJECT_COMPLETE` append;
- `pilotFailureEvidencePrefixDigest` and `t9FailureEvidencePrefixDigest` in a
  decision/failure chain are the prefix immediately before that domain's
  finalization-decision append; the failure event additionally carries its
  own later pre-append prefix in its payload;
- `panelPilotWalPrefixDigest` is renamed
  `panelDerivationInputSegmentedEvidencePrefixDigest` and is exactly the
  prefix immediately after authority and before panel derivation/decision;
- `pilotEvidencePrefixDigest` and `t9EvidencePrefixDigest` are the prefix
  immediately before the applicable terminal append; and
- a closure-synthesized invalid `BLOCK_COMPLETE` uses the fixed detection
  event as its invalid anchor and its own pre-append prefix. No
  `validBlockEvidencePrefixDigest` is synthesized.

The synthesized pilot round and subject envelopes advance to version 3 and
append their corresponding pre-completion segmented prefix immediately before
`numericEncodingSchemaDigest`. The synthesized T9 subject envelope advances
to version 2 by the same rule. A closure-synthesized invalid block uses exact
version 3:

```text
t9BlockReceiptDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-t9-block/3",
  qualificationExecutionId,
  b, attemptOrdinal,
  scheduledSubjectIds,
  orderedSubjectReceiptDigests,
  orderedRawSampleEventDigests,
  "INVALID",
  t9InvalidityCode,
  "",
  invalidBlockEvidenceAnchorDigest,
  blockCompletionPreAppendSegmentedEvidencePrefixDigest,
  segmentedEvidencePrefixSchemaDigest,
  numericEncodingSchemaDigest
]))))
```

Version 2 block receipts remain valid only when published in the action WAL;
version 3 is mandatory and valid only for the authority-bound synthesized
invalid completion. The invalid anchor is the one earlier committed detection
event, while the prefix is the later cut immediately before completion; those
two fields cannot alias or substitute for each other.

Decision receipts advance to version 3 and append
`decisionPreAppendSegmentedEvidencePrefixDigest` and
`segmentedEvidencePrefixSchemaDigest` after their prior version-2 fields.
Pilot failure advances to version 5 and T9 failure to version 4: each retains
its immediately prior exact fields, interprets its existing failure-evidence
prefix as the decision-pre-append segmented prefix, and appends the failure-
event pre-append segmented prefix plus the prefix-schema digest. Pilot panel
advances to version 5 and binds only
`panelDerivationInputSegmentedEvidencePrefixDigest`; pilot terminal advances
to version 5 and T9 terminal to version 4 and binds its terminal-pre-append
segmented prefix. T9 execution advances to version 4 and binds
`segmentedEvidencePrefixSchemaDigest` plus the completed version-3 segmented
WAL digest. These are retain-and-append version changes from the exact §13.1
versions; no displaced older field reappears and no prefix may be omitted.

The derivation-input rule is deliberately narrower than publication: a pilot
panel or statistics artifact is computed from
`segmentedEvidencePrefixDigest[1]`, immediately after its authority event and
before its decision. It binds that input prefix. Its later decision binds the
artifact digest, and the later panel/statistics publication event binds its
own pre-append prefix containing the decision. Making the derived artifact
bind the pre-publication prefix would create a decision↔artifact cycle and is
forbidden.

Final WAL formulas advance to version 3, retain the §13.1 ordered action and
closure arrays/counts, and append `segmentedEvidencePrefixSchemaDigest` and
the final `segmentedEvidencePrefixDigest[finalClosureEventCount]`. The final
prefix is computed after the terminal; neither terminal nor any earlier event
binds that final descendant.

### 14.3 Exact per-peer and batch statistics artifacts

Statistics consume the complete valid raw T9 block evidence and the derivation
input prefix immediately after T9 authority. For peer-cell index `q`, let
`canonicalPeerCellRow` be byte-for-byte
`orderedPeerCellsInGroup[q]`; its fields are exactly
`[baselineSubjectId,baselineBundleDigest,pairwiseDenominatorDigest,
peerCellManifestDigest]`. Define one row per logical block:

```text
perBlockPeerStatisticsRow[b] = [
  b,
  orderedControllingBlockReceiptDigests[b],
  baselineMedianDurationF64be,
  candidateMedianDurationF64be,
  speedRatioF64be,
  pairedLogSpeedRatioF64be,
  speedRatioF64be > f64be:3ff0000000000000 ? "WIN" : "NON_WIN"
]
```

Rows are in exact `b=0..blockCount-1` order. Their receipt projection equals
`orderedControllingBlockReceiptDigests` byte for byte. Each receipt is the
sole controlling valid primary or replacement block receipt for that logical
block and transitively binds every subject/sample event; an invalid, duplicate,
missing, out-of-order, or foreign receipt prevents VALID statistics.

For each peer derive its version-5 bootstrap seed using that row's exact
baseline bundle and pairwise denominator. Define:

```text
peerStatisticsArtifactDigest[q] = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-peer-statistics-artifact/1",
  qualificationExecutionId,
  t9FinalizationAuthorityReceiptDigest,
  statisticsDerivationInputSegmentedEvidencePrefixDigest,
  qualificationKind,
  qualificationId,
  qualificationRunId,
  environmentClassDigest,
  batchPlanDigest,
  batchScheduleDigest,
  batchActivationReceiptDigest,
  statisticsResourcePlanDigest,
  statisticsResourceAdmissionReceiptDigest,
  buildSchemaDigest,
  runtimeSchemaDigest,
  runnerSchemaDigest,
  statisticsSchemaDigest,
  bootstrapRngSchemaDigest,
  numericEncodingSchemaDigest,
  segmentedEvidencePrefixSchemaDigest,
  canonicalPeerCellRowSchemaDigest,
  canonicalGroupPartitionDigest,
  groupIndex,
  canonicalGroupDigestForGroupIndex,
  q,
  canonicalPeerCellRow,
  candidateSubjectId,
  candidateBundleDigest,
  baselineSubjectId,
  baselineBundleDigest,
  pairwiseDenominatorDigest,
  peerCellManifestDigest,
  blockCount,
  orderedControllingBlockReceiptDigests,
  orderedPerBlockPeerStatisticsRows,
  aggregateLogSpeedRatioF64be,
  aggregateSpeedRatioF64be,
  bootstrapSeedHex,
  10000,
  orderedBootstrapAcceptedDrawReceiptDigests,
  orderedBootstrapMedianLogRatioF64beScalars,
  bootstrapLowerLogRatioF64be,
  bootstrapUpperLogRatioF64be,
  bootstrapLowerSpeedRatioF64be,
  bootstrapUpperSpeedRatioF64be,
  winningBlockCount,
  requiredWinningBlocksOrNull,
  peerStatisticsState,
  "VALID"
]))))
```

`statisticsDerivationInputSegmentedEvidencePrefixDigest` is exactly
`segmentedEvidencePrefixDigest[1]`. The accepted-draw array has exactly
`10000*blockCount` entries in `(r,d)` order; the median array has exactly
10,000 entries in `r` order. Every scalar uses `f64be`; all counts/indices are
safe non-negative JSON integers. `bootstrapSeedHex` is lowercase hex of the
exact version-5 seed. `winningBlockCount` is the count of literal `WIN` rows.
For `wave`, `requiredWinningBlocksOrNull` is JSON `null` and
`peerStatisticsState` is exactly `WAVE_GREEN`, `WAVE_AMBER`, or `WAVE_RED`
under BR-6. For `final`, it is exactly
`ceil(0.90*blockCount)` and the state is exactly `FASTER`, `SLOWER`, or
`INCONCLUSIVE`. `WAVE_BLOCKED`, registry/evidence states, or `INVALID` cannot
appear in a VALID peer statistics artifact.

After every peer succeeds in exact canonical order, define:

batchStatisticsArtifactDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([

]))))

`orderedPeerStatisticsArtifactDigests` has exactly
`orderedPeerCellsInGroup.length` entries. At every index `q`, its artifact's

`STATISTICS_COMPLETE` publishes that same digest. The batch has no decision,
event, terminal, WAL, or execution digest field and therefore creates no
backedge.

The earlier broad requirement that a statistics artifact itself bind a T9
decision, `STATISTICS_COMPLETE` event, terminal, final WAL, execution receipt,

direction is only: derivation-input prefix and authority → peer artifacts →

execution → wave/final/history/close. Each downstream artifact binds the

descendant. Report-only MAD, min/max, and coefficient-of-variation values
remain outside these controlling identities and cannot affect a state unless a

formulas.

**Status:** controlling formation repair for current A10-01. This section

### 15.1 Outcome-free complete canonical peer census

For each candidate, `qualificationKind`, and `publicOperationId`, T0 first
materializes the complete required baseline census from the frozen subject
registry plus fixed/immediate-anchor receipts. No semantic, artifact, ABI,
timing, expected-speed, prior-verdict, grouping, or environment result may
remove a registered predecessor or required anchor from this census.

When one baseline fulfills several roles, merge them into one non-empty
`orderedRequiredPeerRoles` array in this exact order when present:
`REGISTERED_PREDECESSOR`, `FIXED_FIRST_ACCEPTED_ANCHOR`,
`IMMEDIATELY_PREVIOUS_ACCEPTED_ANCHOR`. The outcome-free row is exactly:

```text
canonicalPeerCensusRow = [
  baselineSubjectId,
  baselineBundleDigest,
  orderedRequiredPeerRoles
]
```

Sort rows by unsigned lexicographic comparison of their exact UTF-8
`JSON.stringify` bytes. Equal row bytes, a repeated role, an empty role array,
or two rows with the same `baselineSubjectId` is structural RED. A separately
reconstructed bundle must already have a registry-versioned subject ID.

completeCanonicalPeerCensusDigest = hex(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify([
  "v-pi-complete-canonical-peer-census/1",

The existing exact four-string `canonicalPeerCellRow` is now an

row schema.

### 15.2 One static first-match disposition and one fixed-field receipt

After canonical-shape, registry-completeness, role, candidate, anchor, and
duplicate checks pass, but before any equal-work class or T8 observation,
classify every census row by this strict first-match order. Later predicates
are not inspected after a match:

1. `NOT_APPLICABLE` when the frozen operation registry proves that the
   subject does not implement `publicOperationId`;
2. `UNAVAILABLE_ARTIFACT` when the operation applies but the exact registered
   baseline bundle/build closure cannot be durably reconstructed under BR-2;
3. `UNSUPPORTED_ABI` when that exact closure exists but cannot execute the
   frozen qualification runtime ABI;
4. `INCOMPARABLE` when both operations exist and execute but complete BR-3
   review cannot establish the same semantics and cost boundary without a
   parser/evaluator, compatibility shim, or other distorting adapter;
5. `NO_COMMON_DENOMINATOR` when same-operation/same-cost comparison is
   otherwise available but at least one mandatory selected-frozen pairwise
   stratum has an empty included-row list; or
6. `EXECUTABLE` only when the artifact and ABI are available, the operation is
   applicable and comparable, every mandatory stratum is non-empty, and the
   complete BR-3 eligibility, denominator, manifest, and projections are
   mechanically GREEN.

These six tokens are exhaustive. The five non-`EXECUTABLE` tokens are the
only terminal peer-cell dispositions. A missing outcome, conflicting
predicate evidence, or use of `INVALID`, `UNAVAILABLE_ENVIRONMENT`, a wave/
final state, or timing data as a preflight disposition is structural RED.

`dispositionEvidenceDigest` is the SHA-256 digest of exactly one tagged array:

```text
NOT_APPLICABLE [
  "v-pi-not-applicable-evidence/1",
  publicOperationRegistryDigest,
  subjectOperationRegistryRowDigest,
  publicOperationId,
  "OPERATION_ABSENT"
]

UNAVAILABLE_ARTIFACT [

  "NO_DURABLE_EXECUTABLE_CLOSURE"

]

INCOMPARABLE [

]

EXECUTABLE [
  "v-pi-executable-peer-cell-evidence/1",
  pairwiseEligibilityReceiptsDigest,
  pairwiseDenominatorDigest,
  peerCellManifestDigest,
  canonicalPeerCellRow,

]
```

`incomparableReasonCode` is exactly one of
`SEMANTIC_PROJECTION_UNAVAILABLE`, `COST_BOUNDARY_MISMATCH`, or
`DISTORTING_ADAPTER_REQUIRED`, selected in that order when several are proved.
`orderedEmptyMandatoryStratumDigests` is non-empty, sorted by unsigned digest
bytes, contains every and only empty mandatory stratum, and is also visible in
the pairwise denominator/manifest. Thus an empty mandatory denominator cannot
disappear or become an executable zero-row workload.

### 15.3 Executable-only groups without favorable regrouping

Only `EXECUTABLE` partition rows enter workload-equivalence classes,
`orderedAllPeerCells`, canonical groups, `orderedSubjectArtifacts`, `m`,
Williams plans/schedules, §14.3 valid statistics, or §14.4 partial traces. The
executable projection is partitioned by the existing exact workload tuple;
class order is unsigned order of `workloadEquivalenceClassDigest`, within-
class order is the existing four-string row-byte order, and each class uses
the existing forced consecutive maximal 15-baseline chunks. Filtering or
regrouping by timing, expected speed, previous verdict, author choice,
environment observation, or desired `m` is structural RED. Terminal cells
remain in the full census/partition binding but cannot split, merge,
rebalance, reorder, or poison an executable group.

If the executable projection is empty, the class/group/plan set is exactly
empty: no fictitious `q>=1`, `m`, Williams schedule, activation, T8, T9, peer
statistics, or batch statistics artifact exists. Terminal receipts still
cover the full census and flow to wave/final/close. A no-peer cohort can never
make peer-beating GREEN. If the projection is non-empty, every executable row
occurs in exactly one forced group and is timed exactly once per required
qualification kind/environment; every terminal row occurs in no group and is
reported exactly once.

The version-2 batch plan advances to `v-pi-batch-plan/3`, retains all version-
2 fields in order, and appends exactly:

```text

peerCellDispositionPartitionDigest,
orderedExecutablePeerCellsDigest,
orderedTerminalDispositionReceiptsDigest,
peerCellPartitionBindingDigest,
orderedExecutablePreflightReceiptDigestsInGroup
```

The schedule, mechanical schedule-verification, and activation envelopes
advance from their immediately prior controlling versions to their next
integer versions, retain all prior fields in order, and append
`peerCellPartitionBindingDigest` followed by
`orderedExecutablePreflightReceiptDigestsInGroup`. Qualification/run
identities bind the version-3 plan transitively. Every T8/T9 action, authority,
statistics, execution, and supersession/history receipt for an executable
group binds those same appended fields directly or through that exact plan/
schedule/activation chain.

Section 14.3's peer and batch statistics are valid-only artifacts for the
executable rows of the exact group. Their cardinality rules are unchanged: a
terminal receipt, registry/evidence state, `WAVE_BLOCKED`, or `INVALID` cannot
be laundered into a valid peer artifact. Section 14.4 likewise ranges only
over that group's executable rows.

### 15.4 Static terminals, orthogonal environments, and strict close

A terminal disposition is immutable for its reviewed candidate/registry/
anchor/ABI/corpus freeze and launches no T8 or T9. No duration, throughput,
bootstrap, block, environment observation, or timing result may reclassify it.
Artifact recovery, ABI change, operation addition, semantic projection, or
denominator repair requires the existing causal/E-3 supersession path and a
new partition binding while preserving the old receipt and history.

For every required role on a terminal census row, apply exactly:

| terminal disposition | wave effect | final/strict-close effect |
|---|---|---|
| `NO_COMMON_DENOMINATOR` | `WAVE_BLOCKED` | evidence `BLOCKED`; mandatory non-win |
| `UNAVAILABLE_ARTIFACT` | `WAVE_BLOCKED` | evidence `BLOCKED`; mandatory non-win |
| `UNSUPPORTED_ABI` | `WAVE_BLOCKED` | evidence `BLOCKED`; mandatory non-win |
| `INCOMPARABLE` | retain literal registry state; aggregate performance `RED` | performance `RED`; never `FASTER` or a peer win |
| `NOT_APPLICABLE` | retain literal registry state; aggregate performance `RED` | performance `RED`; never `FASTER` or a peer win |

No terminal is GREEN, FASTER, debt-clearing, or transferable to another role.
`INCOMPARABLE` and `NOT_APPLICABLE` remain registry non-wins rather than
evidence-BLOCKED; the other three remain evidence-BLOCKED rather than timed
losses. Every empty mandatory denominator remains visible in its non-empty
empty-stratum list and exact BLOCKED close cell.

Required-environment availability is orthogonal to this static partition.
Before T7, every frozen required environment class receives exactly one
`AVAILABLE` or `UNAVAILABLE_ENVIRONMENT` receipt in vector order. The latter
binds `closeEnvironmentVectorDigest`, `environmentClassDigest`,
`peerCellPartitionBindingDigest`, its admission-evidence digest, and literal
`NO_T8_OR_T9`; it yields `WAVE_BLOCKED` and final evidence `BLOCKED` for every
otherwise executable required cell in that vector without mutating its static
`EXECUTABLE` disposition. A report-only or different environment cannot
rescue it.

All history, supersession, wave, final, aggregate-verdict, and perfection-
close envelopes advance from their immediately prior controlling versions,
retain prior fields in order, and append `peerCellPartitionBindingDigest`,
`orderedTerminalDispositionReceiptsDigest`, and the applicable executable
preflight/statistics digest or terminal receipt. Empty executable plan/result
sets are encoded as exact empty arrays, never omitted. The close check
reconstructs the full census; proves every census index appears once in an
executable result or terminal receipt and every required environment appears
once; and applies the table conjunctively. An absent/duplicate cell, absent
required environment, terminal launch, executable cell without one forced
group, or dropped terminal receipt is structural RED and prevents perfection.
